`StateMark` (3.23): the eight market states, the four obligation states, and the dotted ring a needs-you row uses for an abandoned draft (3.15). It sits at the head of a market card's kicker, a needs-you row's meta line, a running row and a covered card's kicker, with the citron dot before it where a clock is waiting on this person. The sentences it replaces are gone: "Waiting on an answer", "Settled", "No answer", "Never settled", "Squared up", "Called it even", "Waiting for Gabe", "Everyone's in", "Nobody's in yet". What may sit beside a mark is a clock ("Closes tonight", "Voting ends tomorrow", "Resolving tonight") or, on a needs-you row, the reason the row exists (who said what happened, a count of who is in). A locked question reads "locked" until somebody has voted and "in voting" after; a deadlock is the arbitrate rule open with votes on both sides. The market's own screen draws its open and resolved marks in its ink; "you're in" is always the person's hue.

Rejected: keeping the kicker sentence under the mark for people who cannot read a dashed ring. The mark carries an `aria-label`, which is what a screen reader gets; sighted people get the form, which is the point.

## 2026-09-24: The type budget is a lint, and today it is a ratchet

`scripts/lint-type-budget.ts` runs under `npm run lint` and before every `next build`: no legacy token and no literal pixel size anywhere outside the control components; at most four of the nine tokens and one serif size per screen, counted over the page and everything it imports under `src/`, because a screen is composed of components and the budget belongs to the screen; at most three per card. Counting references over-approximates a screen that renders several states, which is what 1.2 asks for ("if a screen's markup references five, something on it is decoration").

Run against the build as it stands, every screen but You and the invite landing is over four, and most carry two serif sizes. The obligation token alone brings `numeral` (dollars, 2.2) and `serif-m` (quoted invented units, 2.2) to every screen that shows one; a covered card brings `label`, `body` and `body-sm`; captions and section labels sit on nearly everything. So the specification's budget and its own component inventory disagree: a person view built exactly from 3.10, 3.18 and 3.4 references seven tokens and two serifs. That is reported at the seam rather than resolved here. Until it is ruled on, the rule is a ratchet: `scripts/type-budget.baseline.json` records each screen over the budget with its count on the day the rule arrived, the build fails the moment any screen gets worse or any new screen exceeds four, and it passes once a screen is burned down. Rejected: shipping the rule failing (nothing would build) and shipping no rule (nothing would hold).

## 2026-09-24: Market inks: decided when a question is asked, stored, picked from its screen

`dares.ink` and `dares.ink_source` (migration `0014_market_inks`, additive and nullable so the build already deployed keeps inserting). `inkFor` in `src/lib/ui/ink.ts` follows 1.8's order: the creator's pick; else the mark's dominant hue snapped to the nearest of the eight; else a hash of the market id; and balance last, so that among the questions still open between the same people no two share an ink while fewer than eight are open, a collision moving the newcomer to the nearest free ink on the wheel (the clockwise one on a tie). Balance applies to marks and hashes and never moves a pick, because a pick is the one case where a person chose. A row from before inks existed has none stored and reads as the hash it would have got, so nothing is backfilled. The pick is eight dots behind More on the market's screen, for the creator only, one tap: the specification's "one tap from the market screen, never a step in creating it".

On the market's own screen the four structural tokens are swapped for the ink's layers by setting the custom properties on the screen's root, which every `bg-ground`, `bg-surface` and `border-line` inside it reads without knowing; the wrapper paints the grain over the ink's ground. Type colours, the chalk button, the citron dot and person hues read none of them. Everywhere else the ink is the stamp behind the mark, on its field.

Two things the specification leaves open, recorded rather than decided. A mark inside an obligation token belongs to a unit, not a market, and 1.7 gives every stamp "the market's field"; a unit's stamp takes the neutral field until ruled. And "reds fold into Rose" (1.8) is not what nearest-hue snapping does: pure red sits at hue 29 in OKLCH, 16 from Clay and 21 from Rose, so it folds into Clay. The snap is implemented as the rule says, nearest of the eight, and the sentence is flagged.

No market carries a mark today: the question step's mark picker was never built (section 7 lists it as undrawn), so every ink in the database is a hash until it is. The emoji hue table 1.8 calls for is computed once from Noto's emoji images in the browser, since nothing in the build rasterizes an SVG, and is kept as `src/lib/ui/emoji-hues.json`; an emoji the table has not seen is hueless.

## 2026-09-24: The anchor is gone, and the columns wait for the deploy

Nothing generates or shows a starting number: the scoping tool no longer asks for one (its fixture re-recorded from the API with the new schema), the draft carries none, the entry stage has no "Start at N", and the details sheet does not mention it. `anchor_value`, `anchor_at` and `anchor_rationale` leave the Drizzle schema in this change so the new build never reads them, and the migration that drops them is applied only once that build is deployed: the build running today selects every column it knows, and dropping one from under it would break every market read. The columns are dead, not yet gone.

## 2026-09-24: Copy that left the screens

Per 4.9, on the market screen: the scoring explanation on the settled screen ("Every pair squares on how much closer…"), the lock instruction above the lock button, the consent sentence under a ruling, and the participant sentences the mark now carries. The terms, the deadline, the unit and the tiebreaker are the four facts of the details `dl` (3.25) under every state, and everything else the product has to say about how a question works is behind More, with the exact group's number and the creator's ink pick. The stake step carries the two kept lines ("The most you can be out is what you put on it." and "You only settle with people who land closer than you, and only by the gap between your numbers."), the blind chip says "Numbers show when everyone's in", and the who's-in step says "Nobody needs an account to look." The percent form of every number ("70%", never "7 in 10") is the odds line's and arrives with it in the second half.

# The v2 migration, second half (2026-09-25)

## 2026-09-25: Three rulings from the seam

**The type budget, recounted with control labels out.** The lint now places every token reference on the JSX element that carries it and leaves out a button, a form field, its label or a chip (`CONTROL_TAGS` in `scripts/lint-type-budget.ts`, with `--explain` listing every reference and its element). The recount changed no screen's total: on every one of the eleven screens over four, each token used on a control is also used on content somewhere on the same screen. So the ratchet stays as enforcement, the baseline is unchanged, and the contradiction is real and goes to the design session: the market screen built to the specification's own inventory reads serif-l (the question), body (the details' values), label (their keys), caption (clocks and the asker line), body-sm (supporting lines), numeral (the leaderboard's rank and the code) and serif-m (the quoted invented words inside an obligation token). Rejected: relaxing the budget, and counting controls in (both against the ruling).

**Reds fold into Rose, as an exception over nearest-hue.** `nearestInk` sends any hue under 40 to Rose before looking for the nearest ink (`RED_MAX`). sRGB red is 29 in OKLCH, past the midpoint between Rose (8) and Clay (45), so by the wheel alone a red mark would be Clay, which is the case 1.8 names. Forty is where red gives way to orange (orangered sits on it), so a fox or a peach still reads as Clay. Rejected: moving Rose's hue, which would move its ground and surface too.

**The tiebreaker is credited to the agreement, never to the app.** The settled screen's ruling section, the details' "If it's unclear" line, the arbitration sheet and the result tile all say "the tiebreaker everyone agreed to". The card reads as settled by what everyone accepted at entry, not as the app deciding. Rejected: "The app was asked to call it", which is true and reads as the app ruling.

## 2026-09-25: The pinned sheet

`PinnedSheet` (`src/components/ui/pinned-sheet.tsx`, docs/design.md 3.24): fixed to the bottom, the current place's surface, a line along the top, 12px top corners, no shadow, 16px sides and 24px bottom over the home indicator, 10px between rows. Two heights only when there is a `high`: a grabber and the header row under it are the drag handle, and only they, so the move itself (a slider, a field, a button) keeps its own gestures; snap by direction at 24px; a tap on the grabber toggles. It is not modal: nothing behind it dims and the page still scrolls. A moment that binds other people (a vote, asking for the tiebreaker) still gets the modal `Sheet` on top of it.

The room the sheet takes is paid by `Screen`: the sheet measures its resting height and sets `--sheet-room` on the document, and `Screen`'s bottom padding adds it. Rejected: a spacer rendered in flow by the sheet itself, which lands wherever the component happens to sit in the tree (the first cut put three hundred pixels of nothing under the question band).

Converted together, as the brief asked: the market screen (the odds line, then the link, then the call, then the result), the ask flow's primary at every step that has one (the question, the careful questions, who's in, the terms, and the declined screen's dare offer), the code screen's Join with its form-level problem above it, the claimant screen's "That's me", the first screen's "Yep, all N are right", and the person view's "I got this one" (`ActionArea` is now the sheet). The cover form's primary stays in flow: the brief named the three surfaces and 3.24 names the code and claimant screens; the cover form is neither, and it is the next screen to convert.

## 2026-09-25: The odds line and the weight line are one object

`OddsLine` in the sheet, `WeightLine` in the screen, the same ten buckets (`bucketOfPercent`). The value is placed by pointer on the drawn line, because a native range does not put its thumb where a finger taps on every phone; the native range is still there over the line at opacity 0, for the keyboard and the screen reader, with an `aria-valuetext` that says "not picked yet" until it is. Nothing starts at 50%: the value is null until touched, the primary reads "Slide to pick your odds" and is disabled, and the first touch raises the sheet. The stake chips are three across at 44px ($5, $10, $20, or 1, 2, 3) with "Something else" for a field; rejected: the four presets of the tile grid, which do not fit three across.

Every number is a whole percent (`percentOf` replaces `tenthOf`; the caption, the marker chip, the entry line and the sparkline all say "45%", never "4 in 10"). The weight line takes each bucket's stake as a decimal string and this person's own stake, and recomputes heights itself in integers, so while someone is changing their number the picture follows the finger (their stake moves bucket) before the save. The group's marker does not move with it: that would need everyone's values on the client, which the reveal rules forbid before lock. Recorded as the one thing in 3.22's "changing" state that is not live.

The entering moment: the sheet lowers, the columns grow from 6px segments over 700ms with this person's share filling in their hue and the marker drawing last, and at 1.8 seconds the sheet becomes "Send it to the chat" under "Anyone with the link can get in until 10:40pm." With reduced motion the next state is there at once. The asker's lock lives in that sheet too: chalk once everyone is in, a secondary row otherwise.

## 2026-09-25: The call sheet

`CallSheet` replaces the ballot, "what happened" and the arbitration panel with 3.24's four states. Not yet known: two equal wells, "Yes" and "No", and "Nobody can tell" as a tertiary; a well raises the sheet to an optional line ("What happened?") and "Say it: yes", which saves the line (the app reads it against the terms, as before) and then opens the vote's own modal. Voting, not said: the count line ("1 of 2 has said yes. One more and it settles."), the chalk agree, "Not how I saw it" for the other outcomes, and who has said what when raised. Said: one line and Change, no second height. Split, under the arbitrate rule, for someone who is in and has said: "Add what you saw", the tiebreaker's deadline or its button, and what each side said when raised.

The wells say "Yes" and "No" rather than the outcomes in words ("He fell asleep"): no outcome phrasing is stored, and adding one means a schema column and a prompt change with every existing market falling back to the words anyway. Recorded as the gap it is. The first person to vote is the claimant: the claim card under the band (3.25) shows their avatar, "Priya says yes" and their line. The app's read is the claim only while nobody has voted, and a caption in the raised sheet after that: the group decides.

## 2026-09-25: The settled sheet and the link tiles

`SettledSheet`: a thumbnail of the result tile, one caption ("Yes. Priya was closest, at 80%."), "Send how it ended", and the whole tile when raised. Gone once sent or once this person leaves the screen: `sessionStorage`, because it is about what this person has seen on this device and not about the market, which is why it is not a column.

`src/lib/ui/tiles.tsx` draws the two tiles (3.27) with Satori: the asking tile (the asker's first name and avatar, "What are the odds?" or "Take the other side." for an argument, the empty odds line with the mark at both ends, the absolute close time in ink-hi) and the result tile without a photo (the mark and the outcome, the call line with the closest person ringed in cream, who called it). One route, `/m/[id]/opengraph-image`, serves the asking tile until the market is settled and the result tile after, so a link sent by "Send how it ended" previews as the result while the first send stays frozen as the ask. The tile with a photo waits for the media pipeline. Two OFL faces are bundled in `src/lib/ui/fonts` and read from disk, traced into the function by `next.config.ts`; Turbopack does not serve `new URL(…, import.meta.url)` to `fetch` in development, so that documented pattern was rejected after it took the whole route down at module load.

`dares.zone` (migration `0016_ask_zone`, nullable): the asker's IANA zone from the display cookie at draft time, so the tile can say "Closes Fri, Sep 26, 10:40pm" in the zone the group lives in; a row from before says the time in UTC and says so. Rejected: a relative time (a preview is frozen into the chat, 3.27), and storing nothing (an absolute time with no zone is a guess).

## 2026-09-25: The phone test: a band behind the status bar, and the viewport put back

Three findings from the installed app on an iPhone (docs/testing.md, session 7). The first was read straight from the code and the rendered page, the other two could not be reproduced here and are recorded as the best-supported reading, not a confirmed one.

**A band behind the status bar.** `body` pays the top inset as padding, which keeps content out from under the translucent status bar at rest and nowhere else: scrolled, content ran under the clock. `Screen` now renders a fixed band the height of the top inset, the ground with its grain, at `z-20` (over content, under every modal). It lives in `Screen` rather than the root layout so that on a market's own screen it inherits the market's ground through `inkVars` (1.8); in the layout it would have painted the plain ground over an inked page. Rejected: making the band the body's own background clipped to the inset (the body scrolls with the page, so nothing on it stays put).

**The bar and the sheet sitting high, and the bar drifting on Now.** The report read as the bottom inset paid twice. It is paid once in the code: `body` pays top and sides only, the tab bar and the pinned sheet each pay their own bottom inset, and no other fixed element pays it. The grain was the first suspect for the drift, since a `filter` on an ancestor makes it a fixed element's containing block: the grain's filter is inside the SVG the background image is made of, not a CSS filter on the body, and the rendered page confirms no ancestor of the bar (main, body, html) carries a transform, filter, will-change, perspective, contain or backdrop-filter. `ViewportRepair` (in the root layout) listens for a field losing focus and for the visual viewport resizing, and when the reading says the viewport is stuck (`viewportStuck`: short or offset, nothing focused, no pinch zoom) scrolls one pixel and back, which makes Safari re-read the viewport. It only runs on iOS. Rejected: turning the app into a scroll container (`main` scrolling inside a fixed-height body with the bar and the sheet in flow), which sidesteps fixed positioning entirely but changes the scroll model of every screen (restoration, anchors, the sheet's own rule that it never locks the page).

**Corrected the same day.** The first version of this entry attributed both symptoms to a documented iOS 26.0 regression (the visual viewport left short and offset after the keyboard, fixed by Apple in 26.1). The phone that showed the drift runs iOS 26.6.2, well past that fix, so the attribution is false for the only device that has shown the bug, and it is withdrawn. Two claims were made and only one is disproven. The mechanism, that dismissing the keyboard leaves the visual viewport stuck and the fixed bar then tracks it, may still be what happens on 26.6.2 for some other reason; and the repair triggers on that symptom, never on the iOS version, so it may still work. It stays in until the phone answers one check (docs/testing.md, session 7): a cold start onto Now with no field touched, scrolled; then the code field, the keyboard dismissed, scrolled again. Drift only after the keyboard means the mechanism holds and the repair either works or needs a larger nudge; drift on the cold start, with no keyboard ever shown, means the mechanism is wrong too and the diagnosis starts over.

## 2026-09-25: The relayer's balance, watched from the tick

The development machine shares the relayer with production, so a local test run spends the gas people on dareful.app depend on (one run of the database suites costs about half a MON), and the relayer ran dry once without anyone noticing until a chain write failed. The tick now reads the relayer's balance after its jobs (`src/lib/chain/watch.ts`), reports it in its JSON every minute, warns in the log while it is under the floor, and at the top of each hour emails `OPS_EMAIL` while it stays there. The floor is 3 MON: a few runs of warning. Rejected: emailing on every low tick (sixty an hour); a table to remember that the operator was told (a row for one boolean, and a missed refill still deserves the next hour's email); a push to an account, since there is no operator account and Principle 1 is about people, not gas. The address is an environment variable, blank in the example, because the repository is public. A balance that cannot be read is reported as unread and never fails the tick.

Confirmed the same day: the production scheduler answers 200 since 15:55 UTC (349 refusals before it, on the same path the route lives at, so the secret was the cause and not the path), and the database suites pass on the refilled relayer.

# Lifecycle (2026-09-25)

## 2026-09-25: The fifth design session's files, in their places

The session's output arrived beside the files it replaces. `docs/dareful_design.md` is now `docs/design.md`, `docs/dareful-marks-and-memories.md` is `docs/marks-and-memories.md`, and `docs/design/reference/Dareful_Design.html` is `docs/design/reference/design.html`; the old three are gone. Nothing in them is needed that the new ones lack: the old "what changed" list (the v2 items) is history this log already records; the old type table's `numeral-hero` and `numeral` rows and the old 1.8 folds are what the session revised; the old 3.26 range text is what number markets replaced. The emoji table is runtime data and lives with the app's other emoji data, `src/lib/ui/emoji-inks.json`; the script that makes it lives in `scripts/emoji-inks.py`, with its running instructions in its header, and both documents now name those paths. The repository has no Python tooling: the development machine has Python 3.14 and Pillow but not Noto Color Emoji, so the script cannot run here as it stands, and it is kept as Python rather than ported, because no Node library rasterises Noto Color Emoji's bitmap font without a browser and the table is regenerated only when the tile renderer's font changes. Rejected: a port to TypeScript on a headless browser, which is how the first table was built and how it came to disagree with the renderer's own font.

## 2026-09-25: One emoji table, the renderer's

The first half's `emoji-hues.ts` (3,750 hues computed from Noto artwork in a browser, snapped to an ink in code) is replaced by the design session's `emoji-inks.json` (1,907 emoji mapped straight to an ink, computed from the font the tile renderer draws with, and holding exactly the emoji that font can draw). Compared before the switch: 1,645 emoji were in both, and the two disagreed on 531 of them (👕 among them: Slate by the browser's artwork, Sea in the reference font); 262 are only in the new table (mostly sequences the first pass never rendered), 73 only in the old (components and glyphs the reference font cannot draw). The table wins, for the two reasons the brief gives: a mark can never become a blank box in a group chat, and a market's colour is what its share card draws. `inkFor` now takes the mark's ink from the table (`emojiInk`) instead of a hue; a mark the font cannot draw is refused when a market is drafted (`bad_input`); seven glyphs from Emoji 17 are the ones left out.

Reds still land on Rose. The table encodes the fold directly (🍎 🍅 ❤ 🌹 🍷 🚗 are all Rose), and the script applies it as three rules rather than the code's one: hues from 345 round to 20 go to Rose always, hues from 20 to 40 go to Rose when their mean chroma is 0.14 or more and to Clay below it (the browns), and greens from 120 to 165 go to Olive. `nearestInk`, kept for pictures and stickers measured at upload, now mirrors those three folds with an optional chroma; the first half's `RED_MAX` is gone.

## 2026-09-25: The budget counts sizes

The lint (`scripts/lint-type-budget.ts`) counts a family at a pixel size, weight free inside it, at most four a screen and one serif, at most two weights at a size and only 400 and 600 (1.2, 4.8). Text inside a control or an obligation token is out: by element (buttons, fields, labels, chips), by 4.8's own marker (`data-type-exempt`, on the code boxes, the room code's boxes and the "$" beside the amount field, none of which is a form element), or by file (the button, chip, tab bar, odds line and obligation token components). The card's own cap is gone. Against the build, outside controls and tokens:

| Screen | Sizes | Count |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Now (`src/app/page.tsx`) | 13, 15, 17, serif 17, serif 26, serif 40 | 6, three states in one file |
| First run, signed out (the same file) | 13, 17, serif 40 | 3 |
| Claimant (`c/[token]`) | 13, 15, 17, serif 26, serif 40 | 5, two serif |
| Join by link | 13, 17, serif 26 | 3 |
| Join by code | 13, 15, 17, serif 26 | 4 |
| Market screen (`m/[id]`) | 13, 15, 17, serif 26 | 4 |
| Ask flow (`m/new`) | 13, 15, 17, serif 17, serif 26 | 5, two serif |
| Cover form (`new`) | 13, 15, 17 | 3 |
| Cover (`o/[id]`) | 13, 15, 17, serif 26 | 4 |
| Person view | 13, 15, 17, serif 26 | 4 |
| Ghost page | 13, 15, 17 | 3 |
| People | 13, 15, 17 | 3 |
| Welcome | 13, 15, 17, serif 40 | 4 |
| You | 13, 17 | 2 |
| Wallet setup | 15, serif 26 | 2 |

Twelve screens pass at four or under, where eleven failed the token count. Three are over and are held to a baseline written today, the size rule's first day, with the reasons: **Now** renders three states from one file (signed out at serif 40, empty at serif 40, populated at serif 17 in the rows and serif 26 on the story cards in "Just happened"), and the populated state alone is five sizes with two serif, because 3.4 gives a story card a serif 26 question while the `Now` board counts serif 17 only: a contradiction inside the specification, for the design session. **The claimant screen** renders its dead, used and own-link states at serif 26 beside the live state's serif 40; per state each is within the budget, and a static lint sums them. **The ask flow** echoes the question and the dare-instead line at serif 17 beside the serif 26 headline; 1.2's own consequence (a screen whose serif is taken sets a listed question in body 600) says what the fix is, and it is not made here, because the session said one built screen changes. The one that did: the leaderboard rank, 17px 600 tabular. Rejected: making the lint per state (a file-level marker or splitting by component), which the specification's DOM lint gets for free and a static one would guess at.

Two more from the session's list: a unit's mark inside an obligation token is a bare 16px glyph on the token's fill, 4px before the words, with no stamp (1.7, 3.2), and a unit's mark anywhere else sits on `--surface-2`, never on an ink; the interim neutral field was that colour already.

## 2026-09-25: Settling and forgiving, from the row

Per 6.3: the person view, the obligation row, a sheet. The creditor's rows are the move (`CloseObligation`); everyone else's are rows. The sheet holds two acts of equal standing, the chalk "Settled" and a secondary "Call it even" at the same size (Principle 5: forgiveness is a status move, never hidden behind settlement), and the photo row above them (Principle 6: settlement is the photo moment). Either closes everything still open on the obligation, signed with the ledger wallet from its own button. The photo goes up after the chain has the close, and a photo that fails never undoes a settlement: the sheet stays with "Try the photo again". Rejected: a quantity to close in part (the contract allows it; 3.2 draws no control for it; and "two of the three beers" is precision the product treats as optional); and closing from the header token, which is a net of several rows and not one obligation.

The other person hears (Principle 1: "Gabe settled up" is a person acting, and PLANNING.md 8d lists settlements among what is sent). `notification_log` now takes an obligation as well as a question: `dare_id` nullable, `obligation_id`, three more kinds (`settled`, `forgiven`, `netted`), once per obligation for the first two and once per pair per six-hour window for a netting, which is about two people and no one row (migration `0017_notices_about_obligations`). A notice names who and, when the cover has a memo, which one; never the unit and never an amount. Rejected: no notification, which PLANNING.md calls broken; and a table of its own for obligation notices.

Two gaps, recorded. An obligation a market minted renders inside the market's story and has no row of its own, so it cannot be closed from the person view yet: the story's consequence needs the same control. And "Just happened" (4.7) should carry closed obligations, which needs a moment to order by: the chain's timestamp is the one thing the timeline never orders by, and settled and forgiven are derived, not columns (PLANNING.md 5b), so this waits for a ruling on a `closed_at` shadow.

## 2026-09-25: The photo pipeline

**The key.** Storage is reached over the Storage REST API from the server with the project's modern secret key (`SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY`, `sb_secret_…`), which rotates on its own; no SDK, and nothing in the browser. Nothing in the code references the legacy `service_role` key or the anon key (the variable sat in `.env.local` unused), so once the secret key is in place both legacy keys can be disabled in the dashboard; `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is out of `.env.example`. The bucket `media` is private, JPEG only, 5MB an object (`src/db/ops/storage.sql`), with row-level security on and no policies: the secret key bypasses it and nothing else may reach it.

**What is kept.** Two derivatives and nothing else: the frame, 1080px on its long edge, and a 256px square, both re-encoded, which is what strips every field of EXIF (GPS above all), XMP and the rest; only the colour profile crosses. The camera's orientation is applied to the pixels first. When it was taken is read before the strip, from `DateTimeOriginal` with the camera's own offset when it wrote one and the viewer's zone otherwise (the person uploading is almost always the person who took it), and stored as `captured_at`. The parser has no path into the GPS block. Rejected: keeping the original (a GPS-bearing file in a bucket, and the platform's 4.5MB cap on a request). The phone shrinks a photo over 3.5MB to 2048px before sending, which loses that photo's capture time; recorded as the price of the cap.

**Who sees it.** `/api/media/[id]` checks who is asking, then answers with a 302 to a signed URL good for a minute; signed out, unknown, and not one of the two people in the obligation all read as 404, so nothing says whether a photo exists. The browser holds no key; the signed URL is the whole capability. The photo rides the covered card as its 84px thumbnail (3.4). Server actions accept 5MB (`next.config.ts`).

## 2026-09-25: Netting, from under the header

The specification says the same unit between two people nets before display (2.1) and PLANNING.md calls netting a partial close on two reciprocal obligations at once, recorded as its own event (5b); neither draws the control. Derived: under the person-view header, one row per unit in each set of people that goes both ways ("Cancel out the $10 each way", with what each has of the other under it), and a sheet that asks once, because it is not undone; one signature from either party, one transaction per unit and set of people, the contract cancelling by the smaller side. "Net" is a banned word (2.1), so the words are cancel out and each way. An obligation the netting empties reads as settled on its card, since 3.23 has no mark for netted. Both the placement and the words are the build's derivation and are flagged for the design session; rejected: the header token itself as the control (a token nets across sets of people; the contract nets inside one).

## 2026-09-25: The rally strip; square; closing the books

The rally (3.11) is drawn: two rows, theirs then yours, a 24px avatar, twelve slots for the last twelve pick-ups nobody expects to settle, oldest first, a 12px dot in the person's hue where they picked one up and a 4px dot where they did not, the sentence under it, nothing under four pick-ups. Square detection is what is built: the People collapse row and the header's "even" both come from Envio's open edges and are computed, never declared. Closing the books (PLANNING.md 10, "when a group goes square") has no home in the specification: groups are not navigation (4.7, section 7), the feature table (6.3) does not list it, and nothing in the design draws a closing summary. Not built, and not given a home here; asked.

## 2026-09-25: Email: the domain's records

The email channel is built (`sendEmail`, Resend) and off until `RESEND_API_KEY` and `EMAIL_FROM` are set; the relayer watch writes to `OPS_EMAIL`, which is set in Vercel and blank in `.env.example`. The records for the domain, for Cloudflare, all DNS-only (proxy off), so that mail from a newly verified domain reaches Gmail rather than its spam folder: the SPF and MX on Resend's sending subdomain, the DKIM key Resend generates for the domain, and a DMARC policy on the root that starts at `p=none` with reports and moves to `quarantine` once a week of reports shows only Resend sending. They are in docs/testing.md, session 8, for approval before anything is entered.

# Lifecycle, closing (2026-09-25)

## 2026-09-25: Three rulings

**Closing the books is cut, superseded.** Its premise was a group as a place that could go square and be closed; the shell removed the group as a place (docs/design.md 4.7, section 7), so the feature has nothing to close. Logged as superseded rather than deferred: if a closing moment ever returns it will be person-level and designed, not this. **Netting stays as placed and worded**: the row under the person-view header, "Cancel out the N each way". **Closed obligations get a moment**: `obligations.closed_at`, written by the app when the close's transaction has gone through (migration `0018_closed_at`). This follows the ordering rule rather than breaking it: the rule forbids ordering by the chain's clock, and `closed_at` is the offchain clock. Whether it was settled or forgiven, and what is still open, stay derived from the chain (PLANNING.md 5b); "Just happened" reads the reason from the indexer for the few closed rows it lists, its one indexer query, and says settled when the indexer has not caught up. A netting writes no `closed_at`: which obligations it empties is the indexer's first-in-first-out attribution, not the app's, so a netting appears in nobody's "Just happened"; recorded.

## 2026-09-25: The viewport repair is removed

The band under the tab bar appeared on a cold start of the installed app, on People, with no text field touched. That rules out the mechanism the repair was built for (a visual viewport left short by a dismissed keyboard) as well as the version it was first pinned on; a repair that fires on a symptom whose cause does not exist is dead code with a scroll in it. `ViewportRepair`, `viewportStuck`, their tests and their mutants are gone. What replaces them is an instrument, not a fix: `ViewportProbe`, behind a tertiary "Measure the screen" on You, prints the numbers that decide the cause on the phone itself (the window's height against the screen's, the visual viewport, both safe-area insets as resolved, where a `position: fixed; bottom: 0` box actually lands, the tab bar's own box, and whether the page is standalone). The reading the author made, that the inset might be paid as position rather than padding, was checked against the served CSS: the tab bar and the pinned sheet are `bottom: 0` with the inset as `padding-bottom`, and the only element positioned by the inset is the Start button, which must clear the bar. So the CSS is the standard shape, and the band has another cause the probe will name. The probe is to be removed with the cause.

## 2026-09-25: The email records as they went in

Resend now delegates through CNAMEs rather than the SPF and MX records the earlier note listed: `send` and `rsend` are CNAMEs to `forge.rmta.net`, plus the DKIM TXT at `resend._domainkey`. DMARC was added by hand as `v=DMARC1; p=none; adkim=r; aspf=r`, with no reporting address, so nothing personal is published in DNS. `RESEND_API_KEY` (sending access, restricted to the domain) and `EMAIL_FROM` are set in Vercel, with `OPS_EMAIL`; one line was sent from the development machine through `sendOps`, the path the relayer watch and every user notice share, and Resend accepted it. The earlier table in docs/testing.md session 8 is superseded by this entry.

## 2026-09-25: An obligation a market minted closes from its story

The gap recorded this morning is closed: markets are about to be the main source of obligations, and a market loser held a debt that could never be settled or forgiven. On the person view, the story's consequence the viewer is owed, while it is still open, is the move (the same `CloseObligation`, in a row mode), and a consequence that has closed carries the settled or forgiven mark before its sentence (3.23). The story card is restructured for it: the link is the story (kicker, question, call line, when) and the consequences sit under it outside the link, because a control inside a link is not a control. Each consequence now carries its obligation id, and the person view derives each one's state from the indexer (`consequenceStates`). What is open on a market-minted edge after a netting is the indexer's figure, first-in-first-out, and the close covers exactly that; the story still shows the quantity the market minted. Not on Now: Now routes rather than does (4.7). Rejected: a settle control on the market screen's own transfers list, which 6.3 does not place there.


# Phase 5: number markets, refresh, the mark picker (2026-09-25)

## 2026-09-25: The scale is a scoring rule, and how it is set

A number market's `range` divides every miss, so the scale is a scoring rule that looks like a display detail (docs/decisions.md 2026-09-24). Built as ruled: fixed when the question is asked, signed by the asker inside `Create` (the contract holds it and `verify-envio` checks the chain's copy against the indexer's), never derived from entries, and stored with whose it is, `dares.range_source` (`asker` or `ai`, migration `0019_range_source`, with a check that a number market always has both). Shown once, in the details, only when the asker set it: "Scored on: Off by 20 shirts or more scores nothing. Closer scores more." A scale the model set appears on no screen (3.26). The asker sets it on the terms step as a whole-number span in the question's own unit ("20 shirts off scores nothing"); 3.26's "in the asker's own words" is read as the asker's own unit and figure, an assumption flagged for the design session. Rejected: a free-text scale the model parses into a number, which puts a model on the path of a signed number; and a low-to-high interval, whose two ends are the anchor 3.26 took out.

**The sanity check** (`src/lib/ledger/scale.ts`). The model is asked, from the question alone, where nearly every reasonable answer would land (`low`, `high`) and for its single most likely answer (`typical`); the scale is `high - low`. It is used only when the three are whole numbers in order and the span sits between a quarter of the typical answer and four times it (a typical answer under one reads as one). Too narrow floors reasonable answers at zero; too wide scores everyone near one, shrinks every transfer, and under truncation collapses small stakes to nothing: the shirts example on a scale of 10,000 instead of 20 moves two cents on a miss of 186. A proposal that fails is not used at all: the terms step then asks the asker for the scale, which is the primary path anyway. Rejected: a default of twice the typical answer, because the model's own numbers are what failed the check; and clamping the span into the band, which silently changes what the model said. The model's scale travels back to the draft under an HMAC token minted for this person by this server, so a client cannot label a scale it typed as the model's and hide it. **Afterward**: when a number market settles, the mirror counts how many entries floored at zero and whether every score sat within five percent of perfect, and logs the market when more than half floored or the scale was that wide; `verify-envio` prints, for every number market, the scale, the answer, the entries and how many floored, so a scale that turned out wrong is visible after the fact without a screen for it.

**Verified four ways**, as binary was. The worked example is five people on "How many shirts can Gabe wear at once?" (scale 20, answer 14): Priya 14 at $5, Gabe 18 at $10, Theo 12 at $5, Maya 9 at $20, John 200 at $5; scores 10000, 8000, 9000, 7500, 0; ten edges (25, 12, 31, 125, 12, 12, 100, 18, 112, 93) truncated each on its own; nets +193, +75, +130, +32, -430, summing to zero and inside every stake. By hand first (the working is in `tests/unit/numbers.test.ts`), then the mirror (`scoreNumeric`, written from the formula), then the deployed contract's own `scoreNumeric` by read-only call across that example, the contract's test table and the edges of the scale, then a real market on Monad with exactly those numbers (three votes for 14 against Theo's 15, which landed as a dissent, and the third 14 settled it), whose ten minted edges match to the cent, and `verify-envio`, which now recomputes number markets from the chain's positions, outcome and scale. Gas was re-measured with the survey's `number` mode: `create` 590,596 and 924,879 for two and five positions, `resolve` 274,309 and 1,232,630 for one and ten edges, within three percent of the yes-or-no figures; the limits stand, and the figures are recorded in `gas.ts`.

**The group's number** on a number market is the stake-weighted mean (docs/design.md 3.22, with the marker's arithmetic written for it: an off-axis entry pulls the mean past an end and the chip says so with an arrow). PLANNING.md 8 says the stake-weighted median; the design is the later and more specific document and its picture only works for a mean, so this is a correction to PLANNING.md, recorded. Rejected: the median, which no off-axis entry could pull, leaving the design's clamp with nothing to do. No point is written to `dare_number_series` for a number market, since that column holds basis points; a slow number market gets no line of the group's number yet, deferred until the series has a column for a value.

**The vote is on a proposed number** (3.24 as written): the claim is "It was 14 shirts" from the number field, the agreeing chalk is "That's right, 14", and "Not how I saw it" raises the sheet to the field. A dissent with a number is a vote for that number, so three at 14 against one at 15 settle at 14 (the split-vote case lands as a dissent, not a deadlock); a dissent with no number, which 3.24 allows, is words on the record and no vote, since the contract can only count a signature over a number: an assumption, flagged. "Nobody can tell" stays as it is. A vote on a number market names any whole number up to nine digits (`outcomeAllowed`); a yes-or-no market still takes only 0, 1 and the void. The settled screen writes the answer as a `serif-l` sentence, the number followed by the claimant's words when they run to sixty characters or fewer ("14 shirts, then a seam gave out."), else the number alone; the caption says who was closest and by how much; the ruler stands where the call line would; closest first ranks by score, which for a number market is distance. The arbitrator has a number variant (`arbitrateNumber`) that returns the number or that the terms do not decide it; the proposal has one too (`proposeNumber`), which proposes nothing when two accounts give different counts and the terms do not say whose counts.

**A notice about a number market carries no number.** The rule that no notification, relay text or share card carries an amount, a unit or anyone's number stands as written: the result and ruling notices say "Decided." and open the screen, where the answer is. The result tile does carry the answer ("14 shirts."), because 3.27 draws it there and the tile is the design's; the tension is recorded rather than resolved by weakening either rule.

**Where the kind is chosen.** 3.29 draws the question step without a place for it. Built as two chips under the band, "Yes or no" and "A number" (a dare only; an argument is yes or no), chosen before the write-up, because the terms say how the answer is counted and the model's write-up differs by kind. Rejected: inferring the kind from the line, where a wrong inference wastes the write-up and the asker's reading of it; and choosing it on the terms step, after the terms were written for the other kind. The unit (singular and plural, in `outcome_labels`, PLANNING.md 5b) is proposed by the model and editable on the terms step. Categorical markets are after submission; the over/under shortcut was dropped in the audit, and `dares.over_under` is dead: nothing reads it, and it comes out after the build that stops selecting it is deployed, since the deployed build still selects every column.

## 2026-09-25: Refresh

Three pieces, because pulling only helps someone who thinks to pull (`src/components/ui/refresh.tsx`, `vote-poll.tsx`). **Pull-to-refresh** from the top of any scrolling screen: a touch that starts at the top and travels 72px re-reads the screen through the router (one server render of what is shown, never a reload); a 2px line under the status band fills with the pull and runs while the read is on, in ink, the same runner a working button carries (5.2). Rejected: a spinner, which 5.3 keeps off the screen, and a browser reload, which loses scroll and state. A touch inside a modal sheet belongs to the sheet. **A re-read on return to the foreground** after more than two seconds away, which covers switching to Messages and back, and on a page restored from the back-forward cache; a flick to the app switcher and back re-reads nothing. **A light poll on a locked market**: the screen renders a pulse, one string that changes whenever what the voting screen shows would change (votes, what was said, the answer, the app's proposal), and every six seconds while the screen is visible asks `/api/m/[id]/pulse` for it again, re-reading the screen only when it differs; after five minutes on the screen the interval is thirty seconds, and the poll stops when the market settles, when the screen is hidden (resuming when it returns), and when the route says 404. Six seconds because a person waiting on a vote glances for a few seconds at a time, so the median wait to see another phone's vote is three, while five people watching one market cost under one request a second between them. The poll runs from lock rather than from the first vote, because the first thing people wait on is the claim. The route reads Postgres only and answers the people in the market's group; the indexer allows a hundred queries a minute across everyone and the market screen's own render reconciles with it, so polling the page itself would have spent that budget on one market. Rejected: Supabase Realtime, which PLANNING.md keeps out until Phase 8 and says the lobby's poll must not pull forward; server-sent events, a held connection per viewer on a serverless host; and polling the page.

## 2026-09-25: The mark picker

Built to 3.29 and its two boards. The question step is the band on the market's field, neutral until a mark is picked, with the mark row (the 64px stamp, dashed with a plus when unset; "Add a mark" over "Optional. It picks this market's colour."; set, "Mark" over the emoji's name and "tap to change") and the question in `serif-l`, and one chalk in the sheet, "Next: who's in". The pace and mode chips the step already had stay under the band, which the design does not draw and does not forbid; rejected: moving them into the sheet, which holds one move. **The picker** is the modal sheet at 560px with Done as a word in the close position (the sheet gained `closeLabel` and `tall`), the search field, the hueless line while it applies, Recent with the dashed None cell first, the nine category chips as words, and the grid of 44px cells at 28px. A tap sets the mark at once and the picker stays open; None returns the neutral room. **The catalog** (`src/lib/ui/emoji-catalog.json`, made by `scripts/emoji-catalog.mjs` from emojibase) is exactly the ink table's keys, so it offers exactly what the tile renderer draws (1,907 marks; the seven glyphs from Emoji 17 the reference font lacks are absent), with each mark's CLDR name, group, tags and skin-tone variants as emojibase spells them; a test holds the two files to each other. English only; rejected: every locale, which multiplies the payload by the number of languages for a picker whose search is a convenience. It loads lazily when the picker opens. A search matches any word of the name or the tags that starts with what was typed. **Recents and tones** are kept per device in the browser's storage, as 3.29 says: a device preference, never a record; rejected: the database, which would make a convenience into a row about a person. A long press (or a right click) on a cell with tones opens the one-row popover. **The ink preview**: the draft's id is made on the question step (`crypto.randomUUID`) and sent with the draft, so the ink previewed for a hueless mark (a hash of the id) is the ink stored; balance is previewed on the who's-in step from each set's open inks, which the page sends, with the same `inkFor` the server stores by. The whole ask screen retints over 200ms (`.retint`), so the form owns the screen and the page hands it the top bar. For a unit's mark there is no entry point yet: no screen makes a unit with a label of its own, so 3.29's "wherever a unit is made" has nowhere to open from; recorded. The number field and the picker are controls for the type budget (3.26 says so for the field; the picker's text is cells, chips and a search field), added to the lint's control list.

## 2026-09-25: Profile stats: the data, and the screen that waits

Calibration and the clean-resolution rate are computed (`src/lib/ledger/calibration.ts`, `cleanResolution` in `settle.ts`) and not shown. The You tab is not drawn: section 7 lists it among what the canvas does not have, and 6.1 only places the two stats there; the brief says to stop rather than invent the design for the screen a judge would read as the product's most distinctive claim, so the display is asked for. The data: for yes-or-no questions, the record is every scored position on a question that ended with an answer, binned by the tenths of the weight line, with how many came true and the mean stated probability per bin; for number questions, the average miss as a fraction of the scale, which is the score's complement, so the payout and the record are one number (PLANNING.md 8e). Voids score nobody and count in neither; expiry counts against nobody and is in neither; the void toll counts against the asker through the clean-resolution rate. **The floor**: the curve exists from ten resolved yes-or-no questions; below ten, one result moves the overall hit rate by ten points or more, the whole width of a bin, so every question would redraw the curve. The clean-resolution rate has no floor. Rejected: a curve from the first question, which reads as broken; and a floor per bin, which hides a person's record behind bins they have not filled.

## 2026-09-25: Two loose ends

`.env.example` was already clean: `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` left it with the photo pipeline and no `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_*` variable has existed since Phase 1; nothing to remove. The band under the tab bar in the installed app is parked as ruled: the probe stays on You and nothing else about it changes.

## 2026-09-25: The median, after all, and the far-off check

**The group's number on a number market is the stake-weighted median**, ruled before the commit: the value with half the stake on each side, built as the smallest value with at least half the stake at or below it. Absolute-error scoring rewards each person for reporting their median, so the group's summary is the statistic the rule asks of each person, and one far-off entry cannot move it unless it holds more than half of what is riding. Yes-or-no markets keep the stake-weighted mean. This reverses the entry above that read the design's mean as a correction to PLANNING.md: PLANNING.md 8 had it right, and docs/design.md 3.22 is amended (dated) to match. The clamp and the arrow went with the mean: the median is always one of the entries, so the marker always stands on a column, an off-axis one included when that entry is the median, and there is nothing to clamp; `Marker` now says which row it stands in. The details line says the number in a sentence, since a median is a whole number and not a figure to one decimal. Rejected: the midpoint of two values at an exact tie, which can land between the axis and an off-axis column and would need the clamp back; the lower median is always an entry.

**A far-off number gets a check, never a block** (`farOffThreshold`, `src/lib/ledger/scale.ts`; the block in the entry sheet). Entries are deliberately unclamped: a far-off entry floors at zero and cannot move anyone else's score, since the scale is fixed, so the check only asks. It fires at a hundred times the model's most likely answer, rounded to one significant figure (1,000 for 10, 4,000 for 37), which catches a slipped finger and not a bold guess, and which never sits at the scale's edge: a scale the model set appears on no screen, and a check that fired at its edge could be probed for it by trying numbers. The model's most likely answer is kept for this (`dares.typical`, migration `0020_typical`, under the same signed token as its scale) and shown nowhere; rounding the threshold keeps the answer itself from being read off it. When the asker set the scale it is already in the details, so the line may name it, and with no most likely answer (the model was down) the threshold is fifty times that scale; a hidden scale with nothing else to measure against gets no check rather than a probeable one. The residual: the threshold can be found by trying numbers, and it says the most likely answer to one figure at a hundredfold remove, which places a hidden scale inside a sixteenfold band and no closer; accepted. The wording ("2,400 shirts is a lot of shirts." with "It's 2,400 shirts" and "Not quite"; the scale's sentence when the asker set it) is the build's and is flagged for the next design pass. Rejected: a block, which the rule against clamping forbids in spirit; and a threshold at some multiple of the scale for the model's scale, which is the probe.

# The media phase: photos on a market, stickers by paste, argument evidence (2026-09-25)

## 2026-09-25: Photos on a market are a second caller of the one pipeline

Built on the lifecycle's pipeline and nothing else: `addMarketPhoto` (`src/lib/media/index.ts`) is the settlement photo's path with the market as the parent (`media.dare_id`, the half of the XOR settlement photos leave empty), the same two derivatives, the same strip of EXIF, the same private bucket and the same door, whose rule for market media (participants and the group it was asked in) has been in `canSee` since the lifecycle. **Who adds**: anyone who was in the market (a position), once it is settled, weeks later included; someone in the group who was not in it sees the frame and cannot add to it. Rejected: the group, which the doc's "anyone who was in the market" excludes. **When**: settled only. A voided or expired question gets no frame and no "Add yours": the doc phases "photos on resolved markets", and a void is not a night anyone won; recorded as a reading, since the doc does not say. **How many**: forty-eight memories a market, a guard on the bucket that no screen counts (the doc: nothing anywhere counts photos), and the refusal says the market is full, never how full. **The frame** (`MediaFrame`, docs/design.md 3.8): the first photo added is the frame, credited by first name on the scrim with the counter, the rest in the strip of 60px squares, four and then "+N" (4.3), a tap on a square bringing it into the frame, which is what the counter counts; "+N" shows the rest. Heights as 3.8 gives them: 200 on the settled screen under the outcome and the caption (3.25), 180 in a timeline card between the question and the outcome (3.4), drawn without controls there because the card is a link. Loading is the hatched placeholder in the market's field and surface; failed is the placeholder, "Couldn't load," and a 44px "Try again". **"Add yours from Friday"** (`fromThatNight`): the weekday of the night in the viewer's zone while it is under a week old, "tonight" and "last night" as calendar days the way "yesterday" is everywhere in the app, and "that night" from a week on, when a weekday would name the wrong one. The phone's own picker opens with no `capture`: the night has already happened, so the library is where the photo is. Rejected: the camera first, which is the settlement photo's moment and not this one.

**The memory screen is not built.** Board 15 on the canvas ("The memory it leaves") exists, but `docs/design/reference/design.html` exports the boards as blank iframes, and the specification's text gives the screen only a frame height (260, in 3.8) and a type budget row (4.8); 6.3 places adding photos on the market's own screen. Nothing else about it can be read, so nothing of it was invented: the frame lives on the settled screen and the timeline card, and the board is asked for.

## 2026-09-25: The photo stays out of the link tiles (a correction to the doc's list)

docs/marks-and-memories.md's hackathon list has "the frame in the share card", and docs/design.md 3.27 draws the result tile with the photo full-bleed. Neither is built, and both are corrected here: a tile is frozen into a chat that can include people outside the market and gets forwarded further, so a photo on it reaches people the visibility rule forbids and shows faces that were never chosen for a wider chat. The rule that governs every share card already covers this (a first name and that there is something to look at, never the thing itself), so the result tile says there are photos and never carries one: one line under the outcome, "With photos from that night." (`PHOTOS_LINE`, flagged for the next design pass), with no count, and the tile's data is checked to carry no photo address. Tapping through is where visibility is enforced. The result tile without a photo is unchanged from 3.27.

## 2026-09-25: Stickers, by paste

**Their own table**, `picture_marks` (the decision of 2026-09-18 applied): a sticker belongs to the person who made it and can be the mark on any question they ask, so it is not a `media` row on one event, and the `media` XOR is not widened. The row holds both keys, the size and the ink; `dares.mark_kind` gains `sticker` with `mark_value` the row's id, and every stamp reads a `MarkRef` (`src/lib/ui/mark.ts`) so a sticker never falls through a check that only knew emoji. **The cheapest path only** (3.28, way 1): the picker takes a paste, through the clipboard event anywhere in the sheet and through a "Paste a cutout" cell that asks the clipboard (iOS answers with its own Paste prompt); the phone checks the image has any see-through pixel, bounds it to 1024 and sends a PNG; the server (`src/lib/media/sticker.ts`) trims the transparent margins, pads to a 512 square, measures the ink and bakes the stamp. Tap-to-cut and "Make a sticker" from a settled photo are not built, as ruled. **Two stored sizes**, as 1.7 and 3.28 give them: the 512 source with alpha and the 256 derivative with the cream die-cut edge baked in, drawn by dilating the alpha with an exact distance transform (`src/lib/media/cutout.ts`), filling it cream and compositing the sticker over it, so the app and the tile renderer draw one edge. **The edge is baked at one width.** One derivative cannot be 2px at every stamp size, so it is sized for the 40px stamp: a sticker fills 80% of its stamp, the 256 derivative is drawn at 32px there, eight derivative pixels to one on screen, and the 2px edge is sixteen. The same derivative reads 1.4px at 28 (1.7 asks 1.5) and 3.2px at 64; the 20px stamp draws the bare source, where 1.7 wants no edge. Rejected: a derivative per stamp size, which the doc's "two stored sizes" rules out; and CSS shadows, which the doc rules out by name. **The ink** is the emoji table's measurement on the sticker's own pixels (1.8: alpha over 0.5, OKLCH chroma over 0.04, a quarter chromatic or the mark is hueless, the heaviest 10 degree bin, the chroma-weighted circular mean within 15 degrees, the folds through `nearestInk`), which is why a cutout picks a truer ink than its photo: the background no longer votes. A teal disc pasted in the real session turned the whole ask screen Sea at once. **Who sees a sticker**: its owner, and anyone in a group where a question wears it, through `/api/mark/[id]`, the photo door's twin (404 for everyone else, whether or not it exists). It is also drawn into the question's link tiles, server-side from the bucket, because a market's mark rides both tiles: choosing a sticker as the mark is choosing to send it where the question is sent. The bucket admits PNG beside JPEG for it (`src/db/ops/storage.sql`). **In the picker**, the reserved "Your stickers" row above Recent, in the same cells: the paste cell first, then this person's stickers, newest first; one caption while there are none ("Lift a subject out of a photo, copy it, and paste it here."), flagged for the design pass. Nothing else about the picker changed, as 3.29 says.

## 2026-09-25: Argument evidence (closes open question 14)

**Screenshots only**, attached to "what happened" through the same pipeline: a link is not fetched from the server, which is its own risk. **Evidence is a different role from a memory, told apart by the control it came through and stored on the row** (`media.role`, migration `0021`): the "what happened" step and a case for the tiebreaker attach evidence; "Add yours from that night" adds a memory. The frame reads memories only and the proposal and the arbitrator read evidence only, so a scoreboard never becomes the memory of the night and a memory never sways a ruling (memories can only be added once it is settled anyway). Rejected: inferring the role from the row's timing or from whether the file has a capture time, which classifies a screenshot wrongly the first time someone attaches a photo of the board. docs/design.md 4.3, rule 4 (the clip that resolved the question sits in the frame above the outcome line) describes a photo of the night attached at claim time through the camera row section 7 gives the claim flow; that row is not built this phase (screenshots only), so the rule stays unbuilt rather than contradicted, and when it is built its photo is a memory that also informs the proposal. **Where evidence shows**: the claim card's 72px clip (3.25) for the claimant's, and the sheet's attached list, 44px with the supplier's first name, once anyone has called it; never the frame, and never the tile. **How it is weighed** (`src/lib/ai/client.ts`, the prompts): a screenshot reaches the model inside a tag naming who supplied it and never bare, so it is read as that person's claim; the proposal is told to say what it took from it and who supplied it; the arbitrator is told that someone in a deadlock supplies evidence to win, that a screenshot can be edited, to say in the ruling who supplied what, to weigh evidence more when the other side's case accepts or does not dispute it and less when it disputes it, and that nobody's screenshot outranks the terms. It informs and never decides: the group still votes, and the arbitrator was agreed to before the outcome was known. Recorded from the model with an invented scoreboard (`tests/fixtures/anthropic/*-with-evidence.json`): the proposal read the board, credited Sam with it, and asked the group to confirm the date; the arbitrator gave Sam's board less weight because Theo disputed it, and said so. In the real session the read of an attached board was "could have been typed by anyone, so this rests on that single report", which is the rule working. **Limits**: three screenshots per person per question, by anyone who may say what happened (the group), while it is being called. The pulse counts them, so a screenshot attached on one phone reaches the others within six seconds.

## 2026-09-25: What the real session found, and two holes in the test tooling

The app's read of a number proposal on the sheet said "The app leans n:14, 75 to 25.": the line spelled the vote word instead of the number (Phase 5, found while attaching a screenshot); it now says "14 shirts". The shared fixture's cleanup deleted a market before the photos hanging off it and a temporary user before their stickers, and the sweep also lacked a market's number series and codes; the first failed run left seven temporary users on the production database until the sweep was fixed. Both are fixed and the sweep is clean.

# Photos, built to the reconciled design (2026-09-26)

## 2026-09-26: The reconciled design is the single copy, and every amendment survived

`docs/design.md` and the export in `docs/design/reference/` are replaced with the reconciled files, not merged. Each of the thirty-eight dated amendments listed at its top was read where the file says it now lives, and each is there as written: the median in the build's wording with the lower value on a tie and no clamp (3.22); the photo never on a link tile (3.27); stickers drawn into the tiles (3.27, 3.28); `picture_marks` as its own table with `media` on two parents (1.7, 3.28); the rest as recorded. The three rules this round replaces are absent from the doc's own text: the frame reading memories only, the far-off check a person could confirm past, and a void taking no photos appear only in the change list, each marked as not folded in. `docs/marks-and-memories.md` stays as it was, with its correction keeping photos out of tiles.

## 2026-09-26: The evidence rule, corrected

Last phase's rule that evidence never appears in the frame was too blunt (docs/decisions.md 2026-09-25, "Argument evidence"): a tester attached a photo of the dog at her bowl while saying what happened and nobody could see it, before or after the market settled. As the reconciled design now says (3.8, 3.24, 4.3): what someone attaches while saying what happened is evidence and everyone voting sees it, on the claim card's 72px clip (with a "+N" chip past one) and in the raised sheet's list with who supplied each; once the market ends, the claim's attachments lead the frame as the resolving clip, credited to the claimant, and evidence anyone else attached stays on the record behind More under "How it was called". `media.role` stays as it was; what changed is what the frame reads (`frameItems(rows, claimantId)`: the claimant's evidence oldest first, then the memories) and what the record holds (`recordItems`). The claimant is the first voter, which is what the claim card already showed. The arbitrator and the proposal still read every attachment as its supplier's claim. Rejected: reading every attachment into the frame, which makes a voter's screenshot of a scoreboard the memory of the night; and keeping the old rule with a "keep this one" toggle, which asks the person to classify a photo at the moment they least want a form. The claim's row is built (3.24: "Add a photo or a screenshot", the library first, up to three per person, each a 44px square with a remove control), and anyone voting may attach through the raised sheet; a case for the tiebreaker may carry its own.

## 2026-09-26: After it ends: the sheet, the empty slot, voided and expired, and the memory it leaves

**The sheet** (3.24, 3.37): for someone who was in, the chalk "Add yours from Friday" ("Add a photo from Friday" while there are none) and the secondary "Send how it ended" with the whole tile at the second height, every visit, weeks later included; sending stays because the tile says there are photos once there are. For someone who wasn't in, "Send how it ended" alone, which goes once sent or once they leave (kept on the device, never as a record; the old settled sheet's rule, now applying to them only). A void or an expiry gets the chalk alone for someone who was in and no sheet for anyone else: no tile tells a void. **One picker** (`PhotoAdding`): the sheet's chalk and the empty slot open the same hidden input, the library first (no `capture`), several at once; each photo goes up in turn with the control pending until the last one lands; a failure leaves the block in the sheet with "Try again" and the rest still go up. Rejected: a second input per control, which would have let the slot and the chalk disagree about what was pending. **The empty slot** (3.8): a 120px dashed button with the ringed plus and "Add the first photo", for someone who can add and nobody else; it is a control, so its words sit outside the budget; while the first photo is going up it is the hatched placeholder with the viewer's credit chip. **Ended means settled, voided or expired** (`ENDED` in roles.ts): a void was still a night, so `addMarketPhoto` takes a memory on any of the three, and the voided and expired screens read as 3.37 gives them ("Nobody could tell." with "Nothing changes hands.", or "The terms didn't decide it." after the tiebreaker; "Never settled." with "Nobody said what happened before it closed for good."), the frame or the slot, the line with every pin and no wash, and no ranking. **The memory it leaves** (3.37) is the same route from the second calendar day in the viewer's zone (`daysBetween`, the way "yesterday" is counted): the band carries the date where the clock was, the frame at 260 leads, then the outcome and one line (the claim's line and who was closest), the line or ruler, what it left as one card grouped by owner (closed ones carry the settled mark from `closed_at`; the chain's forgiven mark is the person view's, not read here), and "The rest of that night": other markets and covers in the window from six hours before it closed to six after it ended that share two of its people with the viewer among them, at most five, oldest first, headed "The rest of that day" when it closed between 5am and 5pm. Closest first leaves the memory screen, as the design says. Rejected: a second route for the memory, which would have split one market's address in two.

## 2026-09-26: The settled screen to board 14, and outcome words

**The order** (3.37): the outcome line in the market's words, the claim's line as its caption, the frame at 200 (the claim's clip first) or the slot, the call line or ruler, "Closest first", "Who's got who" grouped by owner with the tokens under each owner's name and one caption naming who called it even, then the sheet. "What changes hands" and "Who was closest" are gone. The tiebreaker's ruling keeps its card and gets the same settled sheet; the build never had "That's fair" or "Push back", so there was nothing to remove, and the design confirms there was never anything behind them. **Outcome words** (3.25): the write-up returns four short phrasings with the terms (the two wells and the two settled lines), kept in a new `dares.outcome_words` column (migration `0022_outcome_words`, four or null) when all four are usable, and used on the wells, the claim card ("Priya says the kettle boiled dry"), the count line, the agree chalk ("Say it: the kettle boiled dry"), the settled line ("It boiled dry."), the story card, the Just happened row and the result tile. A market without them, any made before or a write-up that returned none, says "Yes" and "No" everywhere, so nothing breaks. After a name the phrase loses its capital only when it opens with a pronoun or a function word (`lowerFirst`): "Priya says he fell asleep", and "Priya says Gabe wore 14", because a name cannot be told from a pronoun by its capital alone. Rejected: `outcome_labels`, which already carries a number market's unit and a yes-or-no market's "no" and "yes", and would have had two shapes in one column; and lowering every first letter, which the real write-up ("John fell asleep") showed would misname people. The fixture was re-recorded with the four in it; the number market's "Add a note instead" tertiary and its caption arrived with the same sheet rewrite (3.24), and a person with a note and no vote is told so.

## 2026-09-26: The far-off number is a block

Replaces last phase's check a person could confirm past (docs/decisions.md 2026-09-25, "The median, after all, and the far-off check"). The number cannot be kept: tapping the primary at or past `farOffThreshold` sets the field's ring to `--ink` and puts one line under it with the alert glyph, "2,400 shirts is past the limit here. Try something under 1,000.", and the primary stays disabled until the number is under the limit, when the line goes with it. Nothing is sent. The reason is the person's: the scale is fixed at creation, so a far-off entry scores zero and moves nobody else, and it is the person who typed it who would lose their stake to a slipped finger; that is also why the limit stays a hundredfold and never tightens to catch a bold guess. The line names the limit, which is not the scoring scale; a scale the model set stays hidden, and the line is the same when the asker set one. It opens with the number because the number is what the line is about (4.6). The threshold rule is unchanged.

## 2026-09-26: The type budget: three baselines gone, and states counted on their own

The baseline file is empty and stays so. **Now**: Just happened is rows (4.7, the `Now` board as always drawn): a market's stamp, the question in `serif-m`, the state mark with the outcome in the market's words and who called it, a 44px thumbnail when there is media; a cover or a close as the 3.15 row with the owner's avatar. The story card left the root, and with it the second serif size. **The claimant screen**: the items are claim rows (3.38, 4.8), a 13px 600 kicker with the date, the subject in `body` 600 and one `body-sm` line with the token; the dead, used and own-link states moved to `ClaimGone`, one file, and the live state keeps its `serif-xl` alone. **The ask flow**: the echoed question on a later step is that step's serif line at 26, the who's-in and careful headlines are `body` 600, and the dare-instead line is `body` 600, all per 4.8's resolution; the flow then fits the four-size cap as one screen, so no per-step split was needed. **Counting per state**: the static lint gained `STATE_SCREENS`, files that render one state of a screen and are counted as their own screen and left out of their page's closure (Now's first-run and signed-out states, the claimant's gone states). That is the per-state count 4.8 asks of the DOM lint, done statically for the states that live in their own files; a state rendered inline still sums with its page. Rejected: a DOM lint, which needs a browser in the build, for a count the file split gives exactly; and leaving the baselines with the screens over budget, which the design resolved.

# Categorical markets: pick one (2026-09-26)

## 2026-09-26: Pick one is a single choice, and the deployed contract already scores it

The owner's decision (docs/design.md 3.30): picking one means choosing one answer and nothing else. No confidence, no odds line, no spread of the rest across the other answers, which read as the app deciding something the person never said. Mechanically every pick carries a confidence of exactly 10000 (`PICK_ONE_CONFIDENCE`, `confidenceFor`), so under the contract's Brier over a distribution the answer that happened scores 10000, any other scores 0, and the people who picked wrong pay the people who picked right, pairwise and capped as usual. **Checked first, as the brief required**: the deployed `DarefulDares` at its address in the environment accepts a confidence of exactly 10000 on two through six answers and scores a one-hot pick full or nothing (`scoreCategorical` by read-only call, every case in `tests/db/scoring-chain.test.ts`), refuses 10001, a pick or an outcome past the last answer and a single answer, and its `create` accepted five positions each carrying 10000 on a real market (the gas survey's `pickone` mode, then `tests/db/pick-one.test.ts`). No contract change. Rejected: a confidence short of everything with the remainder spread, which the doc took out; and a new scoring rule, which would need the redeploy the doc reserves for multi-choice (3.36).

**The mirror** (`scoreCategorical` in `src/lib/ledger/scoring.ts`) is written from the specification's formula (S = 10000 minus the sum of squared differences over 20000, the rest spread by integer division with the remainder dropped, docs/decisions.md 2026-09-16), not from the contract, and meets the contract's own table (9325, 3825, 8334, 7500) and the one-hot cases by read-only call. **Verified four ways**, as the other two kinds were: the worked example by hand first ("Who falls asleep first?", five answers, John does; Priya John at $5, Gabe Gabe at $10, Theo John at $20, Maya Nobody at $7.50, John Priya at $9; scores 10000, 0, 10000, 0, 0; six edges 125, 125, 125, 250, 187, 225, each truncated on its own; nets +375, -375, +662, -312, -350, summing to zero and inside every stake; the working is in `tests/unit/pick-one.test.ts`), then the mirror, then the deployed contract's own function, then a real market on Monad with exactly those picks (Gabe's vote for himself landing as a dissent, the third vote for John settling it) whose six minted edges match to the cent, and `verify-envio`, which now recomputes pick-one markets from the chain's positions (with the confidence each signed), the answer and the count of answers, and prints each one with how many called it.

**Ties are not failures.** Everyone picking the answer that happens scores everyone full; everyone picking wrong scores everyone nothing; both resolve with the outcome recorded, mint nothing and carry no toll (the collapse rule with a tie at either end, docs/decisions.md 2026-09-16), and `cleanResolutionOf` counts them clean: only a void counts against whoever wrote the terms. Checked on the chain with a two-person market both picked right (`Resolved` on the contract, no edges, nets of zero, the asker's clean rate 1 of 1).

## 2026-09-26: The answers, and where they live

Two to six answers (3.29), each a few words or a person, in the asker's order. The words live in `dares.outcome_labels` as PLANNING.md 5b has it ("option names for categorical"); which answers are people lives beside it in a new `dares.answer_people uuid[]` (migration `0023_answer_people`, with checks that a categorical market has two to six labels and that the two arrays align), the person's id where the answer is a person and null where it is words. Rejected: a table of answers, which every read of a market would have to join for six strings; and a JSON column, which the schema does not use anywhere else. The driver reads a null element of a uuid array back as the string "NULL" (the column holds a real null, asserted through SQL in the test), so `answersOf` is the one reader and counts only a well-formed id as a person. A person answer is anyone the asker knows in the app (someone they share a group with) or the asker themselves, as "You" (3.38); an id that is neither is refused at draft. Two answers that say the same thing are refused. The write-up (`scopePickOne`) is handed the answers and told not to rewrite, reorder or add to them; its terms say that if what happens is none of them the question cannot be settled, since an unlisted answer forces a void that counts against the asker, which is the one piece of advice the step gives ("If none of them might happen, add that too."). The people the asker is offered are those they have shared a market with, most recent first (`recentCompanions`), then the rest of the people they share a group with, by name; rejected: only those with a shared market, since 3.29 says a person answer can be anyone the asker knows here.
