Rejected: refusing to lock with a ghost in it (the tick would fail on it every minute and the question would never end), and minting the account-holders' edges at settlement without a confirm. Not built, and noted: dismissing a ghost from a market and re-scoring (PLANNING.md, "Dismissal"), a ghost's concession on a market proposal (built for covers only), and the claimant suggestion by name for market ghosts.

## 2026-09-27: The dev server takes the loopback origin

`allowedDevOrigins: ["127.0.0.1"]` in `next.config.ts`, development only: the loopback address is the one origin whose cookie jar is separate from localhost's in the development browser, which is how a visitor with no session is exercised beside the signed-in session. Without it Next.js 16 blocks the dev resources and the page renders and never hydrates there. Dynamic's own calls fail at that origin by CORS (its sandbox lists localhost), which touches nothing on the accountless path and means signing in from there is not a check that can be run; the phone list carries it.

## 2026-09-27: Monad's words for a colliding nonce

Twelve database suites sent through the one relayer side by side, and three chain tests failed with a send the node refused: "Missing or invalid parameters. Details: An existing transaction had higher priority". That is Monad's wording for a nonce another transaction already holds in its pool, the lagging-node collision the nonce retry exists for (2026-09-27, one lock for every sender), and the retry did not recognise it: it knew "nonce too low", "already known", "replacement transaction underpriced" and "invalid nonce". Each suite alone had passed. Recognised now (`isNonceProblem`), with the phrase in the unit test and a mutant that forgets it; the three suites then pass together. The scenario is the owner's own: several people confirming within seconds of each other through one relayer behind a load balancer. Nothing was lost on the chain either time: a refused broadcast is marked dropped before the person hears of it, and nothing had been told to anyone.

## 2026-09-27: Three rulings from the front-door check-in

The owner ruled: a typed name counts at once, and the asker can remove an entry from someone without an account before the lock (built below as the asker's "Remove" on a ghost's row while the question is open, which dismisses the position and leaves the ghost's seat; a removed ghost can enter again, since the link is still theirs); the signed-out page may name the asker and the group, and the preview that unfurls in a chat keeps to the 2026-09-18 rule (confirmed: `marketShare` puts the question in the title and the terms line in the description, no name and no group; the tile draws the asker's first name alone, as 3.27 draws it; the http suite now checks the description too); and delegation will be built past the gate, for host mode, once the design session's consent screen and host-mode boards arrive, and not before.

## 2026-09-27: The bugs that needed no design

**Back from a new question lands on Now.** The ask flow pushed the new question onto history, so the browser's own back (a swipe on a phone) returned to the flow it had just finished. Creating a question, and sending a game's questions, now replace the flow in history (`router.replace`), so back lands wherever the flow was opened from, Now from Start; the app's own back control already lands on a root and never on history (6.4). Verified in the real session: back from a question just asked landed on Now.

**A notification's tap opens its target.** The worker already focused and then navigated; two gaps closed: among several windows it now takes this app's own showing one (never a tab on another origin, never a hidden one first), and the page collects the remembered address on `focus` and `pageshow` as well as on `visibilitychange`, since the installed app on iOS does not always fire the last when it comes to the front. Both halves are tested against stand-ins (tests/unit/worker.test.ts); what iOS does is on the phone list, as before.

**The keyboard stays on a new answer.** The row a pick-one asker adds takes focus as it appears, inside the same tap, so the keyboard stays up; the number of answers at the moment of adding is what says which row is new.

**Photos open as an album.** The full-screen view takes every photo on the question and opens at the one tapped, swiped by native scroll snapping (a flick, a drag, the arrow keys), with the counter and the controls following whichever photo is in view. Verified in the real session on a question with two photos. Rejected: a carousel library (one more dependency for a swipe the browser already does).

**A set nobody named is shown by its people.** The cover form printed "Group" for each unnamed set. The one rule everywhere (`setLabel`) now names the form's sets too, and the http suite refuses the word. With the cover moved to a person's page the picker is not shown there at all.

**"I got this one" starts from the person it is for.** Off the Start sheet (which keeps asking, arguing and joining); the person's page already carried the move at its foot, a ghost's page too, and it now opens the cover form with that person chosen and nothing else to pick (`/new?for=`), where before it prefilled the person and still offered the set picker. The design session is drawing its final place. Rejected: a Start row that first asks who (a second picker for what the person's page already knows).

**The share text is the question alone.** The composer text, the notice body and the preview's line no longer say "put your number on it"; the design session is removing the phrase from the screens.

**A named subject the line does not place is asked about first.** Careful mode's questions assumed a bare name was a person (asked about Nova, a cat, it asked whether she might refuse to answer). The model may now answer with the name alone when the line does not say what it is and the questions would differ by it; the asker answers in one tap (a person, a pet, something else) and the three questions are written with that as fact. It costs a round only when there is real ambiguity: the recorded calls (tests/fixtures/anthropic/careful-subject.json, careful-questions-pet.json) show the ask for "does Nova refuse to come inside when it rains this week" and three pet questions once told. Verified in the real session with the question card as it is; the design session may redraw it. A subject given and asked about again is refused, so the flow never loops.

**The band under the tab bar on iOS is an iOS 26.0 bug.** The owner's numbers (a window of 830 on a screen of 874, insets of 62 and 34, a fixed element at bottom 0 landing at 830) are the shape of a WebKit regression in iOS 26.0: the standalone web app's viewport is laid out as if Safari's bottom bar were present, leaving a strip the page cannot draw into, and fixed elements sit above it. Apple's engineers acknowledged it (feedback FB19889436; WebKit bug 297779), iOS 26.0.1 does not fix it, and the 26.1 beta does. Nothing in how the app declares itself to iOS (`black-translucent`, `viewport-fit=cover`, `standalone`) causes it or cures it, and a workaround that pushes the bar down by the strip would be wrong on every fixed device. Ruled: no change; the check is the phone's iOS version, and 26.1 when it ships. Sources: Apple's developer forum thread 800125, the WebKit tracker, and reports gathered on the material-ui tracker (issue 46953).

**The unfolded Fold could not be made to fail here.** At two inner-screen sizes (725 by 604 and 874 by 787 CSS pixels) Now, People and a settled question render as on a phone, centred at the app's width. What fails on the owner's device is not known; the phone list asks for the screen and what it shows.

**Judges' test login: Dynamic's test accounts.** In the sandbox, any email with `+dynamic_test` before the @ and any US number with a 555 area code sign in with a static code set on the console's Test Accounts page (docs.dynamic.xyz, platform/dashboard/test-accounts); a live environment gets one specific email and no number. Not built: the submission notes carry an email of that form and the code the owner sets. The account's first login makes its two wallets as any account's does.

**Lifting a subject in the installed app: unverified.** No source says whether an iPhone's long-press on a photo inside a home-screen web app offers "Copy Subject" as Safari does; it is on the phone list with the exact check, and the sticker entry point that depends on it waits for the answer.

## 2026-09-27: The band under the tab bar is not the 26.0 bug, and the fix aims at the viewport iOS lays out short

The owner's phone runs iOS 26.6.2, past the WebKit regression the last round blamed (fixed in 26.1), and the band shows on People and You alone; Now and What's on are fine. The four roots are built the same way: each renders `Screen` inside the one body (`html` at `height: 100%`, `body` at `min-height: 100%`, the ground on both), the document is what scrolls (no inner scroll container anywhere in the shell), the tab bar is the same `TabBar` at `bottom: 0` with its own inset, and nothing on any of them is positioned by the inset but the Start button. The one difference is content height: People and You fit the screen, and Now and What's on usually run past it. So whatever iOS does, it does to a page that never scrolls.

The owner's earlier readings (a window of 830 on a screen of 874, insets of 62 and 34, a fixed box at `bottom: 0` landing at 830) are the shape reported for iOS 26 home-screen web apps by others this month: the page is laid out one bar short of the screen and painted from the top, so a bottom-pinned box sits above the real edge with a strip of the manifest's background colour under it (the same ground, which is why it reads as the app's). One report on 26.6.1 (a 47px shortfall on an 844px phone, WebKit bug 301108) found the shortfall did not depend on the page being scrollable, and tried heights, overflow and reflows without moving it; another found that `100lvh` was the screen while `innerHeight`, `100dvh` and the visual viewport were the short height, and closed its strip by sizing the body at `100lvh` under a transform so fixed layers reached the real bottom. Sources: github.com/enkeldesign/webb/pull/1009, github.com/tpdbf5509/busssss/pull/23, github.com/masumi-network/sokosumi/pull/4666, github.com/ethan8damax/Coffee-SNOB/pull/7 (which also records that the units disagree with each other across status-bar styles, so nothing here assumes which is right).

**Built:** one custom property, `--viewport-gap`, zero everywhere and, in the installed app only (`display-mode: standalone`), `min(0px, calc(100dvh - 100lvh))`: the distance from the viewport the page was laid out in to the large viewport, never positive. Every bottom-pinned box (the tab bar, the Start button, the pinned sheet, a modal sheet, the photo view) sits at `bottom: var(--viewport-gap)` instead of `bottom: 0`, so it is pushed down to the screen's real edge by exactly the strip, and by nothing where there is no strip. Where the two units disagree the other way (one report saw `dvh` larger than `lvh` under `black-translucent`), the clamp makes it zero, which is today's behaviour. The probe on You is on Now as well now, and prints the three units, the gap applied, and whether the page scrolls, so the owner's next two readings (a page with the band, one without) say whether `lvh` is the screen on that phone. **Rejected:** making the two short roots scroll by a pixel or by `100lvh` of minimum height (a fit page would bounce, the report on 26.6.1 says scrollability is not the cause, and it helps only if iOS re-lays out on a gesture); a body at `100lvh` under a transform (the transform makes the body the containing block of every fixed layer, which is the class of bug 1.5 warns about and the pull-to-refresh runner and the status band would fall into it); waiting for Apple (the band is on two of four roots on a current iOS). Nothing here runs iOS, so the fix is verified in the desktop browser only as a no-op (the units agree there, the bar is where it was, tests/http/pages.test.ts holds the class on the bar); the phone check is item 61.

## 2026-09-27: The eleventh design session, the looks

What changes how screens look, built to the doc and committed apart from what changes the rules (the owner's brief). Each item names the section, and where the doc did not cover something, the choice and its alternative.

**Sharing in one place** (3.42, `WhosInRow`). Once you're in, the who's-in row is the 28px stack with the count under it and, at the right end, three 44px icon buttons: share (the phone's share sheet with the question as the title and the link; where there is no share sheet it copies and says so as copy does), copy (the glyph turns to a check for 1.5s and the name becomes "Link copied" through a live region; no toast), and the code to scan (a modal sheet: the question, a 216px code on a chalk plate at the error level that survives the market's mark in a 44px stamp over its middle, and the six characters as read-only boxes, fetched when the sheet opens). "They're right here: show a code", "Send the link", "Copy" and "Send how it ended" are gone with their components (`RoomCode`, `SettledSheet`, `InviteShare` stays for the game page and a ghost's link). While you're the only one in a market you asked, the count reads "Just you so far" and share is a chalk circle; the first person in returns it to an icon. The row shows in every state you're in (open, locked, voting) and on a settled market for anyone who can see it, where share sends the same link and the tile route decides which tile previews; a void or an expiry has no row, since no tile tells a void. **Decided beyond the doc:** a ghost who is in gets share and copy and not the code, because the code is made through the asker's session and a ghost has none; and when the clipboard refuses a copy (a browser that needs the page focused, or asks and is told no) the link appears under the row in a read-only field to copy by hand, because a tap is never silently dropped (5.2). Rejected: a toast on copy (5.4), and a `prompt()` fallback (found in the real session to throw where a browser does not support it, leaving the tap silent).

**Photos, one slot** (3.8, 3.37, 3.39). While a market is open and you're in, your photos sit last on the screen under the details as the same 120px slot, then the same 200px frame and strip as after it ends, with the add tile (a 60px dashed square, named for the night) ending the strip and one caption under it, "Everyone sees these once it's over."; the picker is the camera while it is open and the library once it has ended, as before. "Yours from tonight", the camera button beside sending, and the sheets on the settled, voided, expired and memory screens are gone (`OpenPhotos`, `CameraButton`, `SettledSheet`, `NightSheet`); the game's night has the add tile or the slot where its sheet was. The locked and voting screens show nothing of the photos taken before lock, as 3.39 says. The frame's strip shows three squares and "+N" before the add tile, which is never pushed off the row.

**The explainer cuts** (4.9, the `CutList` board). Every line the board lists is gone: the draft's paragraph (the band wears a dashed edge, "Not sent yet" after the dotted ring, and the people glyph with "For the Friday crew", its people named when it has no name), "Put your number on it" everywhere, the notifications paragraph (now the heads-up sheet, below), "John was out" and the dissent's example in the claim's field, the compact code field's example (six boxes now, at 48px with a 6px gap beside Join), the link page's number example and "so this one is yours" ("Your name" and "Your phone number" over empty fields, and the caption 4.9 keeps: "Nothing gets sent to it. Sign in with this number later and your entries are waiting."), the naming prompt's example, the picker's search examples ("Search") and its hueless line, the mark row's second sentence ("Optional" alone), first run's line, who's in's "add anyone else" line and its caption in 3.38's words, the shared-context band's "Tap one", the number field's "Any whole number", What's on's line, starting a game's two captions, joining's three lines (the screen is "Got a code?", the six boxes, one caption naming the letters no code uses, and "Paste a link" as a row action that reads the clipboard, with the field only where the clipboard cannot be read), the claimant screen's two sentences, the question's and the answers' placeholders. Kept, as 3.29 says "as built": the captions under the kind and mode chips on the question step, which the board does not list. **Nothing here explains the marks in words:** "Numbers show when everyone's in" leaves with the blind rule in the next commit, since 3.31 cuts it "with the rule".

**The pick-one sheet** (3.30). It opens raised, since picking is the move; a swipe down lowers it to one bar, "Pick one" on the left and the count of answers on the right, or, once picked, the pick's avatar, its name and a check; a touch on the bar raises it. The answers, the stake chips and the primary all live in the raised part, so the bar stands alone when lowered. `PinnedSheet` gained the rule for every sheet: a touch on the header row raises a lowered sheet, and only the grabber or a swipe lowers it.

**"I got this one"** (3.43, `CoverSheet`). The person view's sheet (and a ghost's page's) holds the chalk at rest and raises into the cover: what (the pair's units most used first, the presets, units this person named elsewhere, "$", and "Something else" as a field), how many (a 48px minus, the count and unit, a 48px plus; the amount in dollars under "$"), who picks up next as two rows that each show what they'll make (the token in 3.2's proposed style, selected, or "Nobody's paying it back" after a dot in your hue), and one primary that says the whole thing ("I got Claude a beer"). Logged, the sheet lowers and the page re-reads with the token. The two rows are the old "Expecting to square it?" (`settle_expected`) in the design's words: "Nobody's paying it back" is what the rally counts (3.11). The + only asks: Start is a plain link to the question step named "Ask something", the Start sheet is deleted, and "Got a code?" sits at the question step's top right and heads the joining screen. **Left out and asked about:** "Add a photo of it" at logging (3.43, row 4): Principle 6 puts the photo at the moment an obligation closes, and `media` has no home for a photo on an unconfirmed proposal (its two parents are a market and a confirmed obligation), so building it needs a schema decision. **Also asked:** the memo and the private cost note ("What it cost, if you want to remember") are not in the raised sheet the doc draws; Principle 4 (precision optional, retained invisibly off the chain) rests on that note, so the doc's omission may be deliberate or an oversight. `/new` stays as a route with no entry point until a cover for a group is placed (7), because it is the only place a total is split.

Two marks that belong to a tap: on its way (the ring at 0.32 with a quarter at full, turning once every 1.2s, at rest under reduced motion) and didn't go through (the ring with the alert inside). The actions for the person's own kinds (yep, yep-all, settle, call it even, cancel out, the lock, a vote that decided it, the tiebreaker) answer ok with a pending flag where they answered the "Sent, and still going through" sentence, so the screen moves on; a pending registration stays a refusal, since nothing of the person's was recorded. What is on its way (`onWayFor`: told sends pending, or mined and not yet mirrored) shows where it lives with the mark and the words "On its way", never a clock: a yep leaves Needs you and sits in Just happened as an on-its-way row; the asker's lock leaves Needs you and runs with the mark; a settlement or a cancelling out is a Just happened row and, on the person view, the card with the mark ("Settled · on its way") in place of the control; the band on a market wears it while the lock or the resolution this person decided is going through. What never landed (`againRowsFor`, unchanged in what it finds) now wears the didn't-go-through mark with "Didn't go through" and the verb "Try again" on every Now row it touches (the yep row, the lock row, a settlement's or a cancelling out's own row), and the person view's card carries the same line above it with "Try again" opening the sheet. **Kept:** the twenty-second wait for a receipt, so a send that lands in the ordinary few seconds is shown as done, as today. **Rejected:** answering at broadcast and letting the tick mirror everything (the design's "the screen does not wait"), because the tick runs once a minute and every tap would then wear the mark for up to a minute; the next step, if the owner wants the shorter wait, is a completion the screen itself can run on re-read. Delegation's "getting in" case does not arise yet: a position before lock is offchain, so entering never waits on a send.

Added, since the section requires it: once answered, the careful step opens with one line, "Nova is a pet", and Change reopens the three choices; changing rewrites the questions and keeps the answers to any question that survives the rewrite word for word. Verified with two real model calls in the localhost session (a pet, then a person: different questions each time).

**The heads-up** (4.10, `HeadsUp`). One modal sheet, once, on a market screen where this person is in, after the entering moment: the bell and "Want a heads-up?", two marked rows ("When it's your turn", "When it's decided"), the chalk "Turn on notifications", which raises the phone's own prompt and subscribes, and "No thanks". A yes, a no, the phone's own no or a dismissal ends it: the answer is kept on the account (`users.heads_up_answered_at`, migration 0029, applied before this build deploys so the deployed one keeps working), so no phone asks again, and email carries what push would have. ## 2026-09-27: Round A, the rulings: the band properly, the album open the whole time, the cover's notes, and "Got a code?" on Now

**The band, properly this time.** The owner's readings settled it against the last entry's fix. Every reading taken on a page that scrolls was perfect (a window of 874, the tab bar from 775 to 874, a fixed box landing at 874 of 874), the band appeared only on a page that fit the screen without scrolling (which is why Now and What's on never had it), and opening the probe fixed You because the probe made You tall enough to scroll (940 on an 874 screen). The gap adjustment (`--viewport-gap`, pushing every bottom-pinned box down by `100dvh - 100lvh` in the installed app) overcorrected on the short pages and pushed the tab bar off the bottom. **Built:** the gap is gone, every bottom-pinned box is back at `bottom: 0`, and every root (`Screen root`: Now with its first-run state, What's on, People, You) has `min-height: calc(100lvh + 1px)`, so the document always scrolls by at least a pixel and iOS lays the page out the way it lays out every page that scrolls. The probe stays, without its gap line, until the phone confirms (docs/testing.md item 61, now with the probe closed). **Rejected:** the same minimum on every screen (the band was never reported under a pinned sheet on a task screen, and a page that scrolls by a pixel for no reason is a smell on the desktop; if a short task screen ever shows it, `root` becomes the default); a taller margin than a pixel (nothing in the readings says how much is enough beyond "scrolls", and the phone check names the next step if a pixel is not); measuring in JavaScript (the fix is one line of CSS, and the probe already reports whether the page scrolls). **Assumed, not read:** that a page taller by one pixel counts as one that scrolls for the layout iOS chooses. The phone decides.

**The album is open the whole time** (3.37 and 3.39, amended). The owner's decision: from the first photo onward, everyone the market's rule admits (its participants and the group it was asked in) sees every photo, while it is open, through the vote and after; it is a social album, and keeping photos private until the end risked stranding them on markets that never finish. **Built:** the rule changed at the door (`canSee` no longer asks a memory's author or the market's state; the participants and the group are admitted from the moment a memory lands), in the frame (`frameItems` takes no state; `memoryVisible` and `yoursItems` are gone, with "Yours from tonight" and the `yours` split), and on the screen (the same slot and frame as after the end, last under the details, while open and while being called, for everyone admitted; the add tile and the empty slot for someone who can add, which before the end is someone in while it is open; the album while being called is the memories alone, since the claim's clip stays on the claim card). The caption "Everyone sees these once it's over." is gone, since nothing is true for it to say. A memory still never reaches the model (`evidenceFor` reads evidence alone; the test holds it). Someone who has opened the link but not entered is admitted nowhere: the link page shows no photos, and the door answers a signed-in stranger 404, both tested. **Kept:** the timeline's story frame, the tile's "With photos from that night." and "The rest of that night" for markets that have ended (their callers pick those ids; `frameOnMarkets` itself no longer filters), because a story card is a settled thing (3.4). **Rejected:** showing the frame on the open screen only to someone who is in, with the door admitting the group (a screen hiding what the door serves is the mismatch the owner's "in the door's rule, not only the interface" rules out); a ghost who entered seeing photos (a ghost has no session for the door to authorise; the link page shows none either way, and the design says the same, 3.17).

**The cover's notes** (3.43, amended). The photo at logging stays out (Principle 6). The memo and the private cost come back, since the design report never mentioned dropping them and Principle 4 rests on them: optional, below the main choices so the sheet still opens short, under one label "Optional", with no example text in either field (4.9); "What was it" on every cover and "What it cost" with "Only you see this." on a cover that is not in dollars, the cents going to `amount_cents` as the magnitude the ledger keeps invisibly. **Rejected:** the cost field on a dollar cover too (the amount is the cost there, and a second number would be a second act); a caption on the memo (the field's name says it).

**"Got a code?" on Now** (6.1, amended). At Now's top right, on the date's line, placed as the question step places it (a tertiary link to the joining screen), so joining by code is one tap from home. An empty Now keeps the compact boxes. **Rejected:** a row in Now's body (Now routes and never starts, and a row would read as something waiting); replacing the empty Now's boxes with the same link (the boxes are already zero taps).

## 2026-09-27: Round A, the rules half: swipes on Now, holdouts and the blind rule, and the link page's number

What changes rules, built to the eleventh session's doc and committed apart from the looks (the owner's brief). Migration 0030 (`now_archive`, `claim_number_attempts`, `dare_positions.bound_claim`, and `'removed'` admitted by the `resolved_by` check) is additive and was applied before this build deploys, so the deployed one keeps working.

**Swipes on Now** (3.15, `CallOff`). A Running row for a market you asked that nobody else is in slides over the remove square; a Just happened row for a finished market slides over the archive square; a tap on either asks once in a modal sheet in the doc's words, then the row collapses over 200ms and the re-read takes the section with it if it emptied. No hint teaches the swipe; the square is a real button, so a keyboard reaches it. **The contract question answers itself:** an open market is not on the chain (a market goes onchain at lock, and a lock takes two), so removing one is offchain entirely and no early ending is asked of the deployed contract; the stake never left. Removing writes `resolved_by = 'removed'` with the void outcome and the lock time, so the state derives as voided without a new state (`stateOf` is untouched); the market's own screen reads "Called off Sun at 6:52pm", "Called off." and "Nobody else got in." for anyone the link reached; `marketCards` skips a removed market, so it is off Now, off every timeline and never in Just happened; You's count (3.34, the next part) leaves it out as it does expiry. Archiving is a row per person in `now_archive`, read by `nowFor` for Just happened alone. **Rejected:** a new `MarketState` for removed (every switch over states would grow a branch for a screen only the link reaches); archiving a game row (a game is many markets and the doc names markets; asked about below); the row leaving Now with the market expiring at its close (the doc's fallback, which is only for a contract that cannot end early, and nothing here is on the contract). **The `--remove` token is a red** (1.1: `#E5534B`, "behind the remove glyph in Now's swipe action, and nowhere else"), which 4.5's "no red anywhere" would forbid; the session's own table admits it for this one square, and it is used nowhere else. Flagged at the check-in.

**Holdouts and the close** (3.42, 3.1). The people the market was sent to who are not in yet (the set's account-holders without a position, the same people the nudge used to name) follow the stack as dashed avatars, at most two drawn then a dashed "+N", and the count says "4 of 6 in"; while the asker is alone the count stays "Just you so far" and share stays the chalk, since getting people in is still the only move (3.42, "Just you so far"), with the holdouts drawn beside. "Lock it in" is "Close it" everywhere: the button ("Close it with 4", "It takes two to close it"), the server's refusal and the waiting line. The asker's close asks once in a modal sheet with the dashed avatars of who it leaves out and one line naming them; where nobody is left out it closes without asking, since it leaves nobody out. **The stack is a button** opening who's in: one row per person with "Asked it" or "From the link, no account", and for the asker before the lock a Remove on a ghost's row that asks inside the same sheet; the plain "In without an account" list is gone. **Nothing else nudges:** the nudge (the action, the notice, the targets, the six-hour window's name) and the relay text are removed; a netting keeps the window under its own name (`windowSeq`, `NOTICE_WINDOW_MS`). **Rejected:** keeping the nudge behind the icons (3.42 says the icons are how holdouts are reached and nothing else nudges); a blind market with no close time closing when everyone asked is in (not built: no such market can be asked yet, 3.38's open question).

**Blind is final, not hidden** (3.22, 3.31; the session's "What changed", item 7). `numbersVisible` no longer reads the reveal mode: nothing before you're in and everything after, on every market. What makes a blind market blind is that an entry is final once made, enforced on the server for account-holders (`enterMarket` refuses a change on a blind market; the same numbers again pass, since keeping a bound ghost's unsigned numbers is the signature) and for ghosts (`enterAsGhost`; an entry the asker removed may come in again). The screen says so once before anyone commits ("You see everyone's once you're in. Yours is final then.", after the lock glyph, above the primary), the entry line's caption reads "final" and Change is not drawn; the lock chip and "Numbers show when everyone's in." are gone from the weight line, the pick-one bars, the number line and the ruler's hidden state, and the details' sentence says the new rule. The `blind` picture kind is gone with them. **Rejected:** hiding the aggregate while showing the columns (the doc shows "everything that is in so far, heights, marker and all").

**The link page** (3.17, the end; the owner's two additions). Suggestions ("Is one of these you?") appear only once two letters are typed, at most three, and only from people without an account who joined this set's markets from a link with a number (`suggestGhostNames`: an undismissed position on one of the group's markets and a phone hash), so a name that joined without a number is never suggested; picking one fills the field with a check. A picked name joins only with the number it joined with before: any other number, or none, is the field error in the doc's words, at the number field; and because the check confirms or denies a number, wrong tries are counted per name (`claim_number_attempts`, one row per wrong try, nothing of the number) and past five in an hour the check stops answering even to the right number ("Too many tries for Dani. Give it an hour, or type your own name."). People can join without a number: the primary waits for the name alone, and for the number too only when a name was picked. **Rejected:** a rate limit per browser or address (a ghost has no identity, and the thing being protected is the name); suggestions from the group's member list (a ghost joins the set as a member, but the doc's rule is "joined this group's markets from a link", which is a position, and a member picked by the asker with no entry is not one).

**Stone everywhere** (3.1, 3.17). Someone without an account wears the stone avatar with its dashed ring wherever they appear: the who's-in stack and list, the call line's pins (the story card's too), the ruler's pins, the leaderboards, the pick rows and the pick bars' pickers, and their own entry line on the link page. `Pin`, `Standing`, `NumberStanding`, `Picker` and the ruler's pins carry a `ghost` flag from `participantsOf`.

**Link entries on the claimant screen** (3.38). A phone login binds a ghost's positions to the account as before, and the bind now records the ghost on each (`dare_positions.bound_claim`), so the claimant screen lists every unsigned entry on an open market as a claim row by the name it was typed under ("You're in at 70% · 2 beers", the question in the caption), pressed by default, with the one chalk counting pressed rows across covers and entries. Keeping one is its signature (the same numbers, signed by this account through `enterMarketAction`); leaving one out (`leaveEntry`) sends the position back to a fresh ghost with the typed name, no number and the seat in the set, so no login ever binds it again and it stands in stone. The screen opens for someone whose only claim is an entry ("Your entries were waiting."). **Rejected:** listing entries on locked markets (nothing can be signed after the lock; a provisional lock already carries the unsigned position as the ghost's); binding only after the person chose (the bind at login is what makes the entry theirs to sign at all, and the claimant screen is the same moment).

**Also.** The "Who's in" list and the remove ask, the swipe's ask, and the close's ask are three modal sheets on the app's own `Sheet`, none of which opens another. A game row on Now does not answer a swipe (3.15 names markets; whether a game archives as one is asked about).

## 2026-09-27: Round B, part 0: Round A's remainder

The owner's four rulings on Round A's check-in, the guided sticker path, the You page and the storage report, in their own commit (the owner's brief for Round B).

**The red square stays** (1.1, 4.5 amended). Kept behind the remove swipe only; 4.5 now points at the exception 1.1 wrote rather than forbidding red everywhere. **Rejected:** an ink-coloured square (the owner's ruling keeps the table's colour).

**A game on Now swipes as one row** (3.15 amended; `collapseGames`, `removeMarkets`, `archiveMarkets`). The Running row for a game removes its questions together, and only when every question of the game in this set is here, this person's, open and with nobody else in; the Just happened row archives them together once finished. Several go all or nothing: every question is checked before any is written, so a game with a friend in one question is not half removed, and a row stays on Now until every question it stands for is archived. The running row now carries its set (`groupId`) so the collapse is pure; the module-level map that held it is gone. The ask names the game ("Remove this game?", "Its questions leave Now, and they count against nobody."). **Rejected:** a per-question swipe inside the game row (a game is one row, 4.7); removing when the viewer asked every question but a friend is in one (the ruling says nobody else in any of them).

**The count while the asker is alone** (3.42 amended). When the asker named people, the holdouts rule from the first entry ("1 of 6 in", with the dashed avatars); "Just you so far" only when nobody was named; share stays the chalk while alone either way. **Rejected:** "Just you so far" beside dashed avatars (the two rules contradicted on that screen, and the owner chose).

**The nudge and the relay are back** (3.42 amended; `Nudge`, `sendNudge`, `nudgeAction`, `nudgeTargets`, `relayText`). Restored as they were before the rules half removed them, for entering and for voting: the card under the who's-in row for anyone who is in, the six-hour window per person per question (`nudgeSeq`, which the netting shares again; `windowSeq` is gone), the honest report of what a channel took, and the relay through the person's own composer. New: who's in lists the people still out ("Not in yet" while open, "Still to call it" once locked) with "Nudge Maya" beside each for anyone who is in (`nudgeTargets` takes `only`, which never widens the set: still never the nudger, never someone done, never someone outside the pool), and the relay row in the button's place when no device of theirs takes messages. The old tests and mutants are back verbatim, with three new ones for the one-name nudge. **Rejected:** a nudge from outside the market (Principle 1: a person acting, from inside); a nudge in the sheet alone without the card (the owner said wherever they were removed).

**A sticker from a photo** (3.28 amended, frames 1 to 3; `StickerSheet`, `StickerMade`, `PhotoView`, `Sheet clear`). "Make a sticker" beside "Save" in the full-screen photo, as 44px icon buttons with their words, when a cutout can be stored; the guided sheet with the two steps and the chalk "Paste", running the picker's own paste path (the clipboard read, or a paste event anywhere while the sheet is up); the result in the viewer's place with "In your stickers", the chalk "Ask something with it" (`/m/new?sticker=<id>`, honoured only for this person's own sticker, `initialStickerMark`) and "Done" back to the photo. The sheet is the app's `Sheet` with a new `clear` mode: no scrim, touches pass around the panel, so the photo can still be held for the phone's own Copy Subject; it closes by its close or its handle. The photo's long-press is left to the phone (no `touch-callout` or `select-none` on the image), tested. **Rejected:** a one-off panel instead of the sheet (the next round restructures the shared pieces for motion, and a one-off would need retrofitting); cutting inside the app (frame 5, a later round); a modal sheet with a scrim (it would take the hold that the whole path depends on).

**The You page** (3.34 amended; `src/lib/ledger/you.ts`, `src/components/you/*`, `calibrationFor` extended). Built to 3.34 and the `YouEarly` board: the header's caption ("In 43 markets since March", "Joined today"); "How your calls land" as one line while nothing has resolved, the frame with only the diagonal and the count as a fact under ten with the calls as rows, and the reliability plot after (318 by 236, dots by bin at (mean said, share happened) with radius 3 + 1.6 root n over an 80% Wilson whisker, the headline in counts naming the fullest bin, the fuller and then the bolder between equals); "Numbers" with the middle half of the misses as the band (the two hinges), the average as the cream mark and "guessing the middle" at 25%, floor five, nothing with none; "Questions you asked" in counts with one mark per counted question and the caption naming the void and the expiries; and Account as rows. Three readings logged in 3.34: no "One tap" row (pass the phone takes its place next part), "Your number" with its caption and no chevron (the number is never held here), and "Your units" and "Your marks" as read-only sheets. The final score settling a question scores it like a quorum, so those calls count (3.35). **Rejected:** a percentage anywhere in the headlines (3.34: counts say how much a claim rests on); a floor per bin (a thin bin's long whisker is the honest picture); the normal interval (it runs past 0 and 1 on two calls); reading the sign-in number from Dynamic for the row (a live call on every load of a root, for a row the doc gives no screen behind).

**Storage during judging**, read on 2026-09-27 from the Supabase project and its docs, reported at the check-in and not changed: the organization is on the Free plan; 14 objects, 433 kB in the private bucket, the database 26 MB; a stored photo weighs about 36 kB at 1080px (79 kB at most so far) and 5 kB as its 256px square, a sticker about 94 kB source plus 25 kB stamp; the plan's limits are 1 GB of files, 500 MB of database, 5 GB uncached plus 5 GB cached egress a month, and Free projects with too little database activity over a week are paused (a warning email a week before, resume by hand from the dashboard within ninety days). The Pro plan removes pausing and raises the limits (100 GB files, 250 GB egress each way).

**The One tap ask** (3.41). Not built, and by the owner's brief will not be: on the phone holding the login routine actions already show no confirmation, so "Skip this step next time?" has nothing to skip; pass the phone takes its row (part 2).

## 2026-09-28: Round B, part 2: delegation reshaped around pass the phone

The owner's brief, after the check-in: the table of 2026-09-27 ("What delegation would remove, flow by flow") showed that on the phone holding the login routine actions already show no confirmation, so One tap's premise (skip a step) is false there. Delegation's real uses are pass the phone and a second device without the Dynamic login.

**No "Skip this step next time?" ask** (3.41 amended). Never shown; the note in 3.41 says why. **The row on You is "Pass the phone"** (3.34, 3.45), in One tap's place, with the 48 by 28 switch 3.41 drew. Turning it on is one flow on the person's own phone (the device state `ready`, the login and both keys here): the sheet says what it allows ("Getting into a market from a friend's phone, with your PIN"), what it never allows ("Saying what happened, voting, settling, or calling it even"), that it can be turned off any time, and takes the PIN twice; the chalk delegates the ledger wallet through the SDK's own call (`delegateKeyShares`, no screen of Dynamic's) and then sets the PIN (`turnOnPassThePhoneAction`), and the row waits up to fifteen seconds for the webhook to land the share before it reads on. On a device without the login the switch is disabled and the caption says "Turn it on from the phone you signed in on." Turning it off (`turnOffPassThePhone`) wipes the stored material now (`wipeDelegation`, the row kept as the record as Dynamic's own revocation keeps it), clears the PIN, and revokes at Dynamic's end from the person's own phone; it asks nothing (3.41). The switch always says what is true: on means a usable delegation and a PIN, both. **Rejected:** a separate PIN row under One tap (3.45's "Your PIN for a friend's phone"; one row, one flow, by the brief); asking Dynamic's server to revoke from the app's server (the read-only token cannot, and the person's own SDK call is the honest revocation; the wipe here is what makes off immediate either way).

**The server's share only where the device cannot sign** (`useSigner`, `signFromThisDeviceAction`, `delegatedSignatureFor`, `typedDataFor`, `Via`). Every signer call site now names its action for the server (enter, create, confirm, confirm_many, close, net) beside the typed data it signs on the device. On the phone holding the login nothing changes: the device signs. On a second device without the login, when pass the phone is on and the address is the ledger wallet, the signer first asks the server, which rebuilds the typed data from the named ids with the same builders the action verifies against (`enterTypedData`, `createTypedData`, `confirmTypedData`, `confirmManyTypedData`, `closeTypedData` from `closeState`, `netTypedData` from `netNonce`), refuses anything that is not this person's to sign, signs with the delegated share and records it against the request; a null answer means the code step, as before. A client never sends typed data to be signed: `Via` parses the action and its ids and drops everything else, and a vote is not a kind at all. **Rejected:** a generic "sign this typed data" action (a stolen session could then have the share sign anything the ledger wallet can; per action, the server signs only messages it built itself for that person); signing on the server on the phone that holds the login too (the brief: as rarely as possible).

**The PIN** (`pass_the_phone`, migration 0031, additive and applied before this deploys). Four digits, hashed with scrypt (2^14, 8, 1) under a sixteen-byte salt of its own, never logged, never returned; five wrong tries in a row lock it for an hour (`afterWrongTry`, counted under a row lock so two phones cannot share the allowance), the fifth try's owner is told once per lockout on their own account by push else email, naming whose phone ("Five wrong tries on Sam's phone. Nothing got in."), and a right PIN clears the count. `notification_log` gains the kinds `pin_locked` and `entered_from` (the second for part 3). **Rejected:** argon2 (a native package the deploy would have to trace; scrypt is in Node); locking for good (the person would have to reach the owner to fix it; an hour, told, is enough for four digits at five tries an hour); a lockout notice at Dynamic's end (nothing of Dynamic's knows about the PIN).

**What the gate established still holds:** the governance wallet refused in three places and marked denied at creation (untouched); a delegated signature only inside that person's own authenticated request, recorded against it (`signFromThisDeviceAction` runs under the person's session and names itself as the request); the signer answering a signature or nothing (`trySignWithDelegation` unchanged); revocation wiping the material (now from the app's side too). The development page stays as the gate's instrument.

**A cost noted:** the layout reads pass the phone's status on every load (two small queries) so a second device knows it may ask the server; it could be one query, or a claim in the session, if it ever shows.

**Two corrections alongside** (2026-09-28). A removed market's band read "Called off soon", because `closesLabel` says "soon" of any moment that has passed; `endedLabel` says when it was ("at 6:52pm", "Sun at 6:52pm", "Sep 12 at 6:52pm"), found by the design audit in part 1. The see-through sheet from part 0 had a drop shadow, which 1.5 admits nowhere; it is gone, and the sheet's border does the work.

## 2026-09-28: Round B, part 3: handing over the phone, and the link page's steps

**Handing over** (3.45, `src/lib/ledger/hand-over.ts`, `src/components/markets/hand-over.tsx`, `/m/[id]/pass`). The fourth icon on the who's-in row, for someone who is in while the market is open; the first tap explains it once in a modal sheet in 3.42's words, remembered on the account (`users.hand_over_explained_at`, migration 0033, additive). The icon replaces the host's screen in history with the friend's (`router.replace`), so back never returns to the entry, and the handback replaces it again with the host's screen; nothing of the friend's is stored on the phone, no session, nothing cached, since the request runs under the host's session throughout. The friend's screen is what the link page shows before anyone is in (the band with the open mark, the two facts, the entry sheet in host mode) and nothing else: no who's-in row, no weight line, no number of anyone's, the host's included, whether the market is blind or open. "Who's joining?" lists the people the market was sent to who aren't in yet (`handOverCandidates`), the ready ones first and the rest at 0.45 with "Not set up for this yet", and the tertiary "No account? Scan the code with your own phone" opens the code sheet. The PIN step is the friend's 56px avatar and name, "Your PIN", four dots and a keypad of 56px keys; the fourth digit sends. **The order on the server** (`enterFromHost`): the host must be in and the market open, the friend must be a candidate with pass the phone on, the PIN is checked first and counted against the friend's lock with the host named, and only then the friend's own delegated share signs the entry the server built (`delegatedSignatureFor`, the same path as a second device), recorded against the request and with the host on the position (`dare_positions.entered_by`). Three wrong tries end the handoff on the phone and nothing is said to anyone; the fifth in a row locks the PIN and tells its owner (part 2). One notice on the friend's account, "You entered this from Sam's phone.", by push else email (`notifyEnteredFrom`, `entered_from`). **Rejected:** a session for the friend on the host's phone (nothing of theirs may stay); showing the host's own entry line during the friend's turn (3.45: not even the phone owner's number); a client-side PIN check (the PIN never leaves the server's hash).

**A watched PIN stays fixable** (3.45, amended). In an open market the friend changes the entry from their own phone until the close, as any entry. On a blind market the entry is final, so its owner can withdraw it from their own phone before the close (`withdrawHostedEntry`: the position dismissed, "Withdraw it" on the entry line asking once), and can never enter that market again (`withdrewHostedEntry`, refused in `enterMarket` for the person's own phone and the host's alike), so the remedy is never a way around blind. The entry line names where it came from ("from Sam's phone"). **Rejected:** letting the owner re-enter after a withdrawal (a second look at everyone's numbers, which is what blind forbids); withdrawing a self-made blind entry (final means final).

**The link page's steps** (3.17, from the audit; `MarketStage`, `DeadLink`). "Who's joining?" is its own step raised by the primary, with the entry's summary on the right, the fields, the caption, the chalk waiting for the name alone (and the number too when a name was picked) and "Have an account? Sign in" in the sheet; the name chips are 40px (`Chip` gains the size); the facts are two; signed in on a link, "Joining as Sam · Not you?" sits under the primary, and "Not you?" asks once ("Not Sam? Sign out of this phone, and join as yourself from the link.") before signing this phone out, since that is a moment worth a pause; a revoked or malformed link is the code screen with a form-level line, signed in or not (`DeadLink`, replacing "Nothing to see here yet." and the signed-in 404). **Rejected:** "Not you?" signing out on the tap (the phone may be the person's own, handed to a friend by mistake).

## 2026-09-28: Round C, fidelity: the audit sorted by direction

The owner's brief for Round C: the audit compares the build with `docs/design.md`, but the doc is not automatically right, and its grouping mixed removals with additions. So every item in the fourth group was sorted first. **A fix that removes or corrects something was built** (a banned word, a raw error, stray text, an extra section, wrong behaviour). **A fix that adds words or an element was built only where the owner pre-approved it** (the not-found and error pages and the error card, the focus outlines, the who's-joining fallback, cutting a sticker inside the app) and is otherwise listed in the round's report with its exact words, its place and its section, for the owner to approve or cut. **Where the doc conflicted with a later ruling, the ruling won and the doc was amended** (each amendment dated in place). 4.9's rules, no example inside a field and one ask at a time, override any older line. **Rejected:** following the audit item by item (it would have rebuilt the explanatory text the owner had removed on purpose); building the additions and asking forgiveness (the owner's brief says list them).

**Banned words** (4.6, 4.9). "Only the person who’s got this one can close it." replaces "who is owed this", in the product's own grammar (2.1: "You've got Gabe $25"). The Needs you verb for the asker's close is "Close", and the two errors say close ("Only the person who asked it can close it.", "Closing it didn’t go through. Nothing changed."), since "Lock it in" is a sportsbook phrase (4.6). "The dares, the rounds, and the “I got this one”" replaces "The bets" on the signed-out screen and the plain card. "You’re signed in, so get in as yourself." replaces "put your number on it". The ruling is credited to the agreement, never to the app: the terms chip reads "A tiebreaker hears both sides and calls it" (nobody has agreed yet at asking), the details row and the card's line say the tiebreaker, and the ruling notice says "Decided by the tiebreaker everyone agreed to." with 4.10's void sentence, dropping the asker's name from it (the notice was crediting a person with asking the app). **Rejected:** "Only the person it's for" (ambiguous), "the tiebreaker everyone agreed to" on the chip (at asking, nobody has).

**Raw errors never reach a person** (5.4). `ConfirmError` and `CloseError` no longer carry the relayer's message: the failure is logged with its ids and the person reads "That didn’t go through. Try again."; the four `MarketError` sentences that appended the raw error in brackets log it instead; `/api/session` answers sentences (a refused token, a mismatched account, an unfinished signup) and logs the reason; the login bootstrap shows only sentences it wrote itself (`Said`), and anything the SDK or the network throws is logged and said as one plain line. **Rejected:** keeping the raw text behind a "details" control (5.4: no error codes in front of people).

**Examples and captions** (4.9, 3.29). Every example placeholder is gone: the claim's field, the ask form's unit and scale fields, the custom stake, the name step (the cover form's went with the form). The one placeholder kept is the even share in an adjusted split, which is the value in effect when the field is left blank, not an example. The captions under the kind chips and the sticker caption in the picker are gone. **The weight-line caption** speaks only when one stake is more than half of everything riding, on all three pictures (the weight line, the number axis with a new `numberCaption`, the pick-one bars, which already did), and the number axis keeps its one sentence about a number past the end, since the picture hides it; 3.22's "Everyone on one number." caption was amended away. The figure's `aria-label` falls back to its heading. **Rejected:** keeping "You’re first in." (it restates a lone column).

**"Waiting on"** stays in the two places the doc keeps it, the nudge card (3.42) and the game page's locked line (3.33), and goes everywhere else: the claim landing ("Nothing needs a yes from you."), the ghost's dismissal ("Anything still open for Alex closes"), the two refusals ("There’s nothing to call on this one right now.", "There’s nothing here for the feed to settle."), the feed's waiting sheet (its label is "Closed", and its explaining caption is gone: 3.35 says that for two hours the sheet's line is all). The nudge notice "Maya is waiting on you" stays: it is a person acting.

**Now** (3.15, 4.5, 4.7, 6.4). One citron dot per viewport, on the soonest needs-you row with a clock, and none on the tab bar anywhere (the owner's ruling; 6.4 and 3.38 amended; `liveFor` and `timeBound` gone with it, which also drops a query from every root). The two sections beyond the three are gone: "One thing was waiting for you" pointed at proposals already in Needs you as yep rows; "Is this you?" is a Needs you row of kind `claim` ("Is that you?" over "Gabe has things with a Sam", verb "That’s me", the one tap `ThatsMe`), since 4.7 places a claim to accept there. The Running row's meta line is your entry and how many are in ("You’re in at 70% · six of you", "just you so far" while alone; a number or a pick in its own words), and once locked the clock alone ("Resolving tonight", "Voting ends tonight"); no open clock, no count of callers, no state in words. **Rejected:** a count badge or a dot elsewhere for the claim row (the shell rule); "1 of 6 in" on the Running row when people were named (the ruling of 2026-09-27 was for the who's-in row, and 4.7's text for Now says "just you so far"; reported for the owner).

**The pages** (5.4, pre-approved). `not-found.tsx` is the code screen with its one form-level line (`DeadLink`), for every `notFound()` and every address the app has no screen for; `error.tsx` is the error card (`ErrorCard`: "Couldn’t load this one." with a 44px "Try again" that re-renders the segment) under the back control, the failure logged by its digest. **Rejected:** a `global-error.tsx` (it replaces the root layout and its fonts; the root `error.tsx` covers every screen inside the layout).

**Focus outlines** (5.1, pre-approved). The global 2px ink outline, 2px outside the control, is back on the question, the answers, the margin and the number entry (their `outline-none` gone), on Start (its flush ring gone), and the name field's focus border change is gone (focus must never look like an error).

**Two orphaned screens are gone.** The group invite page (`/join/[token]`, its card, `JoinGroup`, the paste reader's group branch): nothing linked to it, and it treated a group as a place with a name and a "You're invited to" (4.7: a group is a namespace). The invite rows and their functions stay for the data, with their database tests. The full-page cover form (`/new`, `NewCoverForm`): the cover is logged from the person it is with (3.43), and nothing linked to the form. Both addresses now answer with the code screen. **Rejected:** aligning the invite page with groups as sets (there is no link to reach it by).

**The market screen** (3.23, 3.25, 3.35, 3.37, 3.38). Once locked, the entry line goes and the claim card sits under the band; "Everyone’s in, and numbers are locked" and its list of each person's number are gone, and "Where everyone landed" heads the picture (the stage for someone in, the line, ruler or rows for anyone else in the group). The claim's clip opens in the album viewer (`ClipView`), never a new tab. The asker line reads "asked the Friday crew", "asked · Papa's birthday" for a name that can't take "the", an unnamed set by its people on a live market too ("asked Gabe, John and you", "and 3 others"), "asked you" for a dyad (`askerLine`). A final score's ending is the one caption line (3.35) and never a ruling card; the card stands for the tiebreaker alone. "locked at 10:40pm" inside the caption takes no capital. The tile's close time has one comma ("Closes Fri Sep 25, 10:40pm"). **The code icon** stays open-only: a room code is one live code per question and dead at lock (the joining rules), so 3.42 and 3.37 were amended rather than the build. **Rejected:** "Resolving tonight" replaced by the Decided term's words in the band (3.38 says so, but 3.15 and 3.23 name "Resolving tonight" as the clock; reported as a doc inconsistency).

**The argument ruled split is cut from the doc.** The tiebreaker rules yes, no, or that the terms don't decide it (`Arbitration.outcome`); a ruling's confidence is a lean the screen shows, never a split outcome; and the contract scores a yes-or-no question against 0 or 1. So no ruling can produce a split, and the screen, 3.2's fraction state, 1.2's uppercase exception, 3.4's split card and 3.40's line go with it, dated in place.

**What's on** (3.33, 3.35, 3.40). The settled lines say the team alone ("Bills won.") while the wells keep "The", so the chalk reads "That’s right, the Bills won" as 3.35 has it; the voting line on a game's card is the clock alone; a void's reason is three words or fewer ("unclear terms" for the tiebreaker's finding). The first drive's menu name stays "The first drive": the doc's "The Bills' first drive" is a possessive of a team name, which a real session read as "the Giants's" (the templates' own note).

**The ask flow** (3.20, 3.29, 3.44). Who's in is a `radiogroup` of `radio` rows, and the kind chips are one too; the "Whoever I send it to" row is the joining decision of 2026-09-27, so 3.20 now carries it. The picker opens on Food and its None cell reads "None". The named subject's step is the card alone, "Nova is" over "a person", "a pet", "something else" (its heading and explaining line gone), and the careful step's chalk is "Next: who’s in".

**"Who's joining?" falls back** (3.45, pre-approved) to the host's own people who have pass the phone on, ready ones only, when nobody was named or everyone named is already in; `handOverCandidates` takes the host. **Rejected:** listing the host's people dimmed and unready (it would name nobody's absence).

**Cutting a sticker inside the app** (3.28, frame 5, pre-approved; `src/lib/ui/cut-subject.ts`, `CutSheet`). MediaPipe's interactive segmenter (`@mediapipe/tasks-vision` 1.0.1, its legacy interactive class, which takes a point) runs in the browser: the photo's pixels come through the door, drawn at the paste path's 1024px bound; the confidence mask for the point tapped is thresholded at 0.5, feathered a pixel by a box blur, applied as alpha, cropped to the subject and sent down the same paste path (`prepareCutout`, the sticker action). The subject wears a dashed cream outline drawn over the photo (the edge band two pixels outside the shape, cut into dashes by a diagonal stripe pattern), the chalk "Keep it" waits for a cut and carries the wait while the model loads (5.2), and "Start over" clears the tap. The runtime (11MB of wasm) comes from the CDN under immutable headers and the model (6MB) is kept in the Cache API by hand, since its host allows an hour and the service worker caches nothing; both load only when the sheet opens, once per page. Where either cannot load, the sheet becomes the lift path (frames 2 and 3). One caption reads the model's timings, an instrument for the phone check (docs/testing.md item 94) that leaves once answered. **Rejected:** the package's new split-mode `InteractiveSegmenter` (it needs a different model than the published one); a delegate of "GPU" (uneven on phones; the CPU path is the safe default until the phone's timings say otherwise); copying the model into the repository (6MB of binary in a public repository, for a file its host serves with CORS); a spinner while the model loads (5.3).

**Sorted and not built, for the owner** (the additions list in the round's report): the two stake-step sentences 4.9 keeps; the ballot count line before anyone has said; "What was it?" over the number vote; the app's read on a claim card of its own; the band's clocks after the end; the "Everyone's number" label, the roll call and the rest of 3.4's story-card lines; the person view's "Coming up" and its lines; the claimant screen as drawn; the naming prompt and the first-ever picker; the "How people answer" label; the offline bar and the upcoming card; People's Standings and plans; the closed mark on the memory view's consequences (it needs the indexer's state per edge on that screen); "Try again" inside a form's summary block and the 10-second step; the backstop email's shape. Components at the wrong size or shape stay for the feel round; after-submission items stay there. Two rules with no check stay open and are named in the report: re-validation on blur with focus to the first field with a problem, and the type budget as a DOM check.

The second sentence, about settling only with people who land closer, goes to the market's information sheet next round by the owner's ruling. On an unquantifiable unit the sentence joins the one line the stake section already had. **Rejected:** the ask flow's terms step (the asker fixes the unit there, not what they put on it).

(`nobodyYetLine`, the threshold in words) is the count line's empty state, as the sheet's header, when the proposal is the score's or the play-by-play's; the app's read stays the header for a dare's proposal, by the owner's ruling that a prominent opinion from the app invites rubber-stamping. The proposal now names its source (`feed` or `app`). **Rejected:** the empty line under the app's read as well (two headers).

The doc's example, "Maya and Priya have got him too.", reads the rows grouped by the person owed; the build groups by the person who has got, so the caption keeps that grammar rather than switching mid-list. The list still ends with "Called it even: …" (3.37), since "Everyone else called it even." was declined. **Rejected:** regrouping the rows by the person owed to match the example word for word.

**The person view** (3.38; `splitTimeline`, `TIMELINE_FOLD`): "Coming up" in `label` over what is still ahead between the two (a market open or locked, a game not over), then the past newest first, folded past twelve behind a 44px tertiary "Show earlier", which is the same screen asked for earlier (`?earlier=1`, keeping the chosen set). Nothing labels the past: the dates say so, and the today rule was declined. **Rejected:** a client-side reveal (the screen is rendered per person per request; a link keeps it one server render).

**Frames on voided and expired stories** (3.4): the story card draws its frame at 180 for a void or an expiry with photos, as it does for a resolved market, since an ended market takes photos whatever its ending (3.37).

**The offline bar** (3.14; `OfflineBar`): a 28px `--surface-2` bar under the header, "Offline. You can still look around.", from the browser's own word for the connection (`navigator.onLine`, through `useSyncExternalStore`), under the top bar on every task screen and under each root's own header. Nothing here queues or retries; the doc's "entries queue and send on reconnect" is not built. **Rejected:** one fixed bar in the layout (it would sit over the status band, not under the header).

(`alreadyInLine`), then one `body` line, "Two weeks with the Friday crew, kept under your name, Maya." (`spanWords` for the span of what was waiting; the one named set it came from, else "your friends"); one group per person, most items first, with the name and the count; one card of claim rows, each a kicker ("Covered · Sep 5"), the memo or "Priya got this one", the token, and a 44px check pressed by default labelled "Confirm: Priya's got you"; the chalk counts the pressed rows ("Yep, these 5 are right"), and the batch it signs is the pressed rows alone (the message's arrays are index-aligned with the ids, so the kept ones are filtered together and the server rebuilds the same batch from the ids it is sent). ## 2026-09-28: Round D, how it feels: the merge, the layers, the motion, light, the shapes, the opening and the information sheets

**The merge.** The twelfth and thirteenth design sessions worked from the copy of `docs/design.md` at f14b2f8 (the eleventh session's files with the developer's note scheduling pass the phone, and none of Round A's amendments), so that copy was the base of a three-way merge (`git merge-file`) with the repository's copy (every dated amendment through Round C part 2) as one side and the sessions' combined file as the other, the thirteenth session's diff as a cross-check on its part. Git found no textual conflict: the sessions' edits sit in the top matter, 1.6, 3.12, 3.13, 3.17, 3.22, 3.29, 3.31, 3.39's viewer, 4.8, 4.9, 5.3, 6.3, 6.4, 7, 8.1 and the new sections 9, 10 and 11, and the amendments sit elsewhere in the same sections, so every seam merged clean. The seams read by hand and resolved: 3.29's header (the sessions' Close, a down chevron, and "Got a code?" beside the icon, over the amendment's "Got a code?" on Now: both stand, 10.3 says so); 3.38's photo viewer (the sessions' close at the top left with the counter centred: stands); 3.39 (the sessions kept photos private until the end, the owner's ruling opens the album the whole time: the ruling stands, the amendment in place, and 10.8's two open lines are resolved by it); 3.42 (the sessions have no nudge, the ruling has it back beside each person still out: the ruling stands); 6.4 (the sessions' corner rule beside the ruling's "nothing on the tab bar": both stand); 5.3 (the sessions' shells beside Round C part 2's ten-second step: reconciled in 5.2 as one set of rules); the top matter's "after submission" for light and "not yet scheduled" for pass the phone, 1.1's Light column, 1.8, 3.34, 3.45, 4.8's rows, 6.3's rows, section 7, section 8's heading and 8.8, 9.10 and 10.1's mentions of One tap: dated notes at each, since light ships now, pass the phone shipped in Round B, and One tap's ask is dropped. The export was replaced with the sessions' and the dropped copies deleted. **Rejected:** merging by hand from the diff (the base is in the history, so the three-way merge is exact and the seams are the whole review).

**The measurements**, in the development browser signed in as the localhost account, Turbopack dev mode, from the tap to the destination's marker in the DOM (the pane is hidden, so paint could not be timed; every number is the DOM's arrival, and dev mode is slower than a build). Before: switching tabs 280 to 608ms; + to ask 358 to 466ms; opening a market from Now 495ms warm and 1163ms on its first compile, back 590 to 617ms; the steps of asking 9ms between steps and 3.0 to 3.8 seconds waiting at "Set the terms" for the write-up. After: a tab visited within thirty seconds 8 to 13ms and a first visit 191 to 433ms; + to ask 25ms once the question step is held (the first tap of a load waits for the step's payload when the prefetch has not landed: 1145ms before the Start button became the one link a root prefetches, 678ms after); a market's shell 8 to 16ms after the tap with the screen landing at 550ms the first time and 29ms from the router's hold, back 35 to 50ms; the steps 5 to 11ms each way, the terms step opening in 10ms with the first words at 2.3 seconds and the whole write-up at 3.2. The stray colours found: the code's two literals, the button runner's track, the who's-in step's selection ring, the team stamp's ring, the outline on a photo, the team abbreviation's two inks, the icon route, the manifest and the layout's theme colour, beside the tiles and the share card that stay one image for everyone.

**The layers** (9.3). A fixed layer renders in place on the server, so the first frame and the markup the tests read have it, and portals itself into the host after the app root once hydrated (`FixedLayer`, `useSyncExternalStore` with a null server snapshot). The grain and the band behind the status bar are layers of the shell, painted from `--ground` on `html`; a market's own screen sets its layers on `html` through one style element in both themes (`InkRoot`), so the sheet, the band and the grain read the market's place, and the page paints no ground of its own. The pinned sheet is laid out at its raised height and sits low by a translate on itself, its hidden part below the low part, with a strip at the viewport's foot in the sheet's surface so lowering never shows what it hides; a modal sheet's backdrop takes the touches and dims nothing. The test walks the markup of every root and task screen for the tab bar, the Start button and the sheet with the rules in `src/lib/ui/layers.ts`; the same rules read the live tree's computed styles in the browser check. **Rejected:** client-only portals (no tab bar in the first frame or the markup); a route group's layout for the bar (a page's empty and signed-out states could not tell it to hide); `contain: size` in the forbidden list (it captures nothing).

**The ask layer** (9.5) is the intercepted route `src/app/@ask/(.)m/new`: reached from inside the app, `/m/new` renders in a fixed layer over the place it was tapped from, which stays mounted, scroll and all, and is made inert; the sheet inside it sits in flow (`InFlowSheets`); Close sinks the layer and goes back; a hard load of `/m/new` is the page as before with Close leading to the root. **Rejected:** a client-side flag opening the form as a modal with no address (a reload or a link would lose it).

**Tabs** (9.6) act on `pointerdown`, the bar marks the destination from the touch, the router holds a root for thirty seconds (`staleTimes`, with every mutation revalidating what it changes), the scroll is put back on arrival, Now prefetches the other three while idle, and the current tab tapped again scrolls to the top. **Rejected:** keeping four rendered roots mounted at once (every root on every load).

**Shells and the market opening** (9.4, 9.7). Every row, card and question card that opens a market carries what it knows on its link (`data-shell`: the ink, the mark, the state, the band's clock from the shared `bandClock`, the question, the asker line where the row can know it, the resting sheet's first line); one document listener draws the shell in the frame after the tap and names the row's stamp for the view transition; the band is one component for the shell and the screen (`QuestionBand`), so the geometry matches; the screen fades in over the shell when it lands, and the slow-load stages are the button's own. Back from a market holds the old snapshot only while the router still holds the root (thirty seconds), then shrinks the ink into the row wherever it now is. Every other push is the arrival half (the header fading, the content 24px from its side), since the router commits when the screen has arrived. **Rejected:** React's view-transition component and the framework's flag (absent from this version); a transition that awaits the fetch (the freeze this section is about); naming every row's stamp at render (a list would hold two elements with one name).

**Streaming the terms** (9.8). The write-up moved behind one function (`writeUp`) the action and a route share; the model call streams the tool's JSON as it is written (`structured` with `onDelta`), the route sends each piece as a line and the same result at the end, and the terms step reads the title and the terms out of the half-written JSON (`partialValues`) with the caret, "Still writing." three seconds after the last word and the block with "Try again" at ten, the values editable once written. Where the stream cannot be read the action answers whole and the caret waits. The careful step's three questions come from one answer and fade in 40ms apart. **Rejected:** streaming through the server action (an action returns once); streaming the careful questions (one answer, three questions).

**Light** (section 8). The stylesheet carries the Light column twice, by choice and by the phone, and a test holds the two blocks equal; the citron keeps its colour and gains its edge; strokes and washes in a person's hue read the stroke variants; the avatar's edge; the light grain; the inks' light layers; the Appearance row on You with its three-row sheet, kept in this browser's storage and put onto `html` before first paint. The code to scan is drawn in the current ink on the surface (the generator's black becomes `currentColor`). The two colours that never follow the theme live in `src/lib/ui/palette.ts`. **Rejected:** the darker yellow for citron (8.5); inline ink variables on a market's wrapper (they would override the light layers).

**The shapes** (the audit's list), each closed: the ghost ring 2px outside; the Mixed token in one pill with `splitMixed` past three parts, dollars last; whole dollars in every token with the cents in the accessible name; the choice chips' selected style; the stake chips at 44px, radius 10, 17px 600, chalk when selected, the smallest by default, "Something else" swapping the row; the call line's cap, cluster, words and track; the leaderboard's track and one annotated line (a wrong-sided number that still ranks above others, `annotation`); the person header's padding, the cancel-out row with its 44px action and the sheet's words, "Gabe settled up", the photo row's action; the rally avatar at 24; the dashed chip naming an unnamed set by its people on a story card; the unit mark at 28 with a 16px glyph; the coming-up glyph on "Coming up"; the grain as a layer; no scrim. **Rejected:** rounding whole dollars down (a list would understate).

**The two corrections.** The count line's empty state sits under the app's read on every ballot and is never a second header; a final score speaks on its source card (3.35), so the count line heads that ballot alone. Covers recorded against a person start unpressed on the claimant screen, their own link entries pressed. **Rejected:** the feed's line as a header over the empty state (it would say the score twice).

**The opening** (section 11). One script renders the launch images for thirteen iPhone sizes in both schemes, the icons and the favicon (a PNG in an ICO container, written by hand) from `assets/logo/on-dark.svg` and `on-light.svg`, the placeholder until the real logo arrives; the layout inlines the first frame in the phone's own scheme and lists the dark set first; the handoff marks `html` dressed, so the tokens and the override own its ground from that frame, and fades the logo over quick and the ground over base. **Rejected:** the runtime icon route (one source, one command); a manifest colour per theme (a manifest cannot follow the phone).

**The information icon and its sheets** (section 10). The icon on every screen 10.1 lists, the roots' 56px header row, "Got a code?" and More beside it, the photo viewer's close moved left with the counter centred; the sheet as 10.4 and 10.5; every sheet written from the code in one registry, keyed per screen and per market state and kind, held to 10.6 by `sheetProblems` and the copy scan in the unit suite; the market's sheet carries the settling rule the stake step no longer states. `INFO_ICON_ON` removes it all. **Rejected:** writing each sheet in its screen's JSX (the lint could not read them together).

**The style lint** now refuses a duration or a curve typed outside the set and a colour typed outside the tokens, with the allow list of the files that must write one, and a fixture for each rule.

## 2026-09-29: The logo round: the cold start, the logo's files, the count, and the sheets' words

**Where the cold start went.** Measured before anything changed. On production (Round D deployed), signed out, the first request after the server had slept took 2.87 seconds to its first byte and the next two took 0.14 and 0.22: the server that answers for Now goes to sleep between visits and takes about 2.7 seconds to wake. Signed in, in the development browser's real session on production with the server awake, nothing of the page arrived for 1.21 to 1.97 seconds (the headers came at 0.08 to 0.10, the page itself only once the server had read everything), the stylesheet took 0.06 after that, and the first paint was at 2.04 to 2.52 seconds, behind 1.5 MB of scripts that arrive and run in the same stretch. On a local build of the same commit the server's first request took 2.69 seconds to the page and warm ones 0.66 to 1.89.
