The server's own share, step by step in a fresh process (`scripts/dev/cold-start.ts`, read-only): a first database connection 274ms, the account's row 56ms, the pass-the-phone read 314ms (two queries side by side, so a second connection is opened for it), Now's data 577 to 979ms, the indexer 5 to 39ms of that; every new connection costs about 270ms from here, and the client drops them after twenty seconds idle. So of the five seconds on the phone: waking the server is the largest part, reading the account and Now before sending anything is the second, and the scripts, the sign-in's own code among them, are the third; none of them is the opening. The root layout waited for the account and for pass the phone before it sent a byte, and Now waited for its data, so the first frame, the shell and the handoff all stood behind every query.

**The shell first.** Now answers in pieces. The root layout starts the session's read and never waits for it (`sessionFacts`, a promise carried to the browser; whatever needs the answer waits where it is used, which on every other screen is the screen itself, as before). Now decides its shell from the cookie alone (`sessionUserId`: the token's own signature, checked in the process, no query), sends the header row, the tab bar, the + and the handoff, and its content (`NowContent`) reads the account and Now and arrives into it, fading in over base where a shell stood waiting and appearing at once where the router already held it. An empty Now has no + and no "Got a code?", so the phone remembers which it was in a cookie beside the zone's (`dareful_now`), the stylesheet hides both on the content's word and until then on the phone's, and the content corrects the hint the moment it arrives. After: the shell's piece arrives at 18 to 26ms on the local build where the whole page took 660 to 2690ms, the content at 620 to 1016ms; painted, in Chrome with a test account, the shell at 60 to 83ms and the content at 869 to 899ms. **Rejected:** a route-level loading state (every screen would answer in pieces, and a missing market would answer 200); the framework's partial prerendering (one switch for every route, each of which would then have to answer in pieces); a static shell with the content fetched by the browser (the content would wait on 1.5 MB of scripts before it was even asked for); a shell kept by the service worker (the worker caches nothing but an address, by rule); leaving the + out of the shell (11.5 draws it there); a query for whether Now is empty (the shell would wait on it).

**Keeping Now's door warm.** The shell cannot paint before the server sends it, and the server sleeps. The scheduler already knocks once a minute, so it also asks for Now's own address once, as nobody: no cookie, no secret, the signed-out screen in reply (`keepWarm`). It is beside the jobs, never ahead of them, and a miss costs one slow start. Not verifiable until it is deployed; the check is in docs/testing.md. **Rejected:** leaving it (2.7 seconds of launch image on most opens, at this traffic); a second scheduler for it (one door); paying for an instance that never sleeps.

**What was left alone, on purpose.** The database client's twenty-second idle timeout, which makes most first reads pay for new connections: raising it is a change to how the app holds the pooler, and it is the owner's to rule on. Now's own queries, which run the account and then Now in turn.

**The logo's files.** `assets/logo` holds the design's files as delivered (`svg`, `icon`, `favicon`); `node scripts/opening.mjs` places them: the launch images written as the bare ground (pixel for pixel the design's own twenty-two, and four more for the two older sizes), the icons and the favicon copied and never redrawn, the badge rendered from the mark alone, and the outlines of the wordmark, the mark and the lockup written as data for the places the app draws them (`src/lib/ui/logo.ts`). The head and the manifest name them as LOGO.md does. The favicon is the design's own `.ico`, which holds the versions drawn to the pixel grid at 32 and 16. **Rejected:** rendering the icons from the master SVG here (the grain is a filter, and two renderers draw two grains); scaling the mark down for the favicon (LOGO.md draws it to the grid instead); keeping the zip in the repository beside the files taken out of it (13 MB twice; it is left unstaged for the owner to rule on).

**Where LOGO.md and the design doc disagreed, LOGO.md won.** The wordmark in a header is `--ink`, the chalk on dark and the ink on light, where 3.38 said `--ink-2`; on the tiles it is the chalk, where 3.38 said the market's ink-hi, which LOGO.md forbids. The share card's dot and the word "Dareful" are the lockup. Where a header has back, the wordmark is not drawn beside it. **Kept against LOGO.md's snippet:** one theme colour per scheme rather than the one dark value, since a single dark value would put a dark bar over the light theme of section 8; the dark value is LOGO.md's.

**The count.** The design's `opening.css`, `opening.html` and `handoff.js` are in `src/lib/ui/opening.ts` character for character (`OPENING_CSS`, `OPENING_MARK`, `HANDOFF_JS`), and the four values sit in one block at the top of the inline style, the one place in the repository they are written. The app's own additions are outside them: the ground on `html` held only until the document is dressed (`dressedOnly`, Round D's fix), the one call that runs the handoff, and the instrument's switch. **Rejected:** the design's files in the repository with a generated module beside them (the four values in three places); the fades as motion tokens (the first frame is drawn before the stylesheet; a test holds them equal to the set instead).

**One handoff, and a fault of Round D's found under it.** The handoff removes `#opening` from the page. When the app starts after that, which is any phone whose scripts take longer than a third of a second, it finds one of its own elements gone, gives up matching the page and rebuilds it, with the opening in it and no handoff left to run. Reproduced on the deployed build in Chrome with the scripts slowed: the opening came back and stayed, and the document was undressed. `#opening` now sits in a box the app keeps (`data-opening-host`), whose inside the app never looks at, and what the scripts set on `html` is left to them. The http suite watches both orders in a real browser, signed out and signed in. **Rejected:** hiding the element rather than removing it (that rewrites the design's handoff); marking the element alone as not to be checked (the app still takes the next element for it).

**The count is rarely seen, and that is the design's own rule.** The shell is in the first piece of the page with the first frame, so the first painted frame already has Now's shell under the opening, the handoff begins two frames later, and no stroke has started (the first starts at 200ms). With the count and without it the shell painted at the same moment: 43 to 82ms against 22 to 85ms signed out, 61 to 83ms against 60 to 82ms signed in, and at a quarter of the speed 72 to 205ms against 73 to 188ms. Held back by hand 800ms, the third stroke stopped three quarters drawn, as LOGO.md's own example has it. The count would be seen on every cold start only if the first frame reached the phone ahead of the server, from somewhere that never sleeps, which is a static first page and a different build; it was not made without asking.

**The sheets.** "Put your number on it" is out of the three sheets that had it and is refused by the build wherever it is typed; "Your vote is signed" is refused the same way. The design's own words are refused by the sheet lint (`DESIGN_WORDS`), the nine the owner listed and five found beside them (the count line, citron, the context chips, stone, the frame). 10.6 rules out "button", so the chalk is named by its label rather than as "the button at the bottom". **Rejected:** lifting 10.6's ban for the sheets (the owner's to rule on).

**The instrument.** "Measure the screen" also reads the cold start: the first byte, the shell's paint, the content's arrival, the scripts, the sign-in's code and the server's own two figures, from the moment the page began to load, and "Open without the count" turns the count off on that phone so the two can be timed against each other. It leaves with the probe. **Rejected:** a build without the count (two deploys to compare one number).

## 2026-10-02: The field round, part 0: counting what real groups do

Real friend groups are on dareful.app, the traction criterion needs numbers from the first week, and the submission closes on October 13. Anything already in a table can be counted later; what leaves no row (a link opened, a share, a code shown, a sticker pasted, an error shown) is lost for every day it goes unlogged. So the counting ships first, on its own, and the owner's findings follow in their own commit.

**One append-only table, apart from the ledger's own log.** `usage_events` (migration 0034, applied 2026-10-02; additive, so the build already deployed runs beside it): when, what, who (an account, a guest's claim, or neither, with the device that reported it), which market or game, and a few properties. `notification_log`, `chain_writes` and the rest stay what they are: records the product runs on. A count is never something the product runs on, so it lives apart, and a row of it is never read to decide anything. Alternative rejected: counting from the existing tables alone. They answer asked, entered, voted and settled; they cannot answer opened, shared, shown or failed, and those are the funnel's first steps.

**Written only on the server, with one door for the browser.** The ledger's modules call `record` where the thing happens (`src/lib/usage/index.ts`: asking in `openMarket`, entering in `enterMarket` and `enterAsGhost`, closing in `lockMarket`, voting in `castVote`, settling in `mirrorSettlement`, `settleProvisional`, `completeExpire` and `removeMarkets`, closing an obligation and cancelling out in `closes.ts`, a nudge and every notice in `notify/index.ts`, a guest bound in `bindClaimToUser` with the door it came through, a code shown and used in the join actions, a sticker with where its cutout came from, a photo with the market's state, a sign-up or a sign-in in `/api/session`). The browser reports four things and no other (`src/lib/usage/events.ts`, `CLIENT_EVENTS`): a link opened, a share by its icon, a notification's tap by its channel, and an error shown by its cause and the screen's shape. `/api/usage` takes a name from that list, checks the properties against the name's own schema, reads who from the cookies (the session, else the guest's claim, and the device's own id either way), and answers 204 whatever happened: a page never sees a count fail. `record` itself never throws into the path that called it. Alternative rejected: a client library that posts any named event. The counts would then be anyone's to write.

**Nothing typed, ever.** Every property is a word from a fixed set, a boolean or a count under a thousand, checked by Zod in `record` as well as at the door, so a caller cannot put a question's words, a name, a number or an amount into the table by mistake. An error's screen is its shape (`/m/[id]`), never its address. Alternative rejected: a free-text `detail` column for later. It would fill with exactly what the table must never hold.

**A link counts as opened once, and never for a fetcher.** The page reports it from a client component (`LinkOpened`, on the market screen signed in and not, the game's link page, the claim link and the code screen), so a link-preview fetcher, which runs no script, never reaches the door; the door refuses the crawlers' user agents as a second belt (Messages, Slack, WhatsApp, the search engines, curl). The server composes a once-key (the link, its target and who: an account before a claim before a device) and the insert does nothing on a repeat, under a partial unique index. A device is a random id in its own cookie (`dareful_device`), set by the door's first answer, nothing else in it; the browser's reports go one after another so the first can set it before the second arrives. Alternative rejected: counting on the server's render. Every preview fetch would count, and a reload would count again.

**Who is counted.** `users.excluded_from_counts` (migration 0034) leaves an account out of every count; it is set by hand, never by the app, and a market counts only when someone counted is in it. No row has been changed: the list of accounts that look like the owner's or a test's is proposed at this stop, and the owner keeps one of his own counted.

**A notification's tap is counted by channel.** Each channel's link carries `via=push` or `via=email` and the notice's id (`viaChannel`), before any fragment, and the page reports the tap and takes both off the address at once, so a reload, a share or a copied link never carries them.

**Left for the owner:** the exclusion list; the leftover `tmp-check:` accounts from interrupted test runs (the sweep removes them; nothing was run); and whether a guest's device should be joined to the account it later becomes in the counts (today the account's later opens are counted under the account, the earlier ones under the device, and a guest bound to an account is its own event).



## 2026-10-06: First contact, second pass: the name you typed, Google first, only the people in, the share in view, questions too far out, and Opus for the tiebreaker

The owner's second brief after first contact, one commit. What a finger touches was confirmed on the iOS 26 and iOS 27 simulators (docs/testing.md session 39). The account paths were not run, for the reason below.

**Join as the name you typed, with the votes line above it.** The owner's screenshot had "Justin incognito" in the field, a full-width "Sign in" under it that read as the step after typing a name, the line "If nobody votes, the final score settles it." between the actions, and "Join as Justin" at the bottom. The step is now the heading, the consent line, "Your name", "Join as Justin incognito" directly under the field as the one main action, and "I already have an account" under that as a quiet text button. The button says exactly what the server saves (`guestNameOf`: trimmed, at most 40 characters, the field's own limit, `GUEST_NAME_MAX`), and a long name wraps onto a second line rather than being cut. Rejected: the first word alone, which is how "Justin incognito" joined as "Justin"; and an ellipsis, which would not name what is saved.

**A phone that remembers a guest offers the account too.** "I already have an account" is on the step for a remembered guest ("Joining as Noah · Not you?"), and the account step follows that guest's entry until it has come once on this phone (`offersKeep`; whether it has come is kept in the browser, a convenience of the phone's and not the account's). Rejected: never offering it to a remembered guest, last round's rule, which left a returning guest no way to an account but the link page's sign-in; and offering it after every entry.

**Google first, with Google's own mark.** The keeping step and the sign-in step lead with "Continue with Google" as their chalk, Google's G at its left; then the email, whose Continue is a secondary while Google is offered; then "Use a phone number"; then "Not now". Now's one card reads "Link Google or add an email" with Google first, a secondary there because Now's chalk is the Start button. The mark is the file Google's own branding page generates, written from the page's path data and checked against it byte for byte by hash (`public/brand/google-g.svg`, `GoogleMark`, 20px, unmodified). It is the one place a colour outside the tokens appears, and the one place red and green do: Google's guidelines require the four-colour G, and it is Google's mark, not a colour of ours, the same kind of exception as the remove square 1.1 admits. Rejected: a G redrawn in ink, which those guidelines do not allow; and Google as a secondary under the email, last round's order.

**Only the people in decide, and nobody asked counts anywhere.** Last round's rule (`locksHere`) sent a question to be decided here whenever fewer of its set were in than a majority of the set, and otherwise kept the chain's bar, a majority of everyone registered in the set; it was deployed on October 5 at 23:05 UTC and no question in production has locked since, so it touched none (the four production questions whose chain bar differs from a majority of the people in locked before it, and none of them was decided by a vote). It is gone. The quorum is the people in, a majority of them decides, and the size of the set plays no part anywhere. Where a question is kept follows from that: the deployed contract asks a majority of every governance wallet ever registered in the set, the asker's included, so the chain keeps a question only when that is exactly the people in (`chainCarries`), and any other question is decided here by the same majority and settles as proposals each debtor confirms, as a guest's question already did. Registration narrows with it: a set registers only the people acting, the two sides of a confirmation, or the people in and the asker at a lock the chain keeps (`ensureGroupOnchain(groupId, userIds)`), never the whole set, so someone who only opened the link never becomes a voter on the chain. A registration the deployed build left in flight is finished as it was sent and registers nobody new (`mirrorRegistration`). The lock's completion compares the chain's snapshot with the people in and logs loudly when a registration that landed between the check and the lock widened it (`snapshotIsThePeopleIn`), since no later step can take a voter out. Rejected: keeping the chain's majority of the set, which is the rule the owner ruled out; a redeploy that takes the quorum from the people in, which changes the contract's interface and every signature and waits for the redeploy list; and a new set on the chain for each question's people, which would split a group's history across sets nobody named. The cost, on the record: a question in a set where someone not in it was registered before now settles as proposals to confirm, not as minted edges.

**"Whoever I send it to" is first** in who's in, above every set, wherever the list appears (asking and starting a game share one component), and chosen to start with. Rejected: below the sets, where last round left it when it made it the default.

**"Share it first" is on screen without scrolling for an asker who has not entered.** On Safari's 714-point screen three places hid it, and each was moved: a draft's moves sat under its terms, so they come first, under the question; a new game's share row sat under the list of its questions, so it sits under the game's header; and an asker's own game question opened its entry sheet raised over the share row, so the asker's sheet rests until they are in (`opensRaised` with `asker`). Measured on both simulators: a draft's "Share it first" at 438 to 482 points above the sheet's top at 484, a game question's share row at 340 to 384, the game page's at 398. On a device that has not checked it's you, the notice at the top (154 points) pushes a game question's share row under the resting sheet; an asker's own phone holds the login and never shows it, and the notice is left as designed. The game's cards also read "0 of 1 in" for an asker who had not entered: the count leaves the asker out until they are in (`countedSetSize`) and says "nobody's in yet" for none (`inCount`, after 3.14's rule against a zero), on the game's cards and everywhere else that count is drawn.

**A question that cannot be decided in range says so, and offers one nearer.** The write-up's date is never moved. A date past the furthest a question can run (three calendar years from today in the asker's zone, `LATEST_YEARS`, `latestDate`) leaves the decide-by chips unpicked, with "That can’t be known until October 6, 2056, and the furthest a question can run is October 6, 2029." at the field and, under it, one nearer version measurable in time, with its own title, terms and date, from the same call ("Ask this instead", `nearer`); the asker may also pick a date. The server refuses a close past that ("That’s more than three years out. Pick an earlier date.", `furthestClose`) and the date picker stops there. Rejected: last round's clamp, which moved a far date to the edge without a word; refusing with no nearer version; and a second model call for it, slower and costlier than one more field in the first. Calendar years rather than 1,095 days, after the first recording proposed a nearer date one day past a day count. Found on the simulator while checking it: terms that name a year ("by October 6, 2029") were compared without it, so the mismatch line could read "The terms say October 6, and it’s decided October 6"; the deadline reader keeps the year, and the line names it when the years differ (`datePhrase`).

**Found on the iOS 26 simulator while walking the too-far flow, and fixed.** The ask flow's three fields (the question, the question echoed on the terms step, and the terms) grow by `field-sizing: content`, which Safari 26.0 does not support and Safari 27 does (read on both simulators): on iOS 26 a three-line question showed two lines (82 points of a 112-point field) and terms past three lines scrolled out of sight inside their box. Where the browser cannot size a field, its height now follows its content as it mounts and as it changes (`useFitsContent`, `fitField`; the same question then stood at 114 points, whole); where it can, nothing runs. And when the write-up asks which of a few readings a question means, each reading was an ordinary button on one line at a fixed height, so a long one was cut off at both ends; each now wraps inside its button, as careful mode's choices already did. Rejected for the first: raising the rows, which wastes the screen on short questions and still cuts long ones.

**The tiebreaker is Opus 5.5.** The three tiebreaker rulings (yes or no, a number, pick one) call `claude-opus-5-5` (`MODELS.tiebreaker`, `AI_MODEL_TIEBREAKER`), with the effort said at medium, room for its thinking (16,000 tokens), and the API's server-side fallback (the beta `server-side-fallback-2026-07-01`, `fallbacks: "default"`), so a call Opus declines is answered rather than left failed. Everything else stays routed as it was. The instructions now say "cannot_decide" unless what is in front of it clearly supports one outcome under the terms as recorded (a lean, a guess, or one side's word the other disputes is not clear support), and when it does, rule it and say in the ruling which outcome. Every past ruling (six, all recorded as voids) ran again on Opus 5.5 and on Sonnet 5.5 with the new instructions, and both voided all six, agreeing with the record (under the old instructions last round, Sonnet named an answer on two). Opus cost $0.0096 to $0.0133 a ruling and took 3.5 to 5.6 seconds; Sonnet $0.0047 to $0.0055 and 2.5 to 3.7 seconds. The recorded rulings stand. The four arbitration fixtures were recorded again on Opus. Rejected: Opus for every call the ruling model makes, which the brief did not ask for; and the effort left to the default unsaid.

**The account paths were not run here.** The rules this work runs under do not let me sign in through an identity provider that is not running on this machine, with a test account or any other, so the thirty-one runs are the owner's (docs/testing.md, items 211 to 213, with what to count). The test account is out of every count: it signed up at 14:35 UTC this morning and opened three links (a code, a game and a question), and `excluded_from_counts` is set on it, as the brief asked. Its code was never printed.

**The audit.** Every one of the 1,480 mutants is killed: 1,477 in the full run, and three run alone after it, two http mutants whose test runs passed the runner's four-minute limit and one the run never reached, because the development server's Turbopack panicked after six hours ("Restore of All for task … failed", the internal panic of the round before). The runner skips its closing baseline when the server goes away; the three suites had run whole on the same code just before (437 unit, 212 database, 119 of 121 http, the other two the deployed-only test and the opening check, whose headless browser was slow to start under load and which passed alone), and the static check names every test but the deployed-only one. One test's own debris was found in production along the way and its test fixed, below.

**Production.** On the owner's go-ahead: the Red Sox question is resettled for the Yankees (ten proposals among its five, no notice sent; `scripts/ops/resettle-feed.ts`), and "Claude Code (IOS 26 PWA)" is out of every count. Last round's thirty guest runs left 62 error rows and one link row from six simulator devices, and the model comparison one escalation; the database suites left nothing, since their rows went with their temporary accounts and questions by the table's cascade. The stats page counts 63 of them, under "Errors shown" and "Link opens" (the escalation is on no number): a guest's or a device's row always counts, and there is no account to leave out. The cleanup is prepared and not run (`scripts/ops/drop-test-usage.ts`, 80 rows: those 64; this round's eight error rows from the simulators, whose two link rows went with the sweep; one sign-in with no account from each mutation audit since the door shipped, which the door's test posts to prove it refused and which the mutant that opens the door let through on no question, where the test's cleanup never looked, so the test now checks and removes every row its own device made; and the Red Sox question's first settled row, which the resettlement left counting the question twice). No count reads a sign-in with no account, so those seven change no number. Rejected: a flag that leaves a device out of the counts, a column for a one-off.

## 2026-10-04: First contact: joining from a link, asking, the sheets, who calls it, Google, and the models

The owner's brief after the first week with real groups, as one commit with the queue tests and the four pitch numbers before it (the entry below this one). Every finding was run to its cause; what a finger touches was confirmed on the iOS 26 and iOS 27 simulators (docs/testing.md session 38).

**A keyboard is a field a finger touched, not any field with focus.** The field round dropped the root to the plain height while `html:has(:focus)`; on the simulator the code screen's field, focused by the page itself with no keyboard up, dropped it and left a band under the sheet. The root is plain only while `data-typing` stands (`src/components/ui/typing.tsx`, `typingStarts`, `typingFromViewport`): a field focused by a touch or inside a tap's handling, or one over a visible part under 80% of the layout. Rejected: keeping `:focus`, which cannot tell a keyboard from a focus. The two sequences of 1.1 then ran ten times each in light and in dark on Now, People and You in the production copy (the tab bar at 775 to 874 after every run).

**The clean-resolution rate counts what a question's people decided, and only where two or more were in.** A guest's question decided by its quorum here (`provisional`) now counts like a vote, and a question one person was in counts in neither number, since its ending says nothing about how the terms were written (`CLEAN_COUNTED_ENDINGS`, `TWO_OR_MORE_IN`, shared by the profile and the stats page). Production before and after: since launch 63% (5 of 8) became 67% (6 of 9); the last seven days 50% (1 of 2) became 67% (2 of 3). Rejected: counting every ending, which let a question nobody joined grade its asker.

**Joining from a link is the name and nothing else, with Sign in right under it** (3.17 as amended). "Who's joining?" holds "Your name" and, directly below at the field's width, the 48px secondary "Sign in". A guest gives no number: the phone field, the suggestions by name and the number check leave the guest's path (`GhostWho` is a name). Rejected: the fifteenth session's two equal choices side by side, which made Sign in a fork before the entry rather than a door beside it; and keeping the number for guests, which made the first step a form.

**The entry is saved first, and the sheet's next step offers to keep it.** After "Join as Sam" the entry is stored as a guest's and the sheet's next step is "Keep your calls in an account": an email and Continue, then "Continue with Google", then "Use a phone number", then "Not now". The codes are Dynamic's own one-time codes, sent from the app's own fields (`AccountStep`, `useConnectWithOtp`); what follows a code (the session, a new account's wallets and its name) is the login's bootstrap, which takes the typed name instead of asking again and never sends someone who came from a link to the welcome screen. What crosses a sign-in (the name, the entry picked) travels in this tab alone, for half an hour (`src/lib/ui/join-handoff.ts`, `resumeFrom`): Sign in at the first step sends the entry picked under the account once it exists, and keeping a guest's entry signs the position the device's claim folded into the account. Rejected: asking for the account before the entry, which costs the entry whenever someone leaves at the code; and Dynamic's own sheet, which asks for an email with no word of what it is for.

**A remembered guest still joins as themselves.** A phone that remembers a guest says "Joining as Noah · Not you?" and enters as Noah, and the keeping step is offered after a first entry only. Rejected: asking every time, which is the question the fifteenth session wanted and the brief's flow does not have; listed for the owner.

**The odds line starts at 50%** (3.13 as amended): the thumb at the middle, "Slide to your prediction" where the band will read until the first touch, and the primary saying what it would send ("I’m in at 50%, $5"). A first entry opens the sheet raised so that primary is on screen without a touch (`opensRaised`); a number starts on its field at rest, and a draft rests so the terms its asker is approving stay in view. An argument still starts all the way to one side, and a pick starts empty. Rejected: a thumb with no value (nothing to send until touched, which the brief replaced).

**The one card for an account the app cannot reach.** An account with no push subscription on any device, no email and no Google sees one card at the top of Now, once (`ReachCard`, `users.reach_card_at`, migration 0038, additive): "Add an email" (Dynamic's code to that address, after the re-check Dynamic asks before a credential is added) or "Link Google", and "Not now". It is drawn only on the device that holds the login, the one that knows the credentials, and recorded as shown the moment it is on screen. Rejected: a banner on every screen, and a notice, which Principle 1 rules out.

**What the simulator found in the joining path, each fixed and held by a test.** The guest's page moved the entry stage from one place in the page to another when the entry arrived, so the refresh remounted it and the keeping step vanished (`ghost.tsx` draws it in one place while open). The iOS 27 simulator reports `navigator.onLine` false with the network up, so the offline bar stood and every tap was refused; the browser's word is now settled by a request to this origin before anything is refused (`src/lib/ui/connection.ts`: a HEAD for the favicon with no cache, three seconds; a word shown wrong is not believed again on that page until the browser says offline anew). Rejected for the second: dropping the check, which would send taps from a phone that really is offline.

**The guest line, ideas and first-visit tips are built** (3.46, 3.47, 3.32's tile, 10.9). The line is a fixed layer the page starts under (`--guest-line`), 64px for "Sign up and Dareful will remind you when it’s time to vote." and 44px for "You called it. Sign up so it counts." and "You were closest. Sign up so it counts.", once the guest is in. Ideas are thirteen questions in four groups (`src/lib/ideas.ts`), two with a blank that opens with the people the asker knows and "Or type a name"; the tile is first on What's on and sits on Now while someone has fewer than three questions running. Tips show up to three of a screen's sheet entries that point at a control on screen, once per screen, on the account (`users.tips_seen`, migration 0039, additive) or for a guest on the phone; the ring now moves with its cut-out at once, since a clip path cannot travel and a sliding ring was caught apart from it.

**Asking.** The labels are "Market type" ("Yes or no", "Pick a number", "Pick one") and, after more space, "AI market setup" ("Quick setup", "Help define the terms"), each keeping its line, for every type: Help define asks its three questions for a number (unit, source, rounding) and for pick one (the answers, a tie) as it did for yes or no. The type follows the question's shape until the asker picks one (`kindForQuestion`: which and who are pick one, how many and how much a number, whether yes or no), and nothing is ever written about "the one picked". An argument may be pick one, each person's answer an answer, and its ruling names one of them by its words or nobody can tell; an argument is never a number. Rejected: one set of setup choices for yes or no alone, which the brief asked to end.

**Why the country question's write-up failed.** The model was asked for hours until the question could be decided with no bound in its schema, and the parse capped the answer at a hundred and twenty days, so a question about something months away failed to parse and fell to the plain fallback ("The write-up didn’t come through"). The write-up now names a date (`decideBy`, a day in the asker's zone), moved into range, today to three years on, never refused (`clampProposal`). Rejected: raising the cap, which keeps hours as a unit nobody decides in.

**Decided by Tonight, This week, This month or a date, and the terms never disagree with it.** The chips start from the write-up's date (the date chip carries it, with the phone's own date picker under it), and a deadline the terms name must be the decide-by date, checked on the phone and again on the server ("The terms say October 13, and it’s decided November 2. Make them match.", `deadlineMismatch`); picking a chip changes the date in the terms with it (`swapDateWords`). Rejected: deriving the close from the terms' text, which a sentence about two dates would misread.

**Who's in starts at "Whoever I send it to"**, on the question step and in starting a game. Rejected: the last set first, which sent a question to the last people asked by default.

**Sharing before entering, drafts and withdrawing.** A draft carries "Share it first" (the Create signature alone, so the share, the copy and the code are there from creation and the asker enters any time before the close) and "Discard it" (gone for good: it was never sent, so nothing of it is history). They sit on the page under the terms, not in the sheet: on a 714-point Safari screen the raised sheet ended short of them. A draft never sent is a Needs you row for a day and then lives on You until it is sent or discarded. A question nobody else got into is its asker's to withdraw from its own screen, asked once, the same act as Remove on Now.

**The last row on a scrolling screen.** Now's tenth row sat under the tab bar and "Stakes, for all 3" under the sheet. The room was right; the box was not: the page (`Screen`'s `main`) was `flex-1` in the fixed scrolling column, so it was held at its minimum height whatever its content and its bottom padding fell outside it. It grows and never shrinks now (`grow shrink-0`). The room also follows the sheet's position live (`roomFor`: what shows at the position, and 20px). Rejected: measuring the room once at rest, which was the brief's guess and not the cause.

**The tucked sheet and the long drag.** Tucked shows the handle row and the strip over the home indicator, a tap on it raises the sheet, and a drag lands where the finger let it go (`nextPosition` with the positions' offsets): from raised, one long drag down tucks. Confirmed in Safari and the installed copy on iOS 26 and in Safari on iOS 27. Rejected: one position per drag, which read on the simulator as a sheet that would not tuck.

**Only the people in a question call it.** The quorum is the people in (within the chain's snapshot of the set), a vote from anyone else is refused at the door and counted nowhere, the ballot and Now's vote row are for the people in, and a question with fewer people in than a majority of its set locks here, where a majority of the people in decides, since the chain would ask the whole set (`locksHere`). Production held one vote from someone never in (an excluded test account's, on the Lightning question); it decided nothing, and that question was voided by the deployed build's tiebreaker before this one shipped, on the chain, where it stays.

**"Add another" answered the error card** because the start's actions threw inside a transition with nothing to catch them; they go through `attempt` now, the error card asks the server again and is counted, a send again after one that failed before its signature reuses the draft it made, and an address that is not a game's id is the code screen.

**One set per pair, named by its people.** A question's set that one friend joined is the pair's set: the pair's existing set is used for a cover, the two-person set first, then the oldest (`ensureDyad`); People shows one row for the same people; a set's name counts its guests and reads "Rachel and you", never "Just you two". Production has two pairs with a second set, both on the chain, where a set cannot be folded into another; they are listed and unchanged.

**The final score's backstop compares answers, not finals.** The Red Sox question voided as a conflict because one source had the Yankees winning 9 to 2 and the other 9 to 0; both answered the question the same way (`outcomesAgree`). The correction is prepared and waits for the owner (`scripts/ops/resettle-feed.ts`).

**The homepage says the line 1.9 chose.** The root's description and the manifest's are `FIRST_LINE`; the root answers a link-preview fetcher with it in the head.

**Google** sits in the keeping step, in Dynamic's sign-in sheet and on the reach card (linking), by Dynamic's redirect handled at the root (the provider finishes the sign-in from the address; `OauthReturn` takes the parameters off it after: the three the SDK reads, `dynamicOauthCode`, `dynamicOauthState` and `dynamicOauthSsoProviderId`, so no code travels with a share). In the installed copy on the iOS 26 simulator Google's page opened inside the app's own window, so it is offered there (`GOOGLE_IN_INSTALLED_APP`, a switch); whether it comes back signed in needs a Google account and is the owner's check. A linked Google cannot sign in until "allow multiple credentials for sign-in" is turned on in Dynamic's dashboard, and a different Google address on a phone signed in by email makes a second account; both are listed for the owner.

**The models.** Haiku 4.5 writes up questions, picks the type and the date, asks Help define's questions and drafts; Sonnet 5.5 triages arguments, proposes outcomes, rules as the tiebreaker and writes Help define's final terms. Nothing calls Fable. A Haiku answer that fails its shape or its time, or comes back without a date, is asked once of Sonnet, whose answer stands either way, and is counted (`withEscalation`, `model_escalated`). Before switching, both routings ran on everything production had asked: 42 write-ups (all 42 parsed on Haiku, one after escalating; 40 of 42 with a date, every date agreeing with its terms, the two without now asked again; no type against the question's shape; the comparison's own word check flagged one for "money", which the copy rules allow), 9 arguments' triage (every tier the same), 6 tiebreaker rulings (Fable's own six recorded voids; Sonnet 5.5 agreed on four and named an answer on two, listed for the owner) and 2 outcome proposals (both the same, both the group's). At the API's prices on 2026-10-04 a write-up went from $0.0059 to $0.0028 and from a median 2.8 to 2.3 seconds, a triage from $0.023 to $0.004, a ruling from $0.042 to $0.007 and a proposal from $0.024 to $0.004; a question written up and proposed went from about $0.030 to $0.007. No test and no audit can reach the live API (`liveCallsAllowed`), and nothing uses prompt caching. Rejected: keeping Fable for the rulings at six times the price, since its agreement with its own past rulings says nothing.

**The audit, and the tests it sharpened.** Every one of the 1,426 mutants is killed: 1,423 in the full run, and three added before staging, each run and killed alone (an email credential with no top-level address read on the login and in the users API's record, and an SSO provider's id taken off the address), when the new tests' Dynamic shapes were rebuilt with Dynamic's own serializer (`JwtVerifiedCredentialToJSON`, and the users API's wrapper read from its key names) instead of typed by hand. Three survived the first pass because their tests never held the guarded case, and each test now carries it: a rule whose words name a control is never a tip, a handoff left over never changes an entry already signed, and the final score's own void is kept out of the rate by the void rule with two people in, not by the headcount. One page mutant survived once while the server strained and died when run alone. The development server's Turbopack panicked partway ("Restore of All for task … failed", an internal panic, with the simulators' open pages reloading on every mutated file), so the page layer was resumed from where it stopped, and once more after a background run's two-hour limit, the tree restored each time. Older tests that held what this round changed were brought to its rules rather than loosened: the signed-out answer from the two new actions, the shell's order read at Now's own content, the handle row named once, the idea slot's press, the asker's count from the who's-in row, a pair's set reading "Eli and you", two people in each question the profile counts, someone in a question saying what happened, a pick-one argument allowed and a number argument refused. Two database tests can lose a race with production's minute tick, which acts on every row in the shared database: the play-by-play test clears the tick's mark before it reads, and the close-row test still can, once in a full run; it passes alone.

## 2026-10-03: After the field round: the two feed queues tested, and four numbers for the pitch

**The two queue fixes that had no test have one.** The feed's backstop with one place a tick and two questions due: the older waits on a second source that has nothing yet, and the place goes to the one both sources agree on (cut at the tick's places, as it was, nothing settles). The finals poll with a listing that throws: its games are marked read at that moment with their results untouched, hold no place a minute on, and are due again after the usual ten minutes. Both run against the real database and neither goes near the chain: the questions are locked here alone, so the final score settles them here. The failing source is named for the run, so the real scoreboard's record of reads is never marked failed, and its row is removed.

**Four numbers on the owner's page, each from the ledger's own tables**, since the events table began on October 2 and these reach back to launch. *Came by a link, then asked*: counted accounts whose first question was asked in the window and who were already in a question someone else asked before it. Rejected: reading it from the link-opened event, which only exists since the counting shipped; and counting anyone who ever got into a friend's question, which says nothing about which came first. The tables cannot tell a link from being named by the asker, and the definition on the page says what is counted. *Sets with two or more questions*: sets of people in which two or more questions were asked in the window by counted askers, a game's questions counting once (one tap starts three) and a question called off not at all. *Clean resolutions*: the profile's own rule (`cleanResolutionOf`) over every counted asker, as a percent: ended by a vote, the tiebreaker or the final score; a void by vote or tiebreaker against it; an expiry and the final score's own void in neither number. The test holds the page's number to that function on the same rows. *Photos and stickers added*: photos on a question or a settlement and stickers made, by counted accounts; a picture used as a mark is not a sticker. Twenty-five numbers now; a snapshot taken before today has no value for the four and reads as zero.

## 2026-10-03: The field round, on the iOS 26 simulator: the short layout, a root a pixel taller, and the pace row under its chip

The owner let Claude use the iOS 26 simulator, and the two sequences of 1.1 ran in installed copies there: the deployed build (production, the owner's own session) and the current one (the local server, as a visitor, since a session cannot be carried to it).

**The old build reproduces the finding.** A fresh launch reads `innerHeight` 874, `visualViewport.height` 874 and the tab bar at 775 to 874. One keyboard on Now, dismissed, and iOS 26 leaves `visualViewport.height` at 806 with no keyboard on screen; the viewport guard believed it and the tab bar stood at 707 to 806 with a 68 point band beneath, until the app was quit. That is the owner's picture, and its cause: the number the guard corrected by is the one iOS leaves wrong.

**The new shell had a fault of its own there, found before it shipped.** A document that never scrolls fits the screen, and iOS 26 lays an installed app that fits the screen out 62 points short (the top inset: `innerHeight` 812 of 874, as the owner read on People and You in September), so every pinned layer stood 62 points above the bottom edge at rest, on every screen. Web content cannot draw into the band: a box pinned 62 points below the layout was clipped at 812. Decision: the root is one pixel taller than the large viewport (`height: calc(100lvh + 1px)` on `html`, overflow still hidden, so a finger cannot scroll it), which iOS lays out whole; while a field holds the keyboard it is the plain height again (`html:has(…:focus)`, text fields only), because with the taller root left in place a dismissed keyboard left the window scrolled by that one pixel and everything pinned a pixel high. The height is set in the stylesheet and by no class on the element, since a utility outranks the rule. The pinned sheet measures its share of the screen on the app root's box, not the document element, which still reads 812. Rejected: leaving the band (it is on every screen at rest); drawing the layers down into it (clipped); a script that sets the scroll back after a keyboard (correcting after the fact is what the guard did); `Screen`'s old extra pixel inside the app root (the document must be the taller thing, and it must not scroll).

**What the runs read**, on the current build's market screen in an installed copy, ten runs of each sequence in each appearance. At rest before and after every run: `innerHeight` 874, `visualViewport.offsetTop` 0, a box pinned to the bottom at 874, the app root at 0 to 874. `visualViewport.height` reads 874 on a fresh launch and 806 after the first keyboard, for good, which is iOS's own number and nothing in the app reads it any more (the unit suite scans for it). With the keyboard up from rest: 498, 313.7 and 461; reopened from the background with the keyboard up: 560, 251.7 and 461. Nothing drifted in forty runs; fourteen of the readings did not come back from the inspector (it timed out), none of them a reading that differed.

**Left as iOS draws it.** On a field in the sheet focused from rest, the phone's caret stood about 62 points above the empty field until the first keystroke, when the caret and the page settled where they belong; reopened from the background it is right from the start. It follows the taller root (with the plain one the caret is right and the band is back). Tried and without effect: the plain height from the moment a finger lands on a field, and a root pinned in place while a field is focused. Chosen: the band gone and the caret late, over the band on every screen. It is a phone check, since a simulator's caret is not proof of a phone's.

**Not run: Now, People and You on the current build.** They need a session at the local address inside the simulator, which only the owner can make (signing in there in Safari and adding it to the home screen carries it over). The old build's Now is the one root read. After the deploy the production copy already on the simulator is the current build with the owner's session, and the same runs take minutes.

**The pace row is part of the Yes or no choice** (2.6, the owner's ruling on the report). Careful mode exists for yes-or-no questions alone, so a heading of its own made the row read as a peer of "How people answer", which is the confusion the tester had. The heading is gone; the row sits indented under the kind chips, with a line at its left, only while Yes or no is selected (`paceRowShows`), with its sentence under it as before. Rejected: keeping "How the rules get written" and hiding the row for the other kinds, which is what stood.

**The set of three is a set of three.** With the owner's go-ahead after the list, `is_dyad` was set to false on the two-person set that a third person had joined (one row; nothing else in it was touched).

## 2026-10-03: The field round, the owner's follow-ups: late closes, the close for everyone who can, and the checks

The owner's reply to part 1's report: one fix that ships with part 1, one correction where the earlier wording misled, gaps in part 2, answers to the go-aheads, and checks. Parts 2 and 3 and everything here are one commit after part 1.

**The data could not say when an entry last changed.** `dare_positions` held `entered_at`, written once, and a change left no stamp. What it could say came from `dare_number_series`, which gets a point at every entry and every change: on the friend's Red Sox question the last point is the fifth entry, at 10:10am Eastern on September 30, ten hours before first pitch, and on the Lightning question the second entry at 8:49pm on October 1, three hours before its close; no entry on either changed after its game began. The series is best effort (a failed write is a gap), so that is a reading of what was recorded, not a proof.

**The close time ends editing, whether or not the close has run.** The friend's question stayed open two days past first pitch, and "anyone in may close a market past its time" made changing a call and then closing a standing move. Entries and changes past the close time were already refused (`pastItsClose`, since the QA round); that is one belt, and a row could not show it had held. Now every entry and every change stamps `changed_at` (migration 0037, additive, null on older rows, which read as `entered_at`), and at any close after the close time (`lockMarket`, for a person's close and the tick's alike) an entry last changed after the time is left out of the close (`countedAtClose`; for a game the close time is its start). With fewer than two that count the question ends as an expiry, nothing scored and no toll, and the tick ends such a question itself, game or not. An argument has no close time of its own and is untouched. The screen follows: past the close time an entry reads as final, Change is not drawn, and someone not in gets no entry sheet, where before each could be tapped only to be refused. Rejected: trusting the refusal alone (the rule must hold at the close, where the stakes are decided); deriving late changes from the series (it does not say whose).

**The close is in the sheet for everyone who can close** (3.24, 3.42; the owner's correction): the asker on any open market with two in, and anyone in once it is stuck past its close time. It is the sheet's main action when everyone asked is in or the market is stuck, and a secondary that asks once otherwise, with one line saying what closing now costs (`closeLine`, the fifteenth session's strings). The tertiary under the who's-in row is gone. `MarketStage` draws it wherever the entry sheet is not standing, so changing an entry still gets the entry sheet. Part 2 had drawn it for the stuck case alone, and in doing so had replaced the stage for someone in, which took their entry line with it; both are corrected.

**The stuck questions go ahead under that rule.** Nothing was changed by hand. At the first tick after the deploy the two with two or more in close (every entry on both counts, by the series), and the game questions with fewer than two end as expiries.

**The two-person set with three in it** is listed for the owner and unchanged: besides the friend's Red Sox question with five in, three open questions with one person in each (one of the owner's, two of the friend's), no covers, no obligations, nothing on the chain. Two of the five on the Red Sox question (a guest, and a guest who signed up minutes later) hold no seat in the set, because a guest is never seated in a two-person set; that is as built and is listed, not changed.

**The reminder's night is the recipient's own.** The browser's zone was a cookie the server saw only on that browser's requests. It is kept on the account now (`users.zone`, migration 0037, written by the session door on each open and at sign-in), and a reminder is held to 9am in the recipient's zone where known, then the asker's, then Eastern (`reminderZone`). Each person is claimed alone, so one person's night never holds the others, and the question is marked reminded only once nobody is waiting.

**The 1.2 pattern, read across every queue the tick drains.** A list cut at the tick's ten (or three, or two) with rows skipped inside the loop lets the skipped rows hold the places. Found and fixed: the votes-decided list counted votes, not the leading outcome, so a split question sat in it for good (`decidedUnresolved`); the tiebreaker's list of three held questions the final score settles, skipped in the loop for days (`toArbitrate` leaves them out); the warnings and the reminders are read whole (`QUEUE_SCAN`), since whether one is due is worked out in JavaScript; the feed's backstop read two rows and waited on them for up to three days (it reads fifty and acts on two); a scoreboard listing that failed kept its twelve places (its games are marked polled and wait the usual interval); a chain write whose mirror kept failing kept its place (`chain_writes` is read least recently tried first). The lock queue was part 1's. The deadline notice, the voting-opened notice, the void rule's expiry and the schedule sync act on every row they select, so nothing holds a place there. Two of the six (the backstop's scan and the failed listing) have no test of their own; the rest do.

**Days and nights out of UTC.** The backstop warning's quiet hours and its clock words fell back to UTC when a question carried no zone, which is 3am on the east coast; they fall back to Eastern (`FALLBACK_ZONE`). The stats page and its backfill counted UTC days, so an 8pm game and its last out were two days; a day is an Eastern day (`STATS_ZONE`, `dayWindow`, twenty-three or twenty-five hours on the two days the clocks change). What's on already grouped by the viewer's zone, and "tonight" and "tomorrow" are the asker's zone's. The display fallback for a browser that has not reported a zone stays UTC for the one screen before `ZoneReporter` redraws it, and a link tile for a question with no zone still says UTC in words.

**"Still going" cannot run for good.** A write that fails outright gives way to the table's words: offline at the tap sends nothing and says so (`tapGoes`); an action that throws answers words wherever it was called (`attempt`, applied to the thirteen components that called actions inside a transition with nothing to catch a throw, where it had replaced the screen with the error card; a redirect passes through); and a write with no answer after a minute gets the block with the words for a failure on our end, and the control takes taps again (`GIVE_UP_MS`). Closing, entering and voting are safe to send twice.

**A vote thirty-one days in.** Dynamic ends its login thirty days after the first sign-in whatever the Dareful cookie says. A person still signed in to Dareful sees nothing different until a tap needs a key the device no longer holds: a vote, or any routine action without pass the phone, opens the code step in the tap's own place, and the tap carries on when the code is in. If they close the step, the tap stops within a second or two (`signInAbandoned`; it used to wait three minutes) and the control says "You’ve been signed out. Sign in to finish this." (`signerFailure`). Read from the code and held by a unit test; not exercised, since it needs a session with no Dynamic login, which the development browser's real session is not. Item 180.

**Now's mark for a game is two bars, not two squares.** Two 28px stamps overlapping by 16px left 12px of the first showing, so "BOS" read "B(". In the 40px mark the pair is stacked: the away team over the home team, each a bar the mark's width and 19px tall with its abbreviation at 10px (`TeamPair stacked`, `STACKED`). Rejected: two squares on a diagonal (the abbreviation at under 9px) and side by side at 19px (under 7px).

**A pasted sticker, measured.** The cutout is shrunk on the device before it is sent (1024px on its long edge, PNG, transparency kept); a stand-in the size of a subject lifted from a 12MP photo (2200 by 3000, 11.6MB) became 1.3MB in 46 to 75ms in the development browser, and the server's part (trim, edge, two objects, the row) took 0.43 to 0.69 seconds from this machine. Unshrunk it would have been refused as too big. Before this round the sticker appeared after the shrink, the upload and the server's part; now it appears after the shrink alone. Leaving the picker before the upload lands still picks it when it does (the ask form stays mounted); leaving the ask flow leaves the sticker stored and in "Your stickers" next time, unpicked; closing the app mid-upload may store nothing, and nothing half-made is ever shown. The stand-in is not a real lifted subject and the timings are this machine's: item 178.

**The numbers page.** Two numbers added (people with a channel, and their share: of every counted account since launch, of the people active in the window otherwise; a channel is a push subscription or an email sign-in, read as an account with no phone hash, since an address is never stored), the chain's own counts from the indexer with the two contracts' explorer links (every account is in those, since the chain has no excluded account), and Eastern days.

**iOS 26.** The 26.0 simulator runtime installed in about five minutes and a simulator was made on it. The sequences in an installed copy were not run: the simulator tool needs the owner to let Claude use the new device, and nobody was there to say yes. The older build was served beside the new one for a before and after and then removed.

## 2026-10-02: The field round, part 3: the numbers, for the owner

**3.1 The numbers page.** `/stats`, the owner's and nobody else's: the owner's account ids come from `OWNER_USER_IDS`, server-only, and anyone else, signed in or not, is answered with the code screen exactly as for an address with no screen, so the page tells nobody it exists (`isOwner`, `src/lib/usage/owner.ts`). Each number (`STATS`, `src/lib/usage/stats.ts`) has a definition printed beside it that says what the window does to it, counted from `usage_events` and the ledger's own tables for two windows, since launch and the last seven days: accounts, active people, askers, questions, questions with two or more in, entries, guest entries, reached voting, settled by vote, by the tiebreaker and by the final score, expired, link opens, shares, notices by push, by email and to nobody, notices opened, errors shown, and (added with the follow-ups) people with a channel and their share. Every count leaves excluded accounts out (`users.excluded_from_counts`), their questions with them; a guest's or a device's events always count, since there is nobody to exclude. A daily snapshot (`usage_snapshots`, migration 0036, additive: one row per day with the counts as JSON, replaced when the day is taken again) is written by the page's "Snapshot today" and by "Backfill the days", which writes every day since the first event that has none and leaves the days already taken as they were; the page lists the last sixty days with their first eight numbers. Rejected: a page for everyone with their own numbers (the product shows nobody a score, 3.34); counting from the events alone (accounts and questions with two in are the ledger's facts, so they are counted there); a scheduled snapshot (the tick is one secret-authed door for the ledger's jobs, and a button the owner presses once a day keeps the numbers a human act).

**3.2 The tally on pull-to-refresh** is held until the shell is confirmed on the owner's phone (item 154), as the brief said: both touch the page's scroll.

## 2026-10-02: The field round, part 2: the sheet, the inputs and the rows

Part 2 of the field round, built straight after part 1. Each item is the owner's finding from the first week with real groups, built to the fifteenth session's document where it specifies the thing and to the finding where the document is silent.

**2.1 One sheet, four positions.** `PinnedSheet` is one component with tucked, resting, raised and full (docs/design.md 3.24 as amended by the fifteenth session), three pure rules held by tests (`visibleAt`, `positionsOf`, `nextPosition`): tucked shows the handle row alone, resting the state's move, raised up to 72% of the screen, and full up to the status band less 60px, the only position at which the sheet's own content scrolls, under a handle row that stays. Full exists only where raised cannot show everything; raised only where there is more than the move; every sheet keeps its handle in every state, so a sheet with nothing more still tucks. A drag past 24px moves one position by direction and the ends hold; the grabber's tap toggles resting and raised; a touch on the header row raises a lowered sheet; the page's room stays the resting height. The grabber is 48 by 6, since the 36 by 5 one was too small for a thumb (the owner's finding; the document's token is amended with the date). The close in the sheet is built for a stuck market alone, on the owner's correction: in normal running the close time closes a market by itself, so only once the time has passed with the market still open and two or more in does the sheet rest on the close ("Time’s up, and nothing closed it. Anyone in it can." and the chalk "Close it with N"), for the asker and for anyone in it (1.2's `mayClose`), held by an http test across three accounts. The calls-are-in stretch, the game page, the guest line, ideas and tips are next round's, as the owner said.

**2.2 The layers hold the page still.** Part 1's shell is the answer: the page is a box inside the viewport, the layers are its siblings, and nothing fixed moves with content any more. Nothing further was built.

**2.3 The PIN's focus.** The two PIN fields on You were password fields with "new-password" autocomplete, which an iPhone treats as a place to offer its password manager and move the focus. They are numeric text fields drawn masked (`.pin-field`), autocomplete off; the second takes focus once the first holds four digits, once.

**2.4 The photo input offers the camera and the library.** `capture="environment"` forced the camera while a market was open (3.39) and on the settlement photo. Both are gone: a phone offers the camera and the library alike, since a photo taken a minute ago is as much the night's as one taken now. 3.39 amended with the date.

**2.5 A pasted sticker shows at once.** The cutout was shrunk on the device already (`prepareCutout`, 1024px); now it is shown in "Your stickers" from the device's copy the moment it is read, with the upload running behind it, not pickable until stored, and the pick lands when the store answers. A failed upload removes the cell and says why.

**2.6 The question reads as a field.** On the field-coloured band the question box was transparent, so it did not read as somewhere to type; the question field and the terms step's title are drawn as fields, the surface with a line. The pace row's label and line are part 1's.

**2.7 Rows.** In who's got who, the tokens under "JP’s got" wore JP's face again; they wear the face of the one JP has got (`ObligationToken` `face="other"`). A count on Now's rows names its second number only when it is real ("3 of 6 in" while someone named is still out, "4 in" otherwise, `inCount`), as the who's-in row already did. A game is one row on Now whatever sets its questions came from, keyed by the game alone (3.15, decided by the fifteenth session), with the most pressing question's set in the row's address and its questions swiping together. The team stamps on Now's rows could not be reproduced clipped: the pair sits in a 40px slot, 28px each overlapping by 16px, inside a cell of 40px, and the row's rounding clips nothing at the first column; it is a phone check with the owner's screenshot.

**Rejected or held:** making the sheet's positions a per-screen prop (one component is the document's point); forcing the camera for a photo added while open (the owner's finding wins over 3.39); the calls-are-in close in the sheet (next round).

## 2026-10-02: The field round, part 1: the owner's nine findings from the first week with real groups

Nine findings from the owner's week on dareful.app with real friend groups, each reproduced on the current build before anything was changed (the simulator's installed app and Safari, the real localhost and dareful.app sessions, a private tab as the visitor), run to its cause, and fixed where the fix was clear; where a fix needs the owner's go-ahead or a production row, it is listed and nothing was changed. The usage events of part 0 count every one of these from here on.

**1.1 The shell is structural: the document never scrolls.** The finding (the tab bar and a sheet risen and stuck after the app came back from the background and a field took the keyboard) could not be reproduced on the simulator's iOS 27, where the document scrolled under the keyboard and came back on its own; the owner's phone is iOS 26, and the owner asked for the structural answer rather than another correction. So the document never scrolls: `html` and `body` are the viewport's height with overflow hidden, `#app` is one fixed box the screen's height that scrolls (`src/lib/ui/scroller.ts`: the one place that reads or sets the page's scroll), and every fixed layer sits beside it, portalled as before. The viewport guard, its two probes, the `--vv-top` and `--vv-bottom` offsets and the translate rules are gone; what a keyboard does is read from the field that took it (`takesKeyboard`). Confirmed on the simulator's Safari against the local build: under the keyboard the visual viewport pans (offset 226 of a 714 viewport) while the app's box stays at 0 and the pinned sheet sits on the keyboard's top edge; with the keyboard gone, and again after a background and return with the keyboard, every number is back where it started. The sequence the owner asked for ten times over, on iOS 26 in the installed app, is a phone check (docs/testing.md 154). Rejected: keeping the guard and widening its cases, since it corrected an offset after the phone had made it and could only ever chase.

**1.2 Game questions never closed, for two reasons, neither the one it looked like.** (a) The scoreboard's day was keyed by UTC (`dayOf`): a game at 8pm Eastern was asked for under the next day's date on both sources, so its final was never read and nothing was proposed; the day is the source's own (`SOURCE_ZONE`, America/New_York, with `isoDayOf` for the second source). (b) The scheduler's lock queue took the ten soonest questions past their time and skipped, in the loop, those with fewer than two in; eight such questions held the first places for two days and the owner's friend's question with five in never reached the front. The query now leaves out what cannot lock, and a game's question past the start with fewer than two in ends as an expiry (nothing scored, no toll, docs/design.md 3.33: everything on a game closes at the start, whoever added it). Also: an open market past its close says "Time’s up" and never "Closes soon" (`closesClock`); past its close anyone in a market may close it (`mayClose`), since a close the time should have made is nobody's privilege; each sport starts in its own word (`startWord`: kickoff, first pitch, tip-off, puck drop, the session 15 strings), on the game page, the link's description and the start flow; "Say it yourself" is already two hours after the expected end (`SAY_YOURSELF_AFTER_MS`); the second source lists by date with no season filter, so a postseason game is found like any other. The questions stuck on production at the time of writing are listed in the report for the owner (eleven under counted askers: two with two or more in, which the first tick after the deploy will close, and eight game questions with fewer than two in, which it will end as expiries; one question with nobody in and no game stays as it is); nothing was changed by hand.

**1.3 A game split across two sets.** The owner's friend started the game from What's on into the two-person set with the owner while the owner's own set on the game was the owner alone; a third friend then joined the friend's question by its link and was seated in the two-person set, which still read "Just you two". Neither path should make a second set, and neither does now for the label's sake: a third person joining a question asked between two makes the set the three of them (`joinGroupOf` clears `is_dyad`) and a set's label follows its people (`setLabel` says "Just you two" only with one other). The existing two-person set with three in it (6c3f7b9f) still carries the flag, and a cover between its two original people would be seated in it by `ensureDyad`: the one-row fix (`is_dyad = false`) is proposed and not made. Nothing is merged, since a question's signatures name its set. No new set controls.

**1.4 Getting lost.** Counted in the development browser: a tab's tap adds one entry, a game row one, a question one; the set chips on the game page added one per switch and now replace, since switching sets is the same screen. The tab bar is drawn on the four roots only, so the current tab's second tap can only scroll to the top (6.4), and every other screen's Back lands on its root. The screens without a tab bar are every screen but the four roots; listed in the report.

**1.5 Now's verbs do what they say.** A row's address names its move as a fragment, which never reaches the server or the cache: `#enter` lands with the entry sheet raised, `#ballot` with the ballot raised, `#close` with the close's ask open (the confirmation, whoever it leaves out, and nobody when everyone asked is in); `useHash` reads it on the client and the sheet raises on the next frame. Finish lands on the draft's own screen, which is its step (the asker's own number and "Send it"). Rejected: a query parameter, which would make a second cache key for the same screen and travel in a copied link.

**1.6 Errors say their cause, and a slow write is never shown failed.** One table of words (`src/lib/ui/errors.ts`), the owner's: offline, a read taking too long, a write still going, the server, signed out, not allowed ("Only JP can close this one."), changed under you, too many tries. A control's wait is one pure reading (`buttonWait`): a write past ten seconds keeps its runner and says "Still going. This one takes a few seconds." and never the block, because a second send of the same thing is the one thing a slow write must not get; a read (a screen asked for again) gets the block with "Try again" at ten seconds. A request that threw in the browser gets the offline words with no network and the server's otherwise, since a thrown action says nothing of why in production (`signingProblem`). Every action answers the signed-out words rather than throwing (`currentUser` and a return, held by a test over the source); the six whose answer cannot carry words (a boolean, a void, a status, a signing answer, the two refusals at a field) answer their own quiet way, and the one form action keeps `requireUser`. Closing a market is idempotent: a second tap, or a tap after the time closed it, finds it closed and is answered as done, so Try again never sends twice; closing one that is decided says it changed and the screen is read again; "Only the person who asked it can close it" names the asker now. The "Closing it. A few seconds." caption is gone. Every word shown is counted by cause (part 0's `error_shown`, with the five new causes).

**1.7 Accounts arriving as guests.** The Dynamic login lasts thirty days in the browser that typed the code (`jwtDuration`), the installed app and Safari each keep their own, and the Dareful cookie lasted thirty days from the sign-in with nothing renewing it, so a phone that opened the app every week was signed out on day thirty and its person entered as a guest. The session slides now: a cookie a day old or more is issued again for thirty days on the next open (`PATCH /api/session`, `SessionRefresh` once per open, `sessionDueForReissue`). Safari's seven-day cap on script-written storage is an assumption about the Dynamic login in Safari (not the installed app) and is left as one. A guest's entries fold into the account at sign-in exactly as at sign-up, each kept once at its number (a test over two questions and one token). The guests matching accounts are listed for the owner (one, the friend's guest "Noah" with one entry on the friend's Red Sox question, against an account Noah not in that set; two of the owner's own guests match nobody); the creator's merge is the remedy, and nothing was bound by hand.

**1.8 Getting people back, and one notice because time passed.** Measured over the week: of the fourteen questions that reached voting, four sent a vote request and each to nobody (no channel), since the only request was the one after a first vote; the asker's close and the time's close sent nothing, and most people had never allowed push. Three notices now. Voting opened (`voting_opened`): everyone in the quorum but the person whose act closed it, whichever path closed it, named for what they did ("JP closed …", "Time’s up on JP’s …", "Rae is in on …"), landing on the ballot; the action that closed it sends at once and the tick sends within a minute for every path it did not (one claim per question, `vote_asked_at`). Everyone asked is in (`all_in`): the asker, once, when the last person they asked gets in, never for their own entry. The twelve-hour reminder (`vote_reminder`): once, by push and email, to everyone still to vote, twelve hours into voting, held to 9am in the asker's zone when it falls between 11pm and 9am (`reminderSendTime`; Eastern when the question carries no zone), never a second (`vote_reminded_at`). The reminder is a notice sent because time passed, which Principle 1 forbids; the owner ruled it in for the first week with real groups, where nobody came back to vote without being told, and the ruling, the reason and the date are recorded here so the rule's exception is one and named. After a close by hand the locked screen's nudge card is the offer to tell the chat; nothing new was built for it. Migration 0035 (additive) carries the two claims.

**1.9 Words.** The signed-out headline cycles three questions ("Will dinner start on time?", "Who wins, Jets or Bears?", "Will the train be late?") held at the tallest's height, the first alone under Reduce Motion, over the owner's line; the argument's prompt, line and button ("Check it."); the pace row labelled "How the rules get written" with the owner's line for careful; the margin's example reads "the Jets by 3 counts as minus 3".

**Left for the owner:** the stuck questions (the first tick after the deploy closes two and ends eight; or say which to leave); the two-person set with three in it (one row); the guest Noah (the friend's merge); the reminder's zone (the asker's, Eastern when none; the brief said Eastern); and whether a game's question with fewer than two in should end at the start or wait for its asker.

## 2026-09-29: The QA round: the owner's ten findings, a walk across four accounts, and what the simulator found on its own

**The four accounts.** Xcode's simulator holds Dareful as a home-screen app and a separate Safari, both signed in to production, beside the real sessions on `localhost:3000` and `dareful.app` in the development browser; a private tab in the simulator's Safari is the visitor with no session. The simulator's web views are read and driven through its Web Inspector (`appium-remote-debugger` over the `webinspectord_sim` socket, a scratch tool that evaluates one expression in one page), so a reading on the simulator is a reading of real WebKit, and a tap is the simulator's own. Alternative rejected: setting a test account's session cookie into the simulator's browser by hand, so the installed app could run the local build signed in; that is materialising a credential, and it was refused (the safety classifier's ruling, honoured). What a fix changes inside the installed app is therefore confirmed either after the deploy or once the owner signs in to `http://localhost:3000` on the simulator themselves; every such item is listed as a phone check.

**The entry sheet lingers (finding c).** A timer of 1.8 seconds kept the sheet after the entry line had arrived. It is gone: the sheet stands only while entering is the person's move, before they are in and while they are changing (`sheetPresent`), and it leaves in the render the entry line arrives in. Alternative rejected: a shorter timer. Any timer puts two states on screen at once.

**The information sheet (finding e).** The sheet was capped at 560px with the name and the fixed line pinned as a banner over a padded scroller, and the icon stood at `z-60`, above the ask layer, which is why the ask screen showed two icons (the root's, through the layer). Now the modal sheet has a `full` mode (as tall as its content, up to the status bar), nothing in it is sticky, the close sits beside the handle row rather than in it, and the icon is part of its page. Alternative rejected: hiding the root's icon while the layer stands. The icon should never have been above the layer.

**The handle's drag (finding d).** Pull-to-refresh started on a drag that began on a sheet; the handle's only claim on the pan was CSS; the modal kept its rise animation with `fill: both`, which overrode the drag's transform. Now a handle row claims its drag twice (`touch-action: none` and a non-passive `touchmove` that cancels the move, `claimDrag`), a pull to re-read may only start on the page (`pullStartsHere`), the sheet's section takes no touches over the page above a lowered sheet, and a raise or lower the screen asks for travels like one the finger made. Alternative rejected: a global touch lock while a sheet is open. The page above a lowered sheet is still the page's.
