**The fixed layers (finding a).** Three diagnoses and the simulator's readings agree: no page ancestor holds the layers (the http suite walks every screen for a captured ancestor). What moves them is the phone's viewport left out of step after a keyboard: the visual viewport offset or short with nothing typing, so every fixed box, the status band and the grain included, sits off its place by the same distance and rides with the page over it. The guard (`ViewportGuard`) reads two fixed probes, nudges the scroll by a pixel, and while the phone stays out of step writes how far each fixed box must move on itself (`--vv-top`, `--vv-bottom`, read by `[data-fixed]` boxes and by nothing that contains one, 9.3); it also remembers the scroll at focus inside a fixed layer and restores it on blur, which is the page left scrolled under the header after the keyboard (seen on production in the installed app). The simulator's iOS 27 recovers on its own in every case tried, so the stuck state could not be reproduced there; the guard was exercised by forcing the keyboard's pan to count. Alternative rejected: a scroll model where the page scrolls inside a fixed box. It changes every screen's scroll for one phone fault; listed for the owner if the guard does not hold on the phone.

**Transitions (finding b).** The phone's swipe back is animated by the browser, and the app's own transition ran on top of it; a market opened as its band alone because its screen was asked for on release. Now a traversal is known in one place (`traversal.ts`: the browser's `hasUAVisualTransition`, else a touch that began at a side edge and was never released to the page), a screen landing from one arrives with none of the app's motion, and a row's screen is asked for on the touch (`touch-fetch.ts`: whole while the router still knows the route's shape, the shape alone otherwise). The shell is pushed onto the history by the tap itself, so the phone's swipe-back picture is the shell. Alternative rejected: fetching whole on every touch. Without the shape the screen renders twice and lands later.

**Measure the screen (finding f).** Confirmed first on the simulator's installed app: the tab bar's bottom at 874 of 874 on People and You with nothing open. Then removed everywhere: the probe, the cold-start instrument and the switch, the marks, the sheet entry and the tests. The cutter's timing caption stays, listed for the owner.

**The argument flow (finding g).** "That didn't come back." was the ten-second wait block over a triage that takes six to nine seconds (longer on a cold function), with a dead Try again on a submit button; "Couldn't save that." was the ask layer standing over the draft after `router.replace`, so the second tap inserted the same id. Now the triage has room (`maxTokens` 2500), a model that refused a forced tool choice is asked plainly from then on (`forcesTool`), Try again under a submit control submits its form (`retryOf`), the layer stands only at asking's own address (`askLayerStands`, with the parallel slot's way out), a second send answers with the draft the first made (`draftAlreadySaved`), and a late answer lands only where it was asked (`answerLands`). The argument screens' tint came from `inkVars` reading the dark table alone; a picked mark now retints the room through both themes' tokens (`inkRoomStyleText`). Alternative rejected: a longer wait before the block. The block is right at ten seconds; the call had to get faster and the retry had to work.

**A close chip's word is the screen's word.** The chips were fixed hour counts (Tonight +8h, Tomorrow +30h), so "Tomorrow" asked at 8:26pm read "Closes Thursday". Tonight and Tomorrow are now a minute before midnight in the asker's own zone (`closings.ts`, with the offset followed across a clock change); the week and the month stay spans, since their words are never read back as a day. Alternative rejected: keeping the hours and changing the labels to "In a day". The design's chips are days, and the screen reads days.

**The zone is reported from the root.** The zone cookie was written only by `When`, so a browser that had never drawn a relative time (a new account, a friend from a link) had every server-drawn clock in UTC: "Decided tomorrow" on the invitation card for a Thursday close. `ZoneReporter` under every screen writes it once per browser and asks for the screen again where the server drew it before knowing. Alternative rejected: reading the zone from a request header. There is no such header; the browser has to say.

**A tap counts only on the control it began on.** On production a 60ms tap on the odds line went down on the range input and came up on the primary that had risen into the spot, and entered the person at 77% and $5 (the sheet jumped, since a parent-driven raise had no transition; on the local build the raise animates, and a held tap still landed the click). The pinned sheet keeps the document's last `pointerdown` target and refuses a click on a control that does not contain it (`taps.ts`); a keyboard's, a screen reader's or a script's click carries no pointer and always counts. Alternative rejected: disabling the primary for 400ms after a raise. A timer would refuse a real tap and let a slow one through.

**A ghost's screen once in.** Once in, the ghost's screen put who's in, the facts and Sign in before the entry line and the picture; once locked it showed "This one closed… so you can watch but not enter" with their entry gone; once settled, "This one's finished." Now it is the market as anyone in sees it (3.17, frame 6): the entry line and the picture first, the entry with its clock and where everyone landed once locked with the poll on the page (the pulse admits a ghost whose browser token names a position), and how it ended once it has. A phone that remembers a ghost says "Joining as Wisp · Not you?" instead of asking a name it would not use, and "Forget them" clears the browser's tokens. Alternative rejected: reusing the member's whole page for a ghost. It reads the session on every branch; the ghost's page stays its own and grows only what 3.17 names.

**Smaller rules, each with a test and a mutant.** Taking a side on an argument raises its sheet as the line does; the call sheet's wells wrap rather than clip; a quorum of one is told its word settles it; every document screen, root or task, is at least a pixel taller than the large viewport (the sheet stood 62pt above the edge on task screens that fit); the roots restore their scroll on a return.

**The database client's idle timeout.** Left at twenty seconds: an instance holds up to five connections and two clients per process are possible, so the brief's condition (one or two per instance) is not met. The owner's to raise.

**The keep-warm visit.** Confirmed from production's logs: `GET /` beside every `/api/tick`, and Now's first byte at 0.11 to 0.25 seconds where it had been 2.9 asleep.

**The mutation audit's static check.** `tests/mutation/check.ts` reads every mutant against the working tree (the find occurs once, or `nth` says which; the suite exists; every named test exists) and lists the tests no mutant names, in seconds and without applying anything. It found 91 mutants that had drifted from the code over earlier rounds, which the audit would have reported one at a time as errors. Alternative rejected: leaving the drift to the audit. The audit takes hours and reports a stale mutant as a failure of the run, not of the mutant.

**What's on as one flow (finding h).** A signed-in person opening a game's link for a set they are not in was handed "Start a game" or another set's page. Now both `/on/<id>/<g>` and `/on/<id>?g=` for a set the viewer is not in render that set's questions as the plain list the signed-out page shows, with Back and no sign-in words, each row opening its question, where the existing join seats them in the asker's set (never a join on the GET). Alternative rejected: 3.33's cards with counts for someone not in, which show what they have no part in yet. The game page's "Send it to the chat" and "Copy" gave way to the who's-in row of 3.42 (the stack is everyone in on any of the game's questions by participant id, ghosts included; the holdouts the set's account-holders in none; the count out of the set's seats; "Nobody's in yet" at zero; share and copy only, no code and no pass; chalk while the viewer started the game and nobody else is in); the viewer counts among the holdouts when not in, so the stack and the dashed avatars add up to the count. The set inside a sentence is its people around whoever the sentence is about (`setInSentence`, `askerLine`): "You asked Priya and Gabe", "Priya asked Gabe and you", "You asked" alone for whoever you send it to, "the Friday crew" with its article, "· Papa's birthday" for a name that cannot take one, "and 3 others" past three names with the viewer counted; never "You asked Just you". The chips are named "Who you're on this with" to a screen reader. A called-off question leaves the game page and no longer blocks "Add another" for its key (`onePerQuestion`, `setsOnGames`, pure and tested). Alternative rejected for the called-off rule: a where clause in SQL, which only a database test could hold.

**The game's link tile (finding h).** Rebuilt as the amended 3.27: the asker's first name, the game's name in serif on two lines broken after "at" between two square stamps, the questions as the menu's short names ("Who wins", "By how much", "Total goals", "The first drive": `menuName`, one exhaustive source the menu reads too), the close time, a 48px band kept clear at top and bottom, every height a constant held by a unit test, a missing creator reading "A friend asks" (`askerName`, shared with the question's tile). Alternative rejected: the drawing without the serif name (stamps at 120px, the questions between them), which says the teams only by abbreviation.

**The names people see (finding j).** One rule for what is not a name (`isIdentifier`: an "@" or "+" anywhere, five or more digits and phone punctuation, or one token with dots or underscores inside it) at the sign-up step, the rename and the link page's "Your name", refused at the field ("Say what your friends call you.") and on the server; an account whose stored name is an identifier is asked the step once more (`nameSettled`, read by the login's decision and the session's facts alike); until it answers, `firstName()` prints the capitalised piece before the separator ("Justin" for "justin.wender"), so no card or tile carries the local part. Alternatives rejected: "A friend" as the fallback for an identifier-shaped name; a looser rule that lets a dotted handle through when typed on purpose (the owner's call). The stored rows in production are the owner's to reset (the dry-run query is in the names diagnosis); no row was changed.

**The outdated words (finding i).** The signed-out line under "Who's got the next one?" is the owner's sentence, its first half the app's description, the manifest's and the plain card's footer (`ABOUT_FIRST`, `ABOUT`, one place); "0 friends are in." is "Nobody's in yet"; one `friendsIn(n, style)` replaces two count arrays that had drifted; the asker's Close row on Now reads the count ("6 of 6 in") where it said "Everyone's in"; a joined notice says "That's 2 in." when the asker is not in, never "two of you"; "1 of 5 has" at one; the Numbers caption on You names no colour; "Send it to the chat" and the old signed-out line join the cut list the build refuses. Alternative rejected: two unrelated literals for the sentence and its first half, which would drift as the count arrays had.

**The styles, the screens and the sheets.** The rings 8.7 says take the stroke variant on paper; the shapes the styles diagnosis listed; the screens' refusals and what the routes diagnosis found; and the 214 information-sheet entries read against their screens, 111 of them reworded or their screens changed so the sheets tell judges the truth (`docs/decisions.md` carries each area's list).

**The ask slot's way out names its addresses.** The slot's catch-all (`@ask/[...rest]`, the first fix for the layer standing over a sent question) made every address a matched one, and an address with no screen then got Next's own fallback page in place of the code screen (5.4): the http suite's "the old cover form's address is nothing" caught it at `/new`. A `default.tsx` in the screen slot calling `notFound()` did not help, for the reason below. The slot now holds a null page at each address asking leaves for (the four roots, `/m/[id]`, `/join`), in `src/app/@ask/nothing.tsx`, and an unmatched address is unmatched again, so the not-found route renders the code screen in the layout at 404. Alternative rejected: keeping the catch-all and rendering the code screen from the screen slot's default at 200, which loses the status.

**`notFound()` paints late, and it is the framework's.** Found under the above and then reproduced in a bare Next 16.3.5 app in both modes: `notFound()` thrown from a page answers 404 with Next's own empty shell (`<html id="__next_error__">`, the app's head metadata, no body), and the code screen is rendered on the client from the inlined payload once the scripts have run. The person sees the browser's canvas first, white in light mode, and then the code screen with the layout. This is every `notFound()` in the app (someone else's draft, a cover that is not theirs, a ghost that is not theirs) and has been production's behaviour since the code screen was made; an address with no screen at all is the one case rendered on the server. Not changed here: the alternative is to render the code screen from the page at 200 (the status the tests and 5.3 hold), a product call listed below.

**The audit asks the server before it believes a kill (2026-09-30).** Forty-five minutes into the round's audit the dev server's compiler crashed (a Turbopack panic restoring its cache, grown to 16 GB by the audit's rewrites), and every http mutant after that was reported killed: with nothing listening, the suite's setup throws and every named test fails, whatever the mutant did. The final baseline showed all 110 http tests failing against unbroken code, which is how it was seen. The runner (`tests/mutation/run.ts`) now asks the app before and after every http mutant; one that lost the server is void, the layer stops, and the run says where to resume (`npm run test:audit -- http --from=<id>`), which also skips the baseline a partial run cannot answer. The http layer was run again in three stretches with a live server (the desktop app stopped the preview server twice more, and the guard caught both). Alternative rejected: trusting the baseline at the end to reveal a dead server. It reveals that something died, not which kills were false, and a server that comes back before the end would hide it entirely.

**Seven mutants the audit let through, none of them a fault in the app.** `info-name-pinned`: the test read the markup from the attribute on and never saw the element's own class; it reads from the opening tag. `viewport-shift-rounding`: an equivalent mutant (the values are rounded before the comparison, so `< 1` and `=== 0` are one rule); it now removes the rounding, with a fractional reading asserted. `end-of-day-ignores-the-clock-change`: New York never needs the second pass; Auckland on the Saturday before its clocks go forward does, and is asserted. `suggest-without-a-number`: three of five names with no promised order left the no-number ghost out by chance; a no-number ghost with letters of its own is asserted never suggested. `now-keeps-the-lock-row-on-its-way`: the round's own rule (the asker's Close on time's up needs two in) left the test's question with no lock row to remove; it has a second person in. `ghost-in-a-hue-under-whos-got-who`: the assertion counted dashed avatars to the end of the page, where the who's-in stack supplied them; it reads the ghost's own line and tokens. `nudge-for-outsiders`: `canNudge` is a second guard that is never read for someone not in, because the who's-in row is not drawn for them while a question is open; the mutant now removes both guards, and the first is the one the test sees.

**Left for the owner, listed rather than chosen.**
- Whether `notFound()` should stay (a 404 whose first paint is the browser's canvas, then the code screen) or the pages that call it should render the code screen themselves at 200 (no flash; the status goes, and with it the rule at 5.3 the tests hold). A Next 16.3.5 behaviour reproduced in a bare app; nothing in the layout causes it.
- A question with fewer than two in at its close time: the close is now a hard cutoff (nobody gets in or changes past it), so such a question stays open, is never locked by the tick, and leaves only when its asker removes it from Now. The proposal is to expire it at its time (`resolved_by = 'expired'`, no toll); the cards and the Now row still read "0 of 1 in" and "Closes soon" meanwhile.
- "Tonight" chosen after eleven closes within the hour; the alternative is to hide the chip past eleven.
- The cutter's timing caption ("Model loaded in 2.1s · cut in 0.4s") stays as an instrument for the phone check; it goes when item 94 is done.
- The back control pushes the shell onto the history at the tap, so the phone's swipe back lands on the root the row came from; whether Back should instead call the browser's back everywhere is a product call.
- Fixed layers' view-transition names on real boxes (the duplicate `market-ink` on a game page is refused by the browser and skipped).
- If the viewport guard does not hold on the phone: the scroll model change (the page scrolling inside a fixed box), which touches every screen.
- Pinning the database client to `globalThis` in production and a `connect_timeout`; the idle timeout stays at twenty seconds.
- The triage model and effort, and what "Try again" means for a call that is still running (today it sends the same call again).
- A line with no claim about the world ("Who is the best Roman emperor") passing triage.
- "market" as a word people read (eleven places), "dare" ("It could be a dare instead"), and "locked" in refusals ("Numbers are locked."), each listed by file in the words area's answer.
- The signed-in invitation card (`InvitePreview`, "Join as <name>") against 3.17's own screen; pasted game links on the joining screen; a third person joining a two-person set from a link; the blind chips' words on the terms step ("Shows once you've picked", "Hidden until it's locked" are false under the blind ruling); Dynamic's sign-in sheet in dark only.
- Resetting the stored identifier names in production (the dry-run query is in the names diagnosis; no row was changed), a way to change a name later (3.34 has no name row), and whether a dotted or underscored handle typed on purpose should be refused.
- The data the game link made wrong before this round (a friend's second set on the owner's game) cannot be moved: its signatures name the set. The friend removes it, or the two play it.
- Team stamps overlapping on Now's rows; "In 2 markets since today" on You; the number field's typeface; Safari's floating toolbar over the bottom of a sheet.
- The candidate choices taken for the What's on area (the plain list, candidate A throughout) and the words area (the first sentence as the description and the plain card's footer) are recorded above as taken; the owner may prefer otherwise.
- From the screens area: the claimant's Yep with nothing pressed but a link entry unpressed needs words; "Let them go" records no dismissal so the name stays on People; "Your number · Used to sign in" is drawn for email accounts too; a provisional market's transfer is a yep row naming no question; archiving the last row returns the first-run screen; the Enter row's count can read "4 of 3 in" with ghosts; the ten-second Try again under a code being typed starts a second confirm; and careful mode's prompts for number and pick-one questions (the alternative to hiding the chip, which is what was done).
- From the sheets area: the argument steps share the careful sheet; a game's who-wins and margin questions get the yes-or-no and number sheets by kind; the open-market sheets are at the cap with six controls unlisted; the invitation card and the dead-link screen carry no icon; whether declining a cover should tell the person who logged it.
